# Native port — roadmap to playable

**Goal:** a person stands in front of a webcam and plays Dance Central 3 —
menus, a whole song, believable scoring, results, saves — on Linux, macOS and in
the browser.

Measured state: [STATUS.md](STATUS.md) (2026-11-13). Each item names its owning
files and the check that proves it done. An item is done when its check passes,
not when code lands. When an item closes, record the commit next to it; when the
whole track closes, collapse it to one line.

Size: S < half a day · M ≈ 1–2 days · L = several days.

## Milestones

1. **M1 — Dance on Linux.** P-1, P-2, P-5, P-6, G-5 done: a real webcam plays a
   whole Perform song with no crash, and a deterministic replay test guards it.
2. **M2 — Dance in the browser.** P-9, W-2, W-3 done (all three closed 2026-11-13): the page asks for the
   camera, tracks the player, and the score responds.
3. **M3 — Whole game by dancing.** P-3, P-4, P-7, G-1..G-4 done: gesture menus,
   the player on screen, calibrated scoring, and every mode reachable.
4. **M4 — Mac.** Track M done, verified on the owner's Mac.
5. **M0 — Playtest ready (added 2026-11-13; the next target, ahead of closing M1–M4).**
   Track L done: a person can be handed the game and a webcam and play for an
   hour without us in the room. **Real webcams have driven the game (corrected
   2026-11-20, owner): the owner has played it on the GPD and on the Mac, natively
   and in the browser, and it worked.** The GPD sessions of 2026-11-14/15 are where
   P-17 and P-18 came from; the Mac's state is in [MACOS.md](MACOS.md). Until
   2026-11-20 this paragraph said no real webcam had ever driven the game: that was
   the 2026-11-13 state, never updated, and it was repeated to the owner as current.

Milestone state, 2026-11-13 (from the lane reports, not a fresh measurement):
M1 open on P-2 (owner webcam session) · M2 closed on paper, reopened by W-10/W-11 ·
M3 open on P-4 (photos, depth view) and G-11 · M4 prepared, waiting on M-7.

Since then (2026-11-20, from the owner and the rows below, not a fresh measurement):
P-2's owner webcam session and M-7's first Mac run have both happened (GPD and Mac,
native and browser), G-11 is done, and everything is pushed (L-6).

## Track L — live playtest readiness

The third dogfood pass (`np-dogfood3`, real footage through the live
`pose_server` path, two-player, web with a fake camera) is where most rows below
and the 2026-11-13 rows in P / G / W / H come from. Its scratch is
`/home/free/tmp/claude-1000/np-dogfood3-KWLW/` (not durable: copy evidence into
`archive/` before citing it in a closing commit).

| L-3 | **Frame rate with the pose tracker on the same GPU.** **Profiled (np-pose-perf, [POSE_INPUT.md](POSE_INPUT.md#performance-l-3-np-pose-perf-2026-11-13)).** Inference is the whole frame on both paths; every other stage together is under 2 ms (desktop) or 5 ms (web). Desktop, GPD: `num_poses 2` runs MediaPipe's person detector on every frame while one dancer is tracked, 43 ms (21.7 fps). The new `pose_server.py --probe-second-person 0.5` gets **35.2 fps** with one dancer, with output identical to `num_poses 1`, the setting the scoring calibration was recorded at. A second player is found up to 0.5 s later. Two dancers: 48 ms (19.8 fps), 34 ms with `lite`. Web, GPD, pose pipeline alone (the game does not boot in headless Chromium there): CPU 12.7 fps (73 ms WASM inference), `pose_delegate=GPU` 29–30 fps, but its joints differ from desktop by 22 mm mean (parity). ~~No V4L2 capture cliff (a grabber thread would only trim staleness when inference outlasts a camera period)~~ **wrong, see np-gpd-camfps below**; no GPU delegate in the Python wheel. **Owner decisions:** turn on `--probe-second-person` by default (desktop), and `pose_delegate=GPU` (web, after a scoring check). The P-4 picture costs 3.8 ms of the worker's 78 ms on the GPD, so moving it out of the worker is not the lever np-web3's figures (17.4 fps default, 28.4 best, rig, [WEB.md](WEB.md#pose-frame-rate-w-13-l-3)) suggested. **Web probe (np-web4, 2026-11-14):** `pose_probe=<s>` ports the second-person probe to the web worker (numPoses 1 every frame, a numPoses 2 pass every s seconds; parity: identical joints to `pose_persons=1` with one dancer, 150/150 frames, and finds a second dancer on its first probe, 90/90). It runs serially in the one worker, and on the rig with the game running it was **not** a gain: 16.9 vs 18.1 fps and 10.9 vs 11.7 (two interleaved rounds, load 30–55; `pose_persons=1` 25.3 and 12.3 in the same rounds), so the default stays off. INFERRED: the probe needs its own worker (desktop runs it on a thread). No web setting reached 30 fps on the loaded rig. [POSE_INPUT.md](POSE_INPUT.md#web-second-person-probe-np-web4-2026-11-14). **Web (np-webpose, 2026-11-14):** the GPU delegate is the default now (`pose_delegate=AUTO`, W-19): with the game running on the rig, 18–22 → 26–28 pose frames/s delivered, camera → game p50 72–83 → 26–44 ms, the same score against the cards (W-20). The probe runs in its own worker (W-18) but stays opt-in: numPoses 1 scores lower (H-86). Left: the game's fps with pose on, on the GPD (W-22); two dancers at 30 fps. Until then the game fills a slow camera's frames (L-8). **Capture (np-gpd-camfps, 2026-11-14):** the GPD session's 7.7 and 10.3 pose fps were the camera's 15.4 and 20.6 halved by our own capture: `open_camera` asked for one driver buffer, and a UVC camera then loses every other frame at any processing time (manual 33 ms exposure: 15.4 fps through one buffer, 30.0 through two). Now two buffers and a capture thread that hands the loop the newest frame (`NewestFrameCapture`; frame age 51 ms against 85–98 for a second buffer read in the loop, at 50–57 ms of inference). What is left on that box is the sensor (auto exposure runs it at 15–20 fps in a dim room and no control forbids that; a manual exposure is 30 fps at a tenth of the brightness) and two-dancer inference (57 ms in the session = 17.4 fps; `lite` 33 ms idle). [POSE_INPUT.md](POSE_INPUT.md#capture-buffers-np-gpd-camfps-2026-11-14). **Left: a session with dancers in view on the new capture** (`POSE_STATS` now prints `camera_fps=` beside `fps=`). | S | Both rates recorded in STATUS; 30 fps pose or a stated reason |
| L-4 | **Latency default on a live feed.** **Decided (`np-tracking`): 100 for a live webcam, 0 for recorded footage. Since `np-latency-harness` the source declares it, so no harness needs an env var.** `pose_server.py --video` (the fake camera, `footage_camera.py`, `play.sh --video`, `pose_scoring_gate.sh` video mode) declares 0 in its packet header; `--camera` declares nothing and gets `scoring.dta`'s 100; `--source-latency-ms N` declares N; an explicit `DC3_POSE_LATENCY_MS` always wins. The engine logs one `DC3 POSE LATENCY: scoring at N ms -- explicit / declared / default` line per change. A re-check with no override confirmed the declared 0 reached every song. It could not reproduce 0.70 because the box stayed at load 95–200: ~30 pose frames per window against ~120, ages 182–258 ms, mean 0.04. A same-load side-by-side gave declared 0 → 0.33 and explicit 0 → 0.22 on macarena. A quiet-box six-song rerun is still owed ([POSE_INPUT](POSE_INPUT.md#who-sets-the-latency-np-latency-harness)). MEASURED (the earlier grid, explicit values), six songs through live `pose_server` with the fake camera, score / the original's card: 100 → 0.49 and 0.52 (two runs), 50 → 0.57, 0 → 0.70 (best on 5 of 6; Starships prefers 100), all with a capture → game age of 61–73 ms (`DC3_POSE_LATENCY_LOG`). A repeat at 0 on a loaded box (age 78–120 ms) fell to **0.44, below the default**, so the best knob follows the real delay, and undershooting it is the costly side. The fake camera's whole delay is that age. INFERRED: it has no camera exposure or audio-output delay, both of which a webcam session adds, so its optimum is not a webcam's. Dogfood live-path scores taken at the default read ~30% low. Owner procedure in [POSE_INPUT](POSE_INPUT.md#latency-on-the-live-path-l-4): log the age, play one song at 50 / 100 / 150 | S | Default chosen with a multi-song table; owner session confirms |

| L-8 | **Frame fill for a slow camera.** The GPD's camera gives ~8.4 pose frames/s; the scorer assumes 30, and the same footage at 8 Hz scores 0.39 of 30 Hz. **Done (`np-pose-fill`, 2026-11-14). Default `extrap` at `DC3_POSE_FILL_GAIN=1.0`, deliberately generous: owner decision 2026-11-14, "over credit and then calibrate further"** ([POSE_INPUT](POSE_INPUT.md#frame-fill-np-pose-fill-2026-11-14)). While the camera is slower than 24 Hz (trimmed mean of 15 intervals, song time; was the median, which flapped 349 times in one web song on a 33/50 ms interval mix, fixed np-web4 2026-11-14, [WEB.md](WEB.md#pose-fill-on-web-l-8-np-web4)) each camera frame is delivered at once and stepped on by its joints' velocity, x the gain, for at most one camera period. Guards: a not-tracked joint is not moved; joint speed is clamped to 4 m/s (p99.6 of the footage at 8 Hz); a step that looks like a left/right swap is held (fires on 0.1% of real 8 Hz motion, 99.7% of whole-body swaps); nothing after a frame with nobody, so P-12's hold sees the camera's own frames (8 held frames, fill on or off); fill frames are never recorded. MEASURED (8 footage songs, FAST_TIME, load 25–84): 8 / 10 / 15 Hz → 1.054 / 1.098 / 1.078 of real 30 Hz (off 0.39 / 0.56 / 0.80); 30 Hz rating-identical to `off`. **Calibration lookup**, score vs gain: at 8 Hz 0 → 0.77, 0.25 → 0.82, 0.5 → 0.90, 0.75 → 0.99, 1 → 1.05; at 15 Hz 0.91 / 0.93 / 1.00 / 1.05 / 1.08. Controls, as a fraction of the right dance at real 30 Hz: standing still 0 with the fill on or off; standing still with noise 0.024 (real 30 Hz 0.026); wrong dance 0.019 (real 30 Hz 0.011); wrong dance with noise 0.036 (0.049). Under every injected noise (jitter, inferred/untracked joints, dropped frames, 10% body flips) extrap stays at or below the same noise at real 30 Hz (0.83 / 0.95 / 0.94). `interp` (lerp, 150 ms behind, delay folded into the latency) is the conservative override: 0.92 / 0.94 / 0.96, never above 30 Hz. The 900 s owner-replay timeouts were load (four concurrent replays at load 50–70 all completed at 80–83 fps). **Left:** calibrating the gain down (owner); the guarded default replayed on the owner's session; a live GPD session; not run in a browser (compiles under em++) | S | 8/10/15 Hz recover the most score the fill can give; 30 Hz unchanged; wrong dance and standing still near zero; noise not amplified (**done**); gain calibrated on the owner's own play (**open**, = L-5) |

The image has the same hazard; a Kinect never repeats a joint bit for bit, so the native sensor now adds a 1 µm noise floor to every delivered joint (`Skeleton_Native.cpp` `SensorNoiseFloor`), covering the confidence hold and the 2D providers' constant z. Test: `NativeSensorNoiseFloorTest`. | S–M | Still-skeleton run completes the song; a regression test fails without the fix |

Measured (Better Off Alone, synthetic v3 sensor, GPU): the cube figure stands in the tunnel from beat ~264, 230–660 cells a layer, ~4.4 ms a frame; freestyle takes its 16 photos and the slideshow shows distinct pictures. | P-7 | ~~Scoring believability~~ **ANSWERED 2026-11-13 (`np-scoring`)**: the leniency is the original's, not our input. Evidence: the scoring path is 100% `name_check` with no semantic shadow bodies; a real Kinect v1 stream (UTD-MHAD) replayed through the same path scores like our inputs. On Better Off Alone, still beats a different dance because a still body earns perfect or awesome on the near-static Roxbury move, which happens with real Kinect data too (the image's Kinect frame held still 104k vs real Kinect non-choreo motion 75k). On The Hustle every moving input beats every still one. Two input divergences were found and handled. (1) Frame rate: the old harnesses sent 120 skeletons per song second and the scorer keeps the best of a ±0.23 s window; the new in-process performer (`DC3_POSE_PERFORM`) sends the Kinect's 30. (2) Latency: `DC3_POSE_LATENCY_MS` replaces the Kinect-tuned `latency_offset` (`HX_NATIVE` hook in `MoveDir::LoadScoring`, still 100%); 300 ms late + knob 400 = on time, in all 4 cells. Matrix: 15 inputs × 2 songs × 2 difficulties in [POSE_INPUT.md](POSE_INPUT.md#scoring-believability-p-7-np-scoring). Tests: `PosePerformerClockTest`, `PosePerformerReplayTest`, `SensorLatencyTest` (all sabotage-checked). The old done-criterion "standing still no longer earns awesome" contradicts the original (Roxbury), so it was dropped. **Video calibration 2026-11-13 (`np-video-calib`):** 16 songs of a public story-mode stream (real dancer, real camera, Medium), MediaPipe-recorded and replayed against the original's own results cards; move counts agree (1162 = 1162). Perfect+awesome 0.91 vs 0.96, perfect 0.54 vs 0.79; score 74% of the original's, 78% with a 10° camera-pitch correction (limb error down on 17/17 songs). Still-body jitter on real footage, VIDEO mode, ~1.7 mm high-frequency σ. That is far below the synthetic z 19 mm that lifted a still body on The Hustle from 0 to 19 awesome. So **no smoothing**: a causal One Euro filter cost 13–26% of the score (light and strong settings). The knob's default **stays unset**. A recording's lead (−170..+127 ms per song) mixes stream sync, the dancer's timing and model lag, so it cannot stand in for a live pipeline's latency. The score curve is flat to 4% over ±50 ms. Details: [POSE_INPUT.md](POSE_INPUT.md#video-calibration-np-video-calib-2026-11-13). **Camera tilt 2026-11-13 (`np-pose-tilt`):** the original levels every skeleton by the Kinect's gravity (`SkeletonFrame::Create`: `vNormalToGravity` → `sUpVectorSmoother` → `NuiTransformMatrixLevel`), and the native sensor skipped it (identity stub). The level matrix is now ported from the target listing. `NativeGravityEstimator` estimates the normal from the player's standing posture (gated ~20 s median, minus the reference's own 2.9° lean, once a second, blended). It applies to sources flagged unlevelled (`pose_server.py` header flag, web pushes, `replay:`). Default **on**, `DC3_POSE_LEVEL=0` off. 16 songs: score 0.739 → **0.789** (fixed 10°: 0.781), perfect 0.540 → 0.620, score up on 14/16 (dabutt and electricboogie lose, as under the fixed 10°), limb error down on 16/16. Estimates 7.2–9.4° per song, in-song sd ≤ 1°. Arms: the error is image-plane direction, not length, depth, timing or the (unused) COCO map. Our proportions are closer to a real Kinect than the reference is, so there is no mapping fix (INFERRED: the dancer vs the choreography). Tests `GravityEstimatorTest`, `PoseSensorLevelTest` and others, 15/15 sabotages caught. [POSE_INPUT.md](POSE_INPUT.md#camera-tilt-np-pose-tilt-2026-11-13). **Left:** a live webcam's pipeline latency from `pose_server.py` capture stamps (needs P-2's real session), and a live check of the tilt estimate at a different camera height. The perfect-rate gap (0.62 vs 0.79) stays open. | S | A real webcam's latency is measured and the knob default set from it; jitter measured (done, no smoothing) |

Arm and displacement nodes stay flat. A person copying the screen would drift the same way. Earlier checks: the same decline with a 120 Hz performer; no hip drift. | S |

Was (`np-gpd-test`, GPD: 3 of 3 launches, so no tracking). MEASURED cause: the engine's ALSA output goes through PipeWire's ALSA plugin, whose `module-rt` sets `RLIMIT_RTTIME` to 0/0 when rtkit is absent (`aplay -D default` reads 0/0, `paplay` unlimited). dc3-native's fork/exec passes that to `pose_server.py`, and the kernel kills a mediapipe thread (ftrace: `sig=9 code=SI_KERNEL` from `drishti`). Standalone A/B: `prlimit --rttime=unlimited` survives, `0:0` dies with exit 137. A hard limit cannot be raised unprivileged. main's `75a878e19` (np-pose-rtfix: no `sounddevice` import) cures the standalone case only; N3 had it and died the same way. The workaround then was `MILO_AUDIO_BACKEND=pulseaudio`. Fix: a spawner forked before audio init launches the server with the limits the game was started with, and relaunches a server that dies ([POSE_INPUT.md](POSE_INPUT.md#how-the-server-is-launched-the-spawner-and-restart-on-death)); tests `PoseSpawnerTest.*` and `NativePoseSpawnTest.*`. Three causes, three fixes, each with a switch back: (1) MediaPipe reports one body twice (head and shoulders on top of each other, hips and depth ~0.4 m apart), and player 1's binding flipped between the reports. `PoseTwin_Native.h` keeps one report — the one a player is bound to (or being waited for under), else the one with more joints — and lets a lone report of the dropped twin stand in for her at her depth (`DC3_POSE_TWIN=0`). (2) A dancer back after one missed frame takes her velocity from her last frame inside the gap (`DC3_POSE_FILL_VELOCITY=prev`; 0.8% of person-frames). (3) Song select unbinds player 1 on about half of all sensor frames and does not keep the binding `Players` makes, so the auto-assign handed her to whoever the frame held (m24: ids at z 3.4 m and 1.5 m in turn). Outside a song, for 700 ms after her bound skeleton was last seen, `Players` now binds only within 0.75 m of that spot; a binding `Players` itself made counts as seen (`DC3_POSE_RELEASE_WAIT_MS=0`; off in a song, where an unbound frame pauses). Bound-id changes / hand jumps >0.25 m / frames with player 1 bound, main → landed: res16 8/31/1098 → 3/28/1100; m24 26/160 → 18/143; m17 18/120 → 12/112. Two-player fast-time replays, all three rules off vs on: Better Off Alone and Disco Inferno give identical rating counts for both players, 0 skeleton-loss pauses, scores within 0.2%. The live "134 instant re-binds" count was inflated (a screen that keeps no binding re-requests it every 500 ms): count bound-id changes. The engine fragment shader's alpha fringe fade (`standard_wgsl.inc`, `finalColor *= a / kAlphaFringeFadeStart`) takes it to black, and Multiply makes black a full darken. The image's Multiply (`NgMat`: src Zero, dst SrcColor) ignores alpha, so white leaves the frame alone, and its own `NgMat::SetupFade` fades Multiply/Darken toward (1,1,1,1). Setting the material's alpha to 1 at runtime removes the box. Proposed fix: fade toward the blend's identity, white for Multiply/Darken, through a per-pipeline WGSL `override`. Verified in a scratch engine copy. The empty centre is the authored no-LIVE layout (`center_panel.grp`, which holds the minileaderboard and score, stays hidden). | M | Final-results screenshot matches the Xbox layout |

No pause screen or `pause_panel` in `ui/pause/pause.dta` has a `BUTTON_DOWN_MSG`; resume is only the `resume` handler (CONTINUE → `{ui pop_screen}`). In C++ the only Start case on a shell screen is `ShellInput::OnMsg(ButtonDownMsg)` (100% `name_check`): in controller mode Start calls `ExitControllerMode` and swallows the press. `HamPanel`, `HamScreen`, `UIPanel`, `UIScreen` and `HamUI` have no Start case. | G-14 | **DONE: engine half landed (engine `4e738fe`, dc3 `ff4cea3b3`); `PostProcNoiseTest` now always runs.** The "awesome" post-process covered the whole screen, HUD and pause menu included, in animated static. **Root-caused (`np-postproc`); the fix is an engine change, verified in a scratch engine copy, proposed as `~/tmp/np-postproc-engine.patch`, not landed.** What the original draws (read from the shipped `xbox_shaders` microcode, `postprocess` option bit `0x4`, permutation `0000000000200014`; `0x800000000000` is the midtone form; constants from `NgPostProc::CheckNoise`): the noise is a *texture*, `mNoiseMap`, sampled twice at `(uv + seed) * mNoiseBaseScale` (the second sample also scaled by `mNoiseTopScale`, or by 1 when stationary). The two samples are multiplied to `n`, then overlay-blended into the bloom-screened scene `S` at the intensity `I`: `2nS` where `S < 0.5`, `1 - 2(1-n)(1-S)` otherwise, mixed by `I`. The midtone form uses `sqrt(n)` and branches on luma. It is weighted by `I * 6.75 L (1-L)^2`. The `dci`/`dclive`/`beachfront`/... `_performance_awesome.pp` set `I` = 1, stationary, base scale 1×3 and midtone off (read from `camera.milo` and live). `noise_mono.tex` is an 8×256 DXT1 map of one-texel horizontal stripes in two light greys (about 0.81 and 0.85). So the original look is a static brightening with faint scanlines, not static. The engine instead added `(hash - 0.5) * I` per pixel, re-rolled every frame, which is ±0.5 at `I` = 1. The patch (`PostProcPass.cpp`, `rndshape::PostProcNoiseMap` in `RndShape_DC3.h`, a stub in `RndShape_RB3Wii.h`) binds the map, reproduces both shader forms and keeps stationary seeds fixed. The map is applied after the bloom screen, as in the original; the colour transform is still applied before it. Consumers without the accessors keep the old grain, which is rb3-xenon until it adds them; rb3 is unchanged. dc3 side: `72f309241` adds `GetNoiseMap`/`GetNoiseBaseScale`/`GetNoiseTopScale` (HX_NATIVE) and `PostProcNoiseTest`, compiled only against an engine that defines `MILO_POSTPROC_NOISE_MAP`. **UI ordering, no second bug found:** native grades at `NgRnd::DoPostProcess` (`Rnd::EndWorld`), the image's point. In Perform the move cards are drawn after it and are not graded (measured). The pause screen's `background_pause_panel` and `pause_panel` (`ui/pause/pause.dta`) are drawn before the panel that ends the world, so they are graded, while the tuner and player boxes are not. That this matches the Xbox is INFERRED from the shared code and data. An Xenia capture was tried and failed: guest-memory poke of the pp's noise fields via `/proc/<pid>/mem`; scratch `np-postproc/xenia/poke.py`. The scripted flow stalled at song select, then wandered into Party, and most dumped frames were half black and half blue. Before/after captures and the shader dumps are in the `np-postproc` scratch.  **Correction 2026-11-14 (np-gfx-post round 4):** in game the overlay never actually ran until engine `ecd579f`. The map was never uploaded (no material binds `noise_mono.tex`), and this test presynced it by hand. Xenia's awesome capture now confirms the overlay: the frame is +38% with it (engine doc §34.1). | M | Engine patch lands and `MILO_ENGINE_PIN` moves past it (then `PostProcNoiseTest` joins the gate); an Xenia awesome-state capture to confirm |

| G-27 | **Retail post chain (`np-gfx-post`, 2026-11-14; engine branch `np-gfx-post`, not landed).** DC3's `NgPostProc::DoPost` is ported from the shipped microcode: the per-material pseudo-HDR bloom mask, plain/streak bloom in 8-bit sets, a screen composite with no clamps, the noise overlay, the dp4 colour matrix, the vignette and chroma. The scan-out ramp `pow(v, 0.85)` (`default.dta` `(rnd (gamma 0.85))`) is applied once after the UI, so captures are post-ramp (`DC3_DISPLAY_GAMMA=off` for pre-ramp). Destination alpha follows each draw's equation, not MAX, and a `bloom_multiplier` 0 material writes mask 0. Together these make the UI's debloom quads work: menus match retail video, and the mode icon and song-select cards no longer burn. Menus now bloom (`PostProc_local` I 1.75 / thr 1.0; before, the clamps meant no visible bloom). Evidence: engine `docs/native/dc3-backend-for-rb3-wii.md` §31; `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/post/`. Guards: `native/tests/test_dc3_retail_post.cpp`. **Round 2 (same branches):** motion blur (41 pp incl. the menus' `PostProc_local`; menu transitions now ghost as on Xenia), glare (3 UI pp, none selectable by shipped data), hue converge (`PostProc_Blacklight`, the voice-control letterbox and blacklight pause), `Rnd::DoPostProcess`' override order (blacklight, then `set_postproc_override`, which native ignored), the environ fade-out in the mask; the downsample source settled (retail reads the blurred set, as native already did); hall of time is not drawable on retail (no shader permutation). DCI peak frames now read 0.80–0.97× retail's frame mean (was ~0.57), with the blue cast coming from the peak `bloom_color`. Guards: 13 `DC3RetailPost*` tests, 25 sabotages caught. **Round 3 (same branches):** the velocity blur (bit 42) ported and wired (natively it never ran: `NgPostProc` is not the factory, so `NgRnd::DoPostProcess` now runs `DoVelocity`'s gate); camera term only, from depth, half resolution, no re-draw; cuts reset it as retail does. It shows on fast camera moves only (dci intro sweep, 3–4 px); ≈75 µs on a 3090 at 720p worst case, below noise in a live dci frame. The frame target follows `ActivePostProc()`; `toggle_all_postprocs` honoured. Normal-state frame luma with the chain on matches retail in rink and dclive and sits at the top of retail's range in dci. Guards: 22 tests, sabotages S1–S17. **Round 4 (same branches, against the original under Xenia):** the DCI peak read −30% (scene 35.4 vs Xenia 50.5) because the noise overlay never ran in game: `noise_mono.tex` is bound by no material, so nothing uploaded it and `GetGpuTexView` returned null (engine `ecd579f` presyncs it; dc3 `PostProcNoiseTest.ThePostChainUploadsANoiseMapNothingElseBinds`). A state decomposition shows pre-post scene, colour matrix and bloom already matched; after the fix the peak reads 50.6 vs 50.5 and every same-camera region is inside Xenia's range. The chain's bind groups are cached (H-66). The DCI normal "+43% pendant lamp" is Xenia's: its fork answers every occlusion query with a fixed 1000 samples, so the lamps' `CouchFlare*.flare` draw at ~1/80 strength there; with the fake count raised Xenia reads 100.8–120.3 vs native 107–124, hiding the flares on native reads 77–85 vs Xenia 73–76, and hardware footage shows the glow (guard `FlarePointTestTest`; do not compare flare-lit regions against default Xenia). Engine doc §34. **Round 5 (same branches, no code):** the rollerrink normal floor's missing bloom (Xenia +32 floor luma, native +4; Xenia lane floor 165.6 vs 131.5) is Xenia's, not native's. The rink's seven Multiply light-catcher overlays (`Rink*_overlay`, `StageCarpet_lightCatcher`; blend 6, AllowHDR) write the mask, and the Xenia fork applies MIN/MAX without the blend factors, so the catchers leave max(src.a, dst.a) over the whole floor. A native build with that rule reads mask 34.3/255 vs 3.2, and reproduces Xenia's bloom term (+31.9 vs +32.4) and the lane's regions (floor 160.9 vs 162.2, floor_l 119.7 vs 121.4). With the catchers hidden, both chains agree (+15.6 vs +14.8), which settles threshold, set weights, composite and intensity. Retail rink footage has floor contrast 0.62–0.84. Every Xenia frame reads 0.41–0.48 (washed out), and shipped native reads 0.60–0.70. The menus already refuted MAX (§31.5). The shipped rule stays, guarded by `DC3RetailPostTest.BlendedAlphaFollowsTheDrawsEquation`; the sabotage `kAlphaBlendMax = true` fails 5 checks. On the merged base (np-gfx `bd1da89`/`dfe16cb0f`), the dclive normal floors are unchanged (206.7 vs Xenia 207.5; floor_l 163.9 vs 164.3), the DCI normal floors do not depend on the rule (floor_l 12.1 vs 12.5; floor_r 40.1, 2.2 under Xenia's minimum on one frame and on the MAX build too), and the DCI peak regions are inside Xenia's range. The one exception is DCI peak crowd at one frame (60.3 vs max 52.9, the beat-pulsed stripe band behind the crowd). Do not compare Multiply-overlay regions against default Xenia. Engine doc §35. **Round 6 (same branches, against Xenia's round 2):** depth of field never ran on native. Retail installs `NgDOFProc` from `NgRnd`'s init, which native does not run, so `TheDOFProc` was the base `DOFProc` (`Set` a no-op, `Enabled()` 0), and every intro CLOSEUP/VENUE shot with `use_depth_of_field 1` rendered sharp. dc3 `DOFProc.cpp` now registers a native proc that keeps `NgDOFProc::Set`'s parameters (blur-depth floor, `DOFOverrides`, clamps, game-screen gate). Houseparty intro sharpness drops from 14.2/15.1 to 9.4/10.0, matching Xenia's blurred background on the same forced shots (`venue05` 10.2 vs 11.4, `cu02` 10.7 vs 11.8). Guard: `DC3RetailPostTest.DOFProcKeepsNgDOFProcSetParameters`; sabotages S1–S4 caught. The throneroom normal carpet ("native grade −32% vs Xenia −15%") is not the grade: with bloom off the carpet agrees within 1 level. The gap is the bloom over `carpetShadow.mesh` (Multiply, AllowHDR) under Xenia's factorless MAX alpha, the §35 rink mechanism; a MAX build reproduces it, and with the overlays hidden all agree. The throneroom peak's post lift ("1.62× vs 1.39×") was measured again on frozen frames (`cheat_pause`, the same frame in every state) on the Color-fix base: post 1.53× native vs 1.54–1.58× Xenia, grade 1.228 vs 1.234, bloom median 1.265 vs 1.270. The Multiply overlays play no part there. The 2.5% bloom mean residual is the same alpha artifact over the peak's SrcAlpha/SrcAlphaAdd draws: MAX gives 1.263. Without the Color fix the same measurement gives 1.51×, so the brief's 1.39×/1.62× came from frame means compared across separate runs (INFERRED). Softness: the Xenia lane's own JSON shows native raw = post sharpness on throneroom and native sharper on houseparty, so it is not the post chain; the full-screen passes sample at texel centres. No code change for those three. The throneroom normal carpet is unchanged (`tr_far03` full 22.2, bloom off 21.6, hidden 46.1). Round 6's native numbers, its gate and its consumer checks predate both fixes. Engine doc §37.6. **Round 8 (same branches, no code):** again on np-gfx with the spot lane's beams (merge dc3 `772331126` / engine `6ea6901`; the engine doc's spot section took §36, this lane's is now §37). Throneroom peak, frozen frames: post 1.544× (median 1.547) vs Xenia 1.575 (1.572), grade 1.240 vs 1.234, bloom median 1.261 vs 1.270. The beams are on in those frames: hiding the `SpotlightDrawer`s takes 4.2% off with post on and nothing with post off, as on retail, where `toggle_all_postprocs` skips the drawer's `DoPost`. The 2% post gap is inside the run-to-run spread (another frame mix on the same binary reads 1.653×). Item 3 is closed; the carpet is unchanged. Engine doc §37.7. **Round 9 (same branches; engine `c9cd3ea`, dc3 `3996c5e2a`):** (1) The round-6 sheet's warmer, darker Xenia intro is none of fade-in, post-proc or lighting preset. The selected proc is `world.pp` on both sides with identical fields, and every light matches except houseparty's animated TV light. The round-6 native frames predate the Color/spotlight fixes: floor 45 vs 28, back wall 62 vs 74, B/G 0.54 vs 0.59. On the current base, cu02 reads Y 47.0 vs 48.6, sat 0.562 vs 0.560, B/G 0.58 vs 0.59, and throneroom matches in chroma. Not reproduced; dropped. (2) DOF strength is the same: DC3's `downsample_4x`/blur/`postprocess` bit-3 microcode, disassembled, equals `DofPass` (an exact 4×4 box via mTexHalfPixel, the same taps, and the same depth mapping). A same-run toggle A/B gives a background post/raw of 0.537 on Xenia vs 0.541 on native; the post-frame gap comes from native's softer raw frame. Fixed on the way: `{rnd toggle_all_postprocs}` did not turn native's DOF off (retail's `NgDOFProc` is a post processor that `Rnd::DoPostProcess` skips). Guard `DC3RetailPostGpuTest.TogglePostProcsSkipsDepthOfField`; sabotage `(void)dofRuns` caught. (3) Softness is real on matched raw frames: houseparty BG 13.9 vs 18.3, throneroom near01 13.9 vs 18.6. Most of it is native's 4× world MSAA against the original's 1-sample HD back buffer (`D3DMULTISAMPLE_NONE`). A scratch 1× build matches Xenia's edge profile (AAF 0.18/0.27 vs 0.43/0.47) and closes 2/3 of the houseparty gap and 1/3 of throneroom's. The rest of throneroom's gap is carpet/floor surface detail: `THR_carpet` normal and spec maps, and the floors' `marbleDetail` normal-detail map. It routes to the light lane. Changing `kMSAASamples` is an owner call: it is shared, and DOF, spot beams and velocity need MS depth. Round 6's "native raw = post / houseparty sharper" is withdrawn. With `bloom_intensity 0` on both sides the gap stays (A scene dE 12.9), and MAX closes it there too (2.3), so it is not the bloom chain. The colour sign flips between windows, so it is the streak term's size or extent, not a grade tint. `SetBloomBlurWeightsStreak` weights, offsets and rotation, set sizes, formats, pass order and composite slots all audit equal. The one open lead is retail's +0.5-texel centre tap. Not fixed; the next step is a frozen-frame streak A/B. Its DTA sets on `world.pp` were overwritten every frame by `HamDirector::Poll`, so its windows changed nothing (a native run shows no step at any window edge). On frozen frames through a scratch override proc (`new PostProc`, `copy`, `rnd set_postproc_override`), the streak arm's 0.5-texel centre tap is real: `DxRnd::DrawRect` sets `HalfPixelOffset`, so the 0.5 is a half-texel step per pass, not a convention fix. dclive normal far01, the streak term's position against its own scene: native with centred taps (−0.29, +0.24), the original under Xenia (−1.82, +1.97), native with the 0.5 (−1.70, +1.60), six sets each. Fixed (`kStreakCentreOffset`, DC3 only); guard `DC3RetailPostGpuTest.StreakTermSitsWhereTheOriginalsTapsPutIt` (a CPU model of DoBloom/bloom.ps/SetBloomBlurWeightsStreak, three angles), sabotage `kStreakCentreOffset = 0` fails all five checks at all three. Residual C barely moves: far01 scene dE 5.6 → 4.4, near01 6.4 → 6.4, against a run-to-run spread of ~1.5 dE (raw control 1.8 vs 0.3 on identical code), so C is still open. The plain arm's +0.5 (retail offsets −6.5..7.5) is an open lead: a scratch +0.5 build matches Xenia in x (−0.90 vs −0.87) but not y (−0.12 vs +0.17; centred +0.19); unchanged, since `fs_blur` is RB3's. The rule for reading Xenia is now in one place (§37.10): factorless MAX inflates the Multiply and SrcAlpha families, so screen census rows with a MAX build first. rb3-xenon frames identical (11/11). Engine doc §37.10; evidence `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/post/round10/`. **Round 10 Part 2 (same branches; engine `56ef072`): the enhanced post look** under `DC3_LOOK` (default enhanced; retail unchanged; the world grade only, UI posts stay retail). Three `LookSettings` fields, each its own `native_look` token. `postPrecision`: 16-bit bloom sets with the per-pass saturate, a soft knee, and a static TPDF composite dither; at render scale 2 the box filter into the present and the readback dithers with a fixed 4×4 Bayer pattern (exact levels stay exact). `postBloomFilter`: a tent downsample, a plain-arm combine pass and B-spline upsample, the streak set1 as box4 of set0 (same energy ×0.991–0.999 and extent; plain wobble 0.364 → 0.010 px, streak energy CV 1.48 → 0.002). `postDofFilter`: tent downsample, B-spline composite with the taps pulled in to keep the radius (6.74 → 6.72 px; creases 0.519 → 0.328). Cost (3090, interleaved A/B): post +0.027 ms dci / +0.017 dclive at 1x; DOF composite 0.0157 → 0.0216 ms per DOF frame; at 2x post +0.047, DOF composite +0.024 (kept: integrated GPUs stay at 1x). 890M INFERRED 0.15–0.25 ms of 0.36. UI/HUD: `UiPostIgnoresTheLook` 0 bytes; opaque HUD and song-select fills 0 px changed at 1x and 2x. Live stability: the first run's gap was window parity (null and swap controls). The sparse-dot light board strobes in Xenia too, so its live blink cannot separate the looks. Web: `scripts/web/build.sh --release` builds (exit 0); the chain needs no optional WebGPU feature (rgba16float render, blend and linear filter are core), so the fields stay on there. Guards: `native/tests/test_dc3_look_post.cpp` (12 tests incl. the scale-2 variants) and the enhanced arms of `SteadyFrameCreatesNoBindGroup`; 22 sabotages caught. | G-28 | **Light residuals with post off (`np-gfx-light` round 7, 2026-11-14; record only, no code).** Screened frozen, lit, post off against Xenia, with a `kAlphaBlendMax = true` build as the control for Xenia's factorless-MAX destination alpha. **Fixed by np-menutext's coverage rule:** throneroom peak pillars (alpha_cut had been alpha-to-coverage on top of a SrcAlpha blend; 46.4 → 53.0 vs Xenia 52.9 under both `DC3_LOOK`s). **Dissolved as Xenia factorless MAX:** streetside brick/graffiti (post-on −22/−16% → −2/−2%; post-off −2/−3%), and the post-on share of dclive peak sky. **Dissolved as box artifact or occlusion:** throneroom normal throne, houseparty neon steps, streetside fence, rollerrink booth_top. **Answered:** hidden spotlights do not light on native. Two reading rules: a box with a dancer or crowd silhouette in it is not a lighting measurement, and a post-on gap is re-read post-off before it is called lighting. **Round 8 (2026-11-15, docs only), both open rows closed:** (1) *Houseparty fridge +18%* is a Xenia artifact. Xenia answers every occlusion query with a fixed 1000 samples, so it under-draws every area-tested flare. On the fridge's lit pixels, the gap goes from +32% to +3% with `point_test FALSE`. (2) *dclive peak sky −10%* is the texture term of `DLV_PeakStageCube01–03`: −17..−41% with the texture, +3% without it. It is minification and filtering, handed to the sharpness lane. The original's world target is A8R8G8B8, as ours is. Engine `docs/native/dc3-retail-lighting.md` §12–13; `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/light/round7/`, `round8/` | M | DONE (round 8) |

The sort button cycles Skill Level → Title → Artist → Source (`by_artist` is a name the image carries and never implemented). Left/Right jump to the previous/next section, LB/RB move a screenful (keyboard: arrows, Q/E). Playlist building keeps the image's list. All in native-only files or under `HX_NATIVE`; the 360 objects are byte-identical with it merged (989 of 989). | G-30 | **Song select for a pad or keyboard player is the natively drawn song browser (`np-song-wire`, 2026-11-20).** `SongBrowserScreen` (Recently Played shelf, sectioned list, type-to-search, detail pane) hangs on the image's `song_select_panel` through `HX_NATIVE` hooks; the classic panel stays loaded and entered underneath and only its drawing and its pad presses are taken. Switch `DC3_SONG_BROWSER`: `auto` (desktop default: shown unless somebody steers by hand -- a hand held in the list's own pose for 0.75 s, Start with a body in view, or a song select reached by gesture; a tracked body alone never hides it; a press or a typed character brings it back at once), `1`, `0`; the web defaults to `0`; `DC3_CLASSIC_SONG_SELECT=1` and an unset switch under `MILO_INPUT_SCRIPT` mean the classic list. Only on pick-one-song visits (Perform, Rehearse, Dance Battle). Pick is the panel's `special_select_song`, Back its `on_back`; the classic highlight follows the browser's song, which carries the preview, the song info and the highlight sound. The help bar and the two corner boxes are not drawn under it. The `DC3_NATIVEUI_DEMO` overlay and `SampleBrowser` are retired. Random Song and Playlists are rows above the first section, only the ones this visit's classic list has, and run the classic list's own rows (`onCommand`, `POST /api/songbrowser/command`); the look is the game's indigo with a glass panel, a filled highlight only where focus is, star icons and a hint bar of pad discs and keycaps; the hint bar names the device last used; the preview starts once the selection has rested 0.3 s. Open: keyboard and pad are told apart by asking GLFW at press time (the engine has no query for it, and the web always reads as the keyboard); the overlay and dialog cases are untested; not opened in a browser (web compiles with it off). [SONG_SELECT.md](SONG_SELECT.md#the-song-browser), [reference/SONG_BROWSER_UI.md](reference/SONG_BROWSER_UI.md) | M | Owner plays it with a pad and with a camera |

Not IDBFS: its whole-tree, timestamp-driven sync would let a second tab put an older profile back. `?reset_save=1` empties the store; `?save=off` neither reads nor writes it; IndexedDB missing or silent for 5 s: the game runs as before. Measured: `scripts/web/save-persist.mjs` drives one browser profile through four page loads (fresh, reload, browser restart, reset): 26 of 26 checks pass; with `&save=off` 15 of 26 fail, and a build without the store fails 10 of 15. Read from the matched code (`Load`, `Draw3DChars`, `DrawShowing`, `~WorldCrowd` 100 `name_check`), and Xenia's YMCA capture shows the 3D crowd on the stands. Native diverged in three `HX_NATIVE` blocks of `world/Crowd.cpp`: `Load` forced `Force3DCrowd(false)`, `Draw3DChars` broke out before drawing, `DrawShowing` rendered each character once into a cached texture drawn `kBlendAdd`; that draw was deferred by the transparent queue and reached the flush with the restored alpha-cut material over alpha-0 texels, so **no crowd reached the screen at all**. All three removed (same deletion as the unlanded `w23-crowd` `d64eaf6ba`); Xbox bytes unchanged. Cost (desktop GPU, YMCA, `DC3_CROWD_STATS` `ms=`): 0.48–0.64 ms CPU per frame with 27 members drawn, 0.80–0.89 ms with 57; whole-frame times of the same window varied 5.6–21 ms between identical runs on the shared GPU, so the crowd's share cannot be read from them. Web (release, Chromium/WebGPU, YMCA played to results): 91 samples, 0 impostors, pose changed in every sample, 0.77 ms per frame with 27 members and 1.33 ms with 57, and the page held 59 fps across gameplay; there is no pre-fix web run to compare with. Test: `CrowdTest` (no GPU, ~9 s): every sample draws the crowd in 3D, none as impostors, and the drawn pose changes; 3/3 sabotages caught. **Engine commit** `71f76f1`, in the series `np-crowd-engine` of the clone `/home/free/tmp/claude-1000/engine-crowd` on engine `fe22a2e`: `2035eb9` ThreadCall (np-macprep2), `71f76f1` this colour commit, `84960a9` vertex re-sync and `17d00b6` render-target alpha rule (both np-rehearse, G-11). Verified together on dc3, rb3 and rb3-xenon by np-crowd-land; not landed; the pin commit on this branch says what to amend. With the alpha rule on, `PerformCrowdTest` (np-rehearse) passes on this branch: the crowd is drawn in 3D, so the old native impostor path that turned into white figures is not reached and np-rehearse's `kNativeCrowdImpostors = false` is not needed. The colour commit itself: DC3 `MatColorMod` from `RndMat::GetColorModFlags`/`GetColorMods`, and no alpha fringe fade on texels whose alpha is a `kColorModModulate` band selector (bit 8 of `colorMod.w`). It is the colour half of engine `fe29598` (`w23-crowd`), with the getters behind a detection trait so rb3-xenon (which lacks them) still compiles and renders as before (both rb3-xenon users of `RndShape_DC3.h` compile; `fe29598` does not). It does not touch the render-target alpha write; that is np-rehearse's `17d00b6` in the same series (the white figures np-rehearse saw with it came from the native impostor path this branch deletes). The image's rule, for whoever lands one: `SetColorWriteMask` (`rndobj/Shader.cpp`) is per draw, not per target: alpha is written when `force_alpha_write ? alpha_write : (alpha_write \|\| opts&0x400000 \|\| TheNgRnd.Offscreen())`, `Offscreen()` being "the current camera has a target texture", and `DxCam::Select` clears a target to colour 0, alpha 0 (except `kDepthVolumeMap`). The patch applies on engine `4e738fe` (it joins the band-selector test to that merge's per-pipeline `kSkipFringeFade` in the fringe-fade `if`). YMCA gameplay with it on `4e738fe`: palette-coloured crowd, no blotches on the dancers (frames 7760/8000/8450); the two patched files compile with rb3-xenon's flags. **Without it the crowd draws with untinted clothes and black skin**; with it, palette colours as on Xenia. `crowd_m_* could not find group _00s_cameraperson_ok` is the shipped data: the crowd anim files define only `_00s_cameraperson` and `_00s_cameraperson_realtime_idle`, and the original logs the same kind of notify under Xenia. Now (`WindowLoss_X11.cpp`): each frame, before `BeginDrawing`, the window's `DestroyNotify` is taken off the X queue, and an X error handler treats `BadWindow`/`BadDrawable` on our window as the same loss; both end the loop (`detail=window_lost`, rc 0). Every `exit()` route reports and `_exit`s with its own code via glibc `on_exit`, skipping the static destructors as the loop's return already did (the image never runs them). **SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP = quit at the next frame boundary**, so a save written synchronously in that frame completes; rc stays 128+sig so an external stop never reads as a natural end; a second signal or 5 s stops at once. A window manager's close (`WM_DELETE_WINDOW`) was already clean (`window_closed`, rc 0) and is now tested. | H-10 | ~~`DC3_FAST_TIME` scoring dies ~3 min into a run whose song clock is slower than the wall clock~~ **DONE 2026-11-13 (`np-loadmusic`, `084d9d350`).** Cause (measured): FAST_TIME's song clock is synthetic (`LiveInput::CurrentMs`, 1/120 s a frame) but the song's stream plays on the wall clock, so on a paced or GPU run the stream reached its end mid-song (ymca's 152 s of audio). A finished `StandardStream` is `kFinished`, not `IsReady()`, and the image's `MoveDir::PostUpdateFilters` enqueues filter jobs only while the audio `IsReady()`: from then on no frame was scored and every move rated bad. A probe showed the stream finished from song ≈42 s paced at 30 fps, the measure the ratings turned. Fix: `CurrentMs` (HX_NATIVE, FAST_TIME only) stops the stream once it leads the song clock by more than 100 ms and plays it again when the song catches up (`native/src/platform/FastTimeAudio.h`); a game pause clears the hold. So under FAST_TIME the music follows the song clock and never ends first. `LiveInput::CurrentMs`, `::SetPaused` and the ctor stay 100% `name_check`. Measured, ymca paced 30 to song 50 s: before 17/0/0/7, after 24/0/0/0 with stream time 52124 ms at song 52.08 s, never finished. Paced 60 to song 100 s (232 s of wall clock, past the old ~3 min cliff): 42/0/0/0, where before it went bad from measure 42. Test: `FastTimeAudioTest` (milo-tests) pins the rule and simulates it at the paced-60, paced-30 and GPU rates, with a control that runs the old code and requires the early end. Sabotages: no hold → 2 of 4 fail; never release → 2 of 4 fail. The wiring in `CurrentMs` is covered by the measured runs, not by an automated test (a paced flow test needs minutes of wall clock). | S–M |

Budget (measured): desktop reads the ark, 1,420,945 bytes (1.36 MiB) of the 2 MiB budget, nothing evicted. Web reads the inflated extraction, 5,709,552 bytes (5.45 MiB), so the web build gets ×4 (8 MiB), as PreloadPanel does for W-6; over budget `FileCache::DumpOverSize` evicts equal-priority entries in host `std::sort` order. The cache is separate from the song preload cache (`PreloadPanel::sCache`); each has its own budget, and `GetFileAll` searches both. Music (measured, ymca and omg): the load serves the era's `.mid` from the cache and the stream plays (LoadingPanel's `Unload` trace: ymca 865–1287 ms streamed on desktop, 5156 ms on web; omg 4150 ms on web). Null-backend audio dump during loading: −15 to −19 dB vs main's −32 dB (silence). Loading time unchanged (frames to gameplay ≈12.6k vs 13.2k). Web release build, ymca + omg to gameplay + 10 s: 0 page errors, no `Forced to dump`. Tests: `PersistentFileCacheTest.DesktopHoldsEveryEntry` (with a one-byte-under control that must evict) and `.WebBytesFitTheWebBudget`, `LoadingPanelTest.LoadingMusicLoadsFromThePersistentCache` + control `.WithoutTheCacheTheMidIsNotFound`, flow `LargestSongFlowTest.LoadingMusicStreams`. Sabotages: cache block removed → the flow test fails (assert line, no cache hit); web budget unscaled → both PersistentFileCache tests fail. **Left:** no native renderer calls `Rnd::Init`, so `RndUtlInit` never registers `ResourceFileCacheHelper` (and `RndPostProc::Init` never runs). The list's one `.png` (`ui/image/dc_logo_keep.png`) is therefore keyed raw and caches nothing, which adds a fourth web 404 (`/api/file/ui/image/dc_logo_keep.png`). | H-12 | ~~Gate runs collide when lanes run them at once~~ **DONE 2026-11-13 (`np-harness`).** `GestureMenusTest` died because another process held its port (48041). Every launcher chose the port itself and passed it in: `gesture_menu_flow.py`, the twoplayer and camera-view tests, partyplay (9500+pid%400), the harvest (9191), `pose_scoring_gate.sh` and `test_lighting_events.sh` (7790). Found on the way: cpp-httplib binds with `SO_REUSEPORT`, so **two engines on one port both listened and split the requests** (measured, 9290). Now the engine binds `DC3_HTTP_PORT=0` and writes `DC3_HTTP_PORT_FILE`. It uses `SO_REUSEADDR` only, and a taken port is exit 98 (`detail=http_port_in_use`), not `abort()`. `/api/health` carries the pid. Fixed per-box test paths are per process now (`TestScratchDir()`, the input scripts, failure dumps), and so is the default socket of a spawned pose server. Guards: `ConcurrentFlowTest.TwoCopiesOfAFlowRunAtOnce`, `NativeExitTest.{HttpPortInUseIsANamedExit, SecondEngineOnAPortExitsInsteadOfSharing}`; 3 of 3 sabotages caught. Two concurrent `GestureMenusTest` processes both pass (ports 44633 / 45183). See [TESTING.md](TESTING.md#running-alongside-other-gates) | S–M |

A windowed frame can only be read between submit and present, so the engine gained `WgpuRnd::SetFrameReadyCallback` and `GpuDevice::ReadbackFrame` (headless target or the swapchain texture, BGRA → RGBA), and `/api/screenshot` answers there; with no GPU device it is served after `EndDrawing` as before. Under `HX_NATIVE` a prefix that matches one of those roots as whole path components (case-insensitively, so an already lower-cased prefix is restored) keeps the host's spelling; the game-relative rest is lower-cased as before. `FileNormalizePath` stays 100.0% (`name_check`). Tests: `HostPathCaseTest.*` (6, in-process) and `HeadlessBootTest.BootsFromUpperCaseHostDirectories` (a hard-linked `dc3-native` in an upper-case tree, `DC3_DATA` in an upper-case dir). Sabotages: no prefix kept: 6 of 7 fail; no component-boundary check: the seventh fails. The kit's asset check (`asset_manifest.py`) no longer refuses such a path (dropped 2026-11-14) | S |
| H-18 | ~~The game draws 1280×720 into the top-left of any larger window~~ **DONE 2026-11-14 (`np-window`)**. Was: the swapchain followed the window while viewports and UI follow the Rnd virtual resolution (1280×720, `Rnd.cpp:318`), so a resized, maximised, fullscreen, tiled or HiDPI window showed the picture in its corner. Fix (engine branch `np-window`, `8d59fbf` + `5c46b18`, at the present level only — no render pass restructured): the frame is drawn into a fixed 1280×720 texture and `gfx/PresentScale` copies it into the swapchain as the largest 16:9 rectangle that fits, centred, black bars (letterbox/pillarbox), nearest sampling at whole-number scales and linear otherwise; F11 and Alt+Enter toggle fullscreen on the window's monitor; `MILO_FULLSCREEN=1` (and `scripts/play.sh --fullscreen`) starts fullscreen; `GLFW_SCALE_TO_MONITOR` sizes the window by the content scale; ImGui's display size and mouse coordinates are mapped through the same rectangle. `MILO_PRESENT_SCALE=0` restores the old path. It composes with `np-gfx`'s scan-out gamma ramp (`DisplayRamp`): that pass reads `SurfaceTexture()`/`FrameTarget()`, which are now the 1280×720 frame, so the ramp applies at frame size and the scale after it (`git merge-tree` of the two engine branches: clean). **MEASURED** under Xvfb (`NativeWindowTest.*`, `test_native_exit.cpp`): a 1600×1000 window shows the picture at 1600×900+0+50; resized to 1800×700 → 1244×700+278+0 and to 1000×900 → 1000×563+0+168, bars pure black; F11 → fullscreen 1920×1200 and back to 1280×720, Alt+Enter the same; `MILO_FULLSCREEN=1` → 1920×1080 on a 1920×1080 screen; `Xft.dpi: 192` → a 2560×1440 window and surface, sampled nearest. Found and fixed on the way: Alt+Enter toggled fullscreen **and pressed A** (GLFW sticky keys: the chord returned before Enter was read, so the tap stayed latched and landed when Alt was released; `Joypad_Native.cpp` now reads every bound key first). Tests: `PresentScale.*` (9), `JoypadKeyMap.ATapInsideAChordIsConsumedWithIt`, `NativeWindowTest.{TheFrameIsScaledToFitTheWindow,F11AndAltEnterToggleFullscreen,MiloFullscreenStartsFullscreen,AnX11ContentScaleSizesTheWindow}`. Sabotages: `MILO_PRESENT_SCALE=0` fails the first three (right strip 0.0% lit); `GLFW_SCALE_TO_MONITOR` off fails the HiDPI one (window 1280×720); the modifier-first key read fails the chord test. Resize soak (**MEASURED**, Xvfb, windowed engine on the main menu, a resize every 0.25 s cycling 1600×1000 / 1000×900 / 1800×700, box load 92–122): 300 resizes RSS 524,432 → 530,760 kB (+6.2 MB), 900 resizes 524,200 → 529,116 kB (+4.8 MB), flat 10 s later in both; an idle control of the same length moved −0.2 / +2.0 MB. Growth does not scale with the resize count, so no per-resize leak; the engine kept drawing throughout (PERF 7–14 fps, the box's load, not the resizes). **INFERRED, not tested here** (no compositor on this box): Wayland HiDPI (the prebuilt Dawn has no Wayland surface, H-33, so every window is X11/XWayland today) and the ImGui mouse mapping in `milo-viewer`. Follow-ups: H-55, H-56, H-57 | M |

| H-20 | ~~With a moving person on the sensor, only the first pad press on a menu lands~~ **DONE 2026-11-14 (`np-window`, `75daa3cc4`)**. Was (`np-playtest-kit`, 2026-11-13): on `choose_mode_screen` three Down presses moved the highlight 0→1→1→1 while a replayed dancer moved in view (controller mode `forced`). Cause, traced: neither suspect (`RndAnimatable::IsAnimating`, `GestureMgr::GesturingWithVoice`) — the lost presses never reached `HamNavList::OnMsg`. When the dancer's arms went up, `ShellInput::Poll`'s hands-up arm (the image's, `HandsUpGestureFilter`, 1200 ms) opened `correct_identity_panel`, the Kinect "who is this?" overlay. Its `enter` calls `shell_input exit_controller_mode` and sets `helpbar_allow_controller FALSE`, and its `BUTTON_DOWN_MSG` answers (returns 0 for) every press except `kAction_Cancel`. On the 360 the exit hands control to the hands, which drive the overlay; under `forced` the exit is a no-op, so nothing could drive it and it only swallowed the pad. **MEASURED** before the fix (macarena `.dc3session`, `DC3_POSE_SESSION_SYNC=wall`): 17 of 26 scripted presses lost, all after the arms went up. Fix: under `forced` the overlay does not open (`ShellInput.cpp`, `HX_NATIVE`, `Dc3ControllerModeForced()`); `auto`/`off` keep the image's behaviour. `?Poll@ShellInput@@QAAXXZ` stays 100.0%, 564/564 instructions equal under `name_check`. Test: `PadWithAPersonTest.RaisedHandsDoNotTakeThePadUnderForcedControllerMode` (`DC3_DTA_FLOW_TESTS`; fixture `native/tests/data/pose/hands_up_held.dc3pose`, a standing frame with both arms raised): Down, Down, A on `choose_mode_screen` must select item 2 and the overlay must not load. A 64 KB `M_MMAP_THRESHOLD` instead (`GLIBC_TUNABLES`) still grew 336 → 452 MB. Gate: `MemoryFlatSongOverSongTest` (3 distinct songs, ~130 s): fails with `DC3_MEM_TRIM=0` (rss +25 MB over songs 2–3, +127 MB vs pre-song) and with the widget fix reverted (+1045 objects). (1) `oversized: count_in.lbl (ui/hud/static_hud.milo)`: `RndText::SizeCheck` (100%) remembers one text and its height and re-notifies when a *different* text is checked or the same one grows. The label art really is oversized: an 89 px bitmap font animated to 112–152 px. (2) `creating target cache for <target>, targets changed while playing camera`: `HamCamShot::StartAnim` (100%) ends with `sCache.clear()`, after the shot has filled the cache, so `EndAnim` (100%) misses in `GetTargetCache` for every target and notifies once per target per shot end (5 targets, MEASURED 17–18× per shot name in a 14 min process). | H-28 | **The kit's own probes raise errors in the game.** **Fixed (`np-soak2`, 2026-11-14).** The FitnessProvider FAIL and the recovered SIGSEGV were one bug. `kitdrive` read `data_symbol 4` of a 4-row list (`get_num_display` is the slots on screen, 5). The range FAIL returned, because MILO_FAIL is non-fatal on native. `DataSymbol` then read `mFitnessOptions[4]` past the vector's end, and serializing that garbage symbol crashed. Fixes: (a) `/api/dta/eval` evaluates under a try scope (`DtaEval::FailScope`). A FAIL in a debug script throws back to the endpoint (the image's MILO_TRY path) and the eval answers an error instead of running into undefined behaviour. Tests `DtaEvalSupportTest.FailInsideEvalStopsTheScriptAndReportsIt` / `FailScopePassesACleanScriptThrough`; sabotaged by removing `SetTry(true)`: 4 expectations fail. (b) HamNavList gets a native-only `num_data` handler (`HamNavList::Handle` still 100%), and `kitdrive.rows()` reads no further. Test `KitDriveRowsTest`; sabotaged back to `get_num_display`: fails asking row 4 of 4. (c) Every link of the focus-list probe is guarded (a screen without a focus panel or a panel without the list FAILed: 15 in one try). Test sabotaged by dropping the panel links: fails. MEASURED: full soak 1, **0 FAIL/ASSERT kinds** in all three processes (soak #5: 1 FAIL + 1 SIGSEGV; try2 before (c): 15 FAILs) | S |
| H-29 | ~~dc3-native draws as fast as it can and recompiles pipelines every frame~~ **DONE 2026-11-14 (`np-cpu-perf`; engine branch `np-cpu-perf`, not landed).** Three changes. (1) **Frame cap** (`DC3_FPS_CAP`, `native/src/platform/FramePacer_Native.cpp`): the image never draws more than 60 fps (`rnd sync 1` → `D3DRS_PRESENTINTERVAL 1`), native drew at the panel's rate (138 fps on the GPD's 144 Hz panel: main thread 55%, package power 25.3 W in the menu). Windowed now sleeps out each frame at 60 snapped to a whole number of refreshes (72 on 144 Hz, 60 on 120/240; off at ≤ 75 Hz, where vsync already paces); headless is never capped unless asked, so flow tests keep running faster than real time. (2) **Render-pipeline cache** for `DrawRect2D` and particles (engine `gfx/RenderPipelineCache.h`): both built a pipeline per draw, and Dawn's cache only holds live pipelines, so every flare/rect/particle draw went WGSL → Tint → SPIR-V → spirv-val → driver each frame: 18% of the gameplay main thread on the rig. (3) **Vulkan object labels** only under a capture layer or `MILO_GPU_LABELS=1` (5–8% of the main thread). Measured, GPD headless (clean box, uncapped, main-thread CPU ms per frame, two runs each): gameplay 11.1/11.5 → 6.3/8.1 (−36%); menu 4.10/4.14 → 3.91/3.93; song select 5.36/5.41 → 5.02/5.35; results 5.24/5.26 → 4.93/4.95. Base gameplay drew only ~7.7 W on the GPU: the frame was CPU-bound compiling pipelines. Rendering: YMCA screenshots base vs new differ no more than base vs base (menu frames < 0.3 mean abs diff; gameplay frames differ run to run by timing); flares, particles and HUD present. Tests: `FrameCap.*` (3), `FramePacer.*` (4, incl. sleeps-not-spins with a busy-wait sabotage caught), `GpuLabels.OffUnlessAskedOrUnderACaptureLayer`, `RenderPipelineCacheKey.EveryPerDrawInputSeparatesPipelines`, `HeadlessBootTest.FrameCapWhenAsked`, and `SurvivesMainLoop` asserts the headless default is uncapped. Profiling driver: `scripts/native_cpu_profile.py` (menu, song select, gameplay, results; per-thread CPU, fps, ms/frame, hwmon watts, optional `perf` dwarf call graphs). Left: windowed after-numbers on the GPD (its panel was off for the whole session, so FIFO throttled to ~1 fps; with the screen off the game used ~1% CPU) | S |
| H-30 | ~~Bind groups made per draw: 14–22% of the main thread~~ **DONE 2026-11-14 (`np-drawperf`; engine branch `np-drawperf`, not landed).** The mesh path (material, object, bone), the shadow casters, the particles and `DrawRect2D` take their bind groups from `gfx/BindGroupCache.h`: key = layout plus every entry's (binding, buffer, offset, size, view, sampler); entries pin their handles so a recycled pointer cannot alias a dead group; groups unused for 120 frames age out (swept every 60), hard cap 8192, `Clear()` on `Terminate` and with the texture caches, `Forget(view)` when a render target is recreated, a bitmap re-uploaded or a texture destroyed. The object/bone/material uniform blocks are reached through a **dynamic offset** into a group bound at ring offset 0 (`PipelineManager::SetDynamicUniformOffsets`, opt-in; dc3 `WgpuRnd` turns it on, rb3's `BandRnd` does not), so uniform *contents* — phase, environ, boxLight, retail fields — are never part of the key. MEASURED (perf, main-thread inclusive, base 264c74b → candidate, box load 44–50): `APICreateBindGroup` menu 6.35→2.68%, song select 9.18→2.34%, gameplay 13.94→1.77%, results 17.30→2.53%; the cache lookup itself costs 1.9–3.3%. Main-thread instructions per frame (`native_cpu_profile.py --instructions`, H-30+H-31 together, 4 base runs vs 6 candidate runs): menu 20.7–20.9 → 11.0–11.9 Mi, song select 28.5–31.2 → 16.3–16.6, gameplay 37.4–38.4 → 22.7–24.6, results 27.5–27.8 → 15.2–15.7 (−37% to −45%). Bounded across songs (MEASURED, 3-song soak with `MILO_GPU_COUNTERS=600`): live groups 48–208, 10,911 created / 10,806 aged out / 0 uncached, about 1,000 hits a frame; menu idle 100k frames: 95 live, no growth. Pixels unchanged: milo-viewer (4 scenes) and rb3-xenon `rb3-render` (11 PNGs) byte-identical base vs candidate. Tests: `native/tests/test_bind_group_cache.cpp`. What still creates groups: H-65, H-66 | M |
| H-31 | ~~One `Queue::WriteBuffer` per draw for uniforms~~ **DONE 2026-11-14 (`np-drawperf`).** The four uniform rings are staged (`UniformRingBuffer::SetStaged`): draws write a CPU shadow, `WgpuRnd::FlushUniformRings()` issues one `WriteBuffer` per ring per frame in `EndDrawing` before the frame's single `Submit` (a ring that grows mid-frame flushes into the old buffer first). MEASURED (same runs as H-30): `UniformRingBuffer::Write` 3.7–11.6% → 0.14–0.99%, the flush 0.7–1.1%; all `APIWriteBuffer` menu 3.79→2.61%, song select 7.01→5.89%, gameplay 9.70→1.41%, results 11.54→2.31%. What is left in song select is `EnsureMeshUploaded` from `RndText` (65 of 83 samples) = H-32. Soak: about 4 `WriteBuffer`s a frame, ~450 KB a frame (the flush uploads the used span, alignment padding included) | S–M |
| H-32 | ~~**`RndText` meshes re-uploaded every frame: ~5% of the menu**~~ **DONE 2026-11-15 (`np-framecost`).** The engine's mesh cache re-read every *unnamed* mesh on every call, on the guess that it was a glyph page. Every glyph-page writer ends in `RndMesh::Sync`, so the cache is now sync-gated for every mesh. An audit found 0 of 1.54M forced re-reads with different geometry. MEASURED `meshUploads` per frame, rollerrink/YMCA with the score moving: song select 111→2, gameplay 9.46→0.01, results 25→0. Seven frozen-frame A/B points are byte-identical. Guard: `TextFrameCostTest`. See engine `docs/native/dc3-backend-for-rb3-wii.md` §38.1. **Same branch, two more frame costs.** (a) *First-song-frame pipeline stall*: the 14 pipelines with fixed keys (shadow caster ×2, DOF ×3, spot beams ×7, point test ×2) are compiled in `WgpuRnd::Init` (`PrewarmFixedKeyPipelines`, DC3 only: `rndshape::kDC3RetailPost`). MEASURED, same binary with and without the warm, runs interleaved: pipelines compiled in the first 121 song frames 31 → 17, and non-material ones 17 → 3. Init pays 23 ms on a quiet box. Every pipeline creation is now counted (`[GPUCOUNT] pipelineCreates=`), and each frame that creates one logs a `[GPUPIPE]` line. What is left mid-song is material pipelines, whose keys are dynamic: 14–15 at the venue frame and about 24 more during the song. Leads: a key census with `PreWarmStep`, Dawn's blob cache, async creation. Guard: `FixedKeyPipelinesTest` (§38.2). rb3-render's per-frame pipeline counts are unchanged. (b) *Texture creates*: every create during a song is a texture's first upload. None re-creates a texture its `RndTex` already had. A byte-identical bitmap now shares one GPU texture. This applies to `kRegular` only, never a render target. The key includes format, level count and type, and a full byte compare confirms each match. A holder whose bitmap changes takes its own texture, and the last holder destroys the shared one. texCreates per rollerrink song 597 → 402: 250 → 129 after the venue frame. Pixels are identical at 7 points. Guard: `TexShareTest`, which reads back from the GPU (§38.3). Lane notes: `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/spotperf/round2/` | S |
| H-33 | ~~A failed window surface spins one core at 6,000–7,000 fps with nothing on screen~~ **DONE 2026-11-14 (`np-window`, engine `8d59fbf`; dc3 `98ab0600e`)**. Was (GPD, Hyprland): `Error: Unsupported sType`, an invalid surface, every present returning at once. Why Wayland fails: **the prebuilt Dawn is built without Wayland surface support** (no `SurfaceSourceWaylandSurface`; `vkCreateWaylandSurfaceKHR` is never resolved), so a native Wayland GLFW window can never be presented to. Fix: `GpuDevice::InitSurface` checks the surface (`GetCapabilities` fails for a window system Dawn cannot present to); a Wayland window that fails is closed and reopened through X11 (`GLFW_PLATFORM` X11, i.e. XWayland) with one log line saying so; a window no platform can present to is a named exit, `DC3_EXIT: how=exit_runtime detail=window_surface_unusable code=97` with `DC3 Native: FATAL - …` naming the cause. At run time a surface whose acquire keeps failing sleeps 16 ms per attempt and, after 5 s without one frame, exits the same way. `MILO_GLFW_PLATFORM=x11|wayland|auto` forces a platform; `MILO_SURFACE_FAULT=init|init-once|acquire` is the test hook. Tests (`NativeWindowTest`): `AnUnusableSurfaceIsANamedExit` (rc 97), `AnUnusableFirstSurfaceReopensTheWindow` (the reopen runs and the game draws), `ASurfaceThatGivesNoFramesStopsWithoutSpinning` (with the H-29 cap off, `DC3_FPS_CAP=0`, since the cap alone holds the loop near 60/s and hid the sabotage): **MEASURED** 301 acquire attempts in 5 s with the fix; with the sleep removed 19,100 and 19,853 in 5 s (~3,900/s) and the test fails. Sabotages: the Rnd init fatal disabled fails the exit test (still running at 180 s) and `init-once`; the X11 reopen removed fails `init-once`. **Not tested**: a real Wayland compositor (none on this box; the failure is injected with `MILO_SURFACE_FAULT`). A Dawn with Wayland surfaces is H-57 | S |

The rAF loop now asks `Dc3RafGate` (`FramePacer_Native.cpp`, web-only use; desktop's `Dc3FramePacer` is unchanged): display rate from the one-refresh rAF gaps (not the median of all gaps, which read half the panel's rate once frames ran over a refresh, MEASURED), the desktop's `Dc3FrameCapDecide` snapping (144 → 72, 120 → 60, 165 → 82.5, 240 → 60, ≤ 60 Hz untouched), late frames made up on the next refresh, `DC3_FPS_CAP` as on desktop. Tests `RafGate.*` (7; 7 sabotages each caught, table in WEB.md). Browser: `scripts/web/frame-cap.mjs --hz N` emulates an N Hz rAF; MEASURED 144/120/165 Hz capped at 72/60/82.5 with one decision, control `DC3_FPS_CAP=0` 68–101 game fps with 0 skips, the same run under the capped check fails. Not measured: a real high-refresh panel (none on the rig). [WEB.md](WEB.md#frame-cap-on-high-refresh-displays-h-34-np-web4) | S |

| H-45 | **Done in part (`np-soak3`, MEASURED): the headless render growth was the unpaced headless loop; with H-75 it is +13 MB over 10 songs.** Song select samples, one process, 10 Perform songs (5 disc, 5 downloadable: macarena, milkshake, cupidshuffle, badromance, iceicebaby, toxic, 12step, partyrockanthem, supersonic, getlow), headless GPU, `DC3_FAST_TIME`, `scripts/playtest/mem_songs.py`. Engine main (unpaced): rss 621 → 669 → 674 → 677 → 678 → **791** → 801 → 804 → **865** → 830 → 833 MB, heap in use 348 → 402 MB, `/dev/nvidia*` mappings 61 → 63 → … → **105** → … → **133** → 115 MB, `Hmx::Object`s flat at 26,227 from song 4 on (census: no class grows). Engine branch `np-soak3`, 2 frames in flight (H-75): rss 530 → 507 → 510 → 503 → 503 → 514 → 506 → 516 → 517 → 517 → **520** MB, heap in use 311 → 324 MB, driver mappings 21–25 MB throughout, objects the same 26,227. ProcessEvents only, unbounded (`MILO_HEADLESS_FRAMES_IN_FLIGHT=0`): 603 → 770 → … → 838 MB, driver 57 → 143 MB, i.e. worse than main. So the steps (+113 MB at song 5, +61 at song 8 here; +36 at song 3 in the earlier run) were the driver sizing its descriptor/command/staging pools to the deepest backlog an uncapped loop (200–7,000 fps) had queued, and the heap profile above was the same thing seen from inside. Not attributed: the residual +13 MB of heap in use over 9 songs with throttling (~1.4 MB a song, no object class moving; live pipelines grow 51 → 68 as new venues/materials are drawn, INFERRED the rest is Dawn/driver caches of that kind). The windowed game never had the problem (present paces it): soak #8's window ran 556–623 MB over six Perform songs where the headless soak #7 (engine main) ran 993–1,186 MB; with H-75 the headless soak #9 ran 561–594 MB over seven Perform songs and back to main, and the window (soak #10) 574–652 MB. Previous text: after H-24's fixes the song select samples were 563 → 626 → 625 → 734 → 708 → 710 → 705 MB, one step at song 3 of +36 MB heap in use and +32 MB of `/dev/nvidiactl` mappings; jemalloc profile: growth inside the NVIDIA driver from Dawn `DescriptorSetAllocator::AllocateDescriptorPool`, `Queue::WriteBuffer` staging and `vkAllocateCommandBuffers`. | M |

So there is nothing for the census to measure on two of the three; if they are ever reached (a character swap in-process), the HamCharacter case is the same shape as H-24 (the waypoint is in no dir, so no Phase 1 frees it) | S |

MEASURED (all 64 DC1 songs, Easy + Expert, FAST_TIME, `--no-gpu`, 6 jobs, load 35-90): split by where the player's scheduled detect frames sit against the async detector's reference (the move's first Expert occurrence, `MoveAsyncDetector`), **Expert at a first occurrence, where the scheduled frames are the reference: 0 of 1,642 not Perfect**; Expert repeats 146 of 1,823; Easy 182 of 3,465; 4 bar-line lag (H-52). It fixes `cantgetyou` Easy (14 → 1 not Perfect: the `Headwrush` Awesome at 0.8000 was 2-3 rad between authored and chord motion on the displacement nodes) but over all of DC1 it is 332 → 356 (17 runs better, 40 worse), every change in the P-11 class, and 1 new Awesome at an Expert first occurrence (`hollabackgirl` measure 6, not chased); 10-song disc control identical either way. | H-51 | ~~`RhythmDetector`'s first "new rhythm detector window w/o finalization" notify prints an uninitialised previous window~~ **DONE 2026-11-14 (`np-score2`): the original's, initialised under `HX_NATIVE`.** The garbage was not `RhythmDetector`'s: `RhythmBattlePlayer::Poll` passes `mScoringWindowStart`/`mScoringWindowEnd` to `RhythmDetector::GetRecord` before `RhythmBattle::Poll`'s first `SetWindow`, and the constructor `??0RhythmBattlePlayer@@IAA@XZ` (100.0% matched) has no store to them (0x28c/0x290): the image reads heap garbage too. `GetRecord` treats any other window as new (`ClearData`: window `[-1,-1]`, finalized), so the garbage window unfinalized the record and the first real window notified. | H-60 | ~~SIGSEGV on the song loading screen under heavy box load~~ **DONE 2026-11-14 (`np-crash`).** Found by the `np-web4` gate (2 of 2 at load ~180, `real_loading_screen` → `game_screen`, `SymbolKeys::SetFrame` → `DataArray::Node` reading 0x10 from `RhythmBattlePlayer::Poll` → `RndPropAnim::SetFrame`). Reproduced (MEASURED): a 1 s sleep per `LoadingPanel::Poll` (temporary probe) crashed 2 of 2 runs with that backtrace; throttling the engine to a 3% CPU quota from the loading screen crashed 1 of 5 and made a NaN rhythm score in 4 of 5; 0 crashes in 17 runs pinned to one or two cores, crowded with other engines, frame-capped at 10/20 fps or held to a 10% quota (their loading frames stayed ≤ ~170 ms). Cause (MEASURED with probes): with ~1 s frames one real frame spans a whole 10-sample (0.1-beat) window of `RhythmDetector`'s blended frames; when a dummy-skeleton joint axis moved by exactly 0 across two frames (probe: `joint=8 comp=2 raw0..9=0,…,0`) the window variance is 0 and `AnalyzeData`'s z-score is 0·(1/0) = NaN → `RecordData.unk10` → `mMaxRhythmInWindow` → `HamPhraseMeter::SetRatingFrac` → `SetFrame(NaN)`. `Keys::AtFrame` fails both range tests for NaN, `KeyLessEq` walks to the last key and `next` was one past the end; the Symbol read from there gave `DataGetMacro` == null. INFERRED why the joint is exactly still: P-1's 1 µm floor is a few float ulps at camera-space metres, so two equal rounded positions in a row are common once frames are long; at 30+ fps a window spans several frames and needs many in a row. Fix: `Keys::AtFrame` answers a NaN frame as before the first key (`math/Key.h`, `HX_NATIVE`; Xbox code unchanged, `SymbolKeys::SetFrame` and `Keys<Symbol,Symbol>::AtFrame` 100% before and after). The NaN itself is the image's arithmetic and is left alone. With the fix the 1 s-sleep run took 204 NaN frames into `SymbolKeys` and exited cleanly. Test: `KeysNanFrameTest` (`native/tests/test_keys_nan_frame.cpp`); with the guard disabled 2 of 3 fail (`next` past the last key; a symbol no key holds), the finite-frame control passes. | M | — |
| H-70 | **Long frames still make a NaN rhythm score** (found by `np-crash`, 2026-11-14, H-60's source). H-60's fix stops the crash, not the NaN: with ~1 s frames `RhythmDetector::AnalyzeData` returns NaN for a window whose joint axis did not move (MEASURED: 5–28 NaN analyses per 1 s-sleep loading screen, 2–11 reaching `HamPhraseMeter`). `mMaxRhythmInWindow` clears on the next poll, but a window that ENDS on NaN hands it to `mNormalizedRhythmScore` and `rating_to_score` (Dance Battle scoring; INFERRED, not driven). Two levers, neither tried: (1) P-1's 1 µm sensor floor is only a few float ulps at camera-space metres (INFERRED from the probe's exact-zero speeds), so a floor well above float resolution makes exact zeros rare, not impossible; (2) a native-only zero-variance guard in `AnalyzeData` would end it, but changes image arithmetic (P-1 chose not to). Measure a long-frame Dance Battle first. | S–M | A Dance Battle run at forced 1 s frames with no NaN reaching scoring |

| H-96 | ~~GPU time per render pass~~ **DONE 2026-11-14 (`np-gputime`, engine branch `np-gputime`, not landed).** `MILO_GPU_TIMERS=1` asks the adapter for `timestamp-query` (only if offered; device creation retries without it on failure), turns Dawn's `timestamp_quantization` toggle off and reads the device's toggles back to confirm it, and gives every one of the engine's 31 `BeginRenderPass` sites a begin/end timestamp pair named by its descriptor label (`unnamed@<file>:<line>` when there is none). Each frame's queries are resolved into a ring of 4 readback buffers mapped asynchronously; nothing waits, and a frame whose buffer is still in flight is counted `skipped`. Same-named passes in a frame add up. Output: `[GPUTIME]` lines every 1 s on stderr and `GET /api/gputime`. Off (the default), no query set is created and each site costs one bool load. MEASURED on the RTX 3090, headless, `rollerrink` ymca gameplay, box load 32–56: the frame's passes add up to 0.57–0.62 ms and span 0.69–0.75 ms of a 4.1–4.9 ms frame. `MainPassResume` is 0.33–0.37 ms (n≈6), `TexturePass` 0.06–0.07, `HudOverlayPass` 0.05–0.06, `RB3RetailBloom` 0.046 (n=9), and every other pass is under 0.03. Two A/B runs of `{rnd toggle_all_postprocs}` (`scripts/playtest/gpu_pass_ab.py`) removed `RB3RetailBloom` both times (0.0467 / 0.0456 ms → absent). An ABBA of timers off/on showed no overhead above the run-to-run noise (medians 4.14–4.87 ms, tracking box load). Tests: `GpuTimers.*` (5) | S |

(2) The web build does not time passes: it logs `GPU timers unavailable: not implemented in the web build` (emdawnwebgpu would need `timestamp-query` and an async map path tested in a browser). (3) `DC3Velocity` (≈0.008 ms) runs only where a venue's post enables velocity (seen on the tutorial screens), so it has not been A/B'd on its own | S |

| H-101 | **DONE 2026-11-15 (`np-exitrace`): `{exit}` early in boot crashed in `HamUI::Draw` (rc 139).** Gate flake in `NativeExitTest.DtaExitSkipsTheStaticDestructors` (~2 of 104 runs). An `{exit}` that landed in the ~50–150 ms after main_screen's `MainMenuPanel::Load` started a content refresh ran `GameTerminate` → `HamSongMgr::Terminate` → `ContentMgr::UnregisterCallback(this, false)`, whose `midRefreshAllowed \|\| !RefreshInProgress()` FAIL (`ContentMgr.cpp:82`, the image's own assert) `Debug::Fail` threw out of `Debug::Exit` (`Debug.cpp`, the `HX_NATIVE` mTry path). Introduced by H-28 (`405098392`, 2026-11-14), so it predates np-gfx-int25 (MEASURED: the commit before H-28, `9d4a733c1`, exits rc 0 10 of 10 with the same assert; int25 touches none of the files involved). Fix: in the native block, `Debug::Fail` does not throw once `mExiting` is set, so the FAIL is non-fatal like every other native FAIL and the exit runs to the end (no Xbox code changed). MEASURED, box load 28–33, 8 engines in parallel: `{exit}` sent the moment main_screen is entered crashed 49 of 49 before, 0 of 220 after (220 of 220 still hit the assert, so each landed in the window); `{exit}` after a forced refresh in the same eval 50 of 50 before, 0 of 220 after. Both `{exit}` tests now wait for `[HttpServer] Listening` before reading the port: `HttpServerInit` runs after the "Entering main loop" line, and the new test failed once in the first full gate after 1.61 s on that read (INFERRED from the source order and the timing; 60 of 60 parallel runs pass after).
