| H-104 | **DONE 2026-11-15 (`np-hitch`, spotperf round 3): no pipeline is compiled after the song starts.** The first-use hitches were pipeline compiles: every song frame over 33 ms (7 of 7, two runs); textures and meshes were 1–3% of the attributed time. The 160–310 ms spikes were NVIDIA driver disk-cache misses, not override constants. Fixes: (1) `DirLoader::Cleanup` (`HX_NATIVE`) → `native/src/platform/PipelineWarm.cpp` walks each loaded dir (meshes, PropAnim blend/z/alpha-write variants, fonts, reflections, ribbons, the fail material, particles, flares, screen masks, RndTexBlender base-map rects) and compiles the keys through the draws' own key functions; mesh keys async, ≤8 a frame; a draw on a key still compiling waits, never skips. (2) The rect/particle bloom-mask scale became a vertex attribute instead of a pipeline constant (it followed the camera's post-proc, so no load walk could know it). (3) A persistent pipeline cache, Dawn's blob cache under `<DC3 cache root>/dawn-pipelines`: keyed by build id, adapter and driver on top of Dawn's key; 64 MiB LRU; a damaged blob is a miss; `MILO_PIPELINE_CACHE=0` turns it off (census 461); compiled out on web; off for rb3/rb3-xenon. MEASURED, rollerrink to results: song+results pipeline time 502–1488 → **0 ms**, `pipelineCreates` flat from playing to the end in both looks and at 1920x1080. With the cache warm, enhanced Init's 36 fixed pipelines take ~30 ms instead of ~122 ms, retail boot's pipeline time 1032 → 139 ms, and load pipeline time 57 → 5 ms. 76 of 134 warmed keys are never drawn (only 4 bloom-mask twins). Web (headless Chromium, one song to results): the warm is `CreateRenderPipelineAsync` there too (a plain create stalled the browser's GPU process, 416 ms loading frames) and waits for `GpuResourcesReady()` -- before that, a particle warm ahead of the asynchronous web renderer init latched an invalid layout, 18,245 WebGPU errors and dropped frames, invisible natively; `scripts/web/smoke-test.mjs` now fails on any uncaptured WebGPU error. After: 0 errors, loading max rAF interval 66.7 ms (base 66.7), song creates 27 → 3, results 2 → 0. 0 differing pixels at 8 points (first venue and results frames included), both looks. Guards: `PerformCrowdTest` (default gate) asserts no create at a draw (`pipelineCreates − pipelineLoadCreates`) from the engine's `Screen 'game_screen' Enter` line to the end -- it first counted from the driver's polled frame, up to ~100 frames late, and a TexBlender rect create escaped it 4 runs in 5 and failed the 5th; sabotage (that warm removed; one drawn key refused) red at Enter + 10 frames; the full-song check inside `GameplayTelemetryTest.WorldPipelineConvergesDuringGameplay` (`DC3_GAMEPLAY_TESTS`); `PipelineWarmTest`; `PipelineWarmLifetimeTest` (who deletes an in-flight warm: callback first, removal first, Terminate, device loss; three sabotages red); `PipelineDiskCacheTest`. Left: textures at load (15–26 ms a song here, decide on the GPD number); ~10 unattributed 20–34 ms song frames, not profiled. Engine doc §39 | S |

| H-106 | **DONE 2026-11-15 (`np-framecost4`, spotperf round 4): the scene bind group is reused, and the slow song frames are attributed.** (1) `WgpuRnd::WriteSceneUniforms` runs at BeginDrawing and on every camera/environ change (one camera per UI panel, ~52 writes a frame); each write lands at a new scene-ring offset and the group binds that offset, so every write made a new bind group: MEASURED 52–94 creates a frame in menus, song select, song and results, both looks, 0.04–0.09 ms a frame on desktop (1,022,671 creates and 1165 ms a song, worst frame 8.13 ms) and on the web 0.22–0.33 ms a frame (2112 ms a song), each a call into the browser. The only frames that recur at the same song time are the end of the song (151.6 s, ~10 frames of 12–23 ms) and the run-up to results; per-part timers show `SystemPoll` taking 10–14 ms of each, i.e. `LoadMgr::Poll`'s 10 ms slice (`mPeriod`, the image's default) loading the endgame/results dirs. (3) Not done: the one late rect warm (1 pipeline in the alley, synchronous `DrawRect2D` cache) is not worth an async 2D path; the 76 warmed-but-undrawn keys cost at most ~0.17 s of async load and ~14 MB of the 64 MiB disk cache (ESTIMATE). | H-107 | **DONE 2026-11-15 (`np-notices`; ENGINE CHANGE on engine branch `np-notices` `0f85cfd`, not yet landed): an open that finds no file is silent, as in the image.** The image's `AsyncFileWin::_OpenAsync` skips `ReadError` for ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND/PATH_NOT_FOUND/NOT_READY (2/3/0x15), because callers probe optional files through it (`FileExists`, `DataReadFile(f, false)`, `Locale::Init`'s `devkit:\\locale\\<lang>\\locale_keep.dta`, `HamPlayerData`'s `devkit:\\dancers.dta`, `Watcher::LoadWatches`' `watches.dta`). The engine's `AsyncFileNative` logged every failed `fopen`; on web `File_Web.cpp` calls every file local, so every open reaches it, and each boot put 5 `AsyncFile: failed to open` console **errors** up (`devkit:/locale/eng/locale_keep.dta`, `devkit:/dancers.dta` x2, `/data/watches.dta`, `/data/ui/image/dc_logo_keep.png`; `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item10/web/after/console.jsonl.gz`). Desktop never showed them: there those paths are not local and go to the archive (`File_Native.cpp FileIsLocal`). Fix (engine `src/platform/AsyncFile_Native.cpp`): `errno` is taken right after the first `fopen`; ENOENT/ENOTDIR are silent, any other failure still logs and now names the error. A web 404 leaves the MEMFS open's ENOENT standing, so it is a not-found too. Test: `AsyncFileNotFoundTest` (`native/tests/test_asyncfile_not_found.cpp`): against engine `34dd1a9` `MissingFileProbeIsSilent` and `MissingDirectoryProbeIsSilent` fail; with `0f85cfd` all 5 pass. The control `UnreadableFileIsStillLogged` (a mode-000 file) passes on both. | H-109 | **DONE 2026-11-15 (`np-shadow`, item 11): the dancers' floor shadows match Xenia's area and depth, and throneroom has them.** Three causes, all in the engine's projected-shadow path (DC3 only, behind the rndshape seam): (1) *casters*: every showing mesh of a shadow object was drawn (both LODs, the `*_lod` shadow meshes on top of the body, hidden props). Now native follows `Character::DrawLod`'s occlusion rule: only with `floor_shadow`, the `shadow` group if there is one, else the draw list plus the current LOD's opaque group; RndDir/RndGroup draw their draw lists. (2) *caster skinning*: the caster shader did not normalize bone weights as the main pass does; the `*_lod` meshes, drawn by no other pass, became wedges toward the world origin (throneroom: no visible shadow, only a wash). (3) *depth*: the projected light's factor scaled the box light only; standard.ps `0000110010030011` scales points and box together, and throneroom's floor is point-lit (shadow at a third of its depth). MEASURED vs Xenia, same shots and protocol (mask px native/Xenia; ratio in/out of the mask, Xenia/native): rollerrink far 1.25 → **1.01**, near 1.46 → **1.04**, cu02 1.36 → **1.02** (ratio 0.772/0.741); streetside boombox 0.88 → **0.95** (0.591/0.587); throneroom far 0.00 → **0.88** (0.749/0.757), boombox **0.84** (0.825/0.835); also houseparty 1.01/0.78, glitterati 0.96/0.88, dci no shadow on either side. Pixel A/B in one paused session (a test-only flag switching the three changes off): 0 stable pixels changed outside the shadow region, and 0 with the casters cleared (the remainder is the HUD move card, which animates under the pause). Not shipped: a map sized to the output (tile 256–2048 at 1080p and 4K) gives the same area and depth, and its edge is 0.8 px narrower at most, which cannot be seen. The blur is fixed in light space, as retail's is. Tests: `RetailLightingTest.CharacterCastsWhatDrawLodDraws`, `ProjShadowCasterPassTest.SkinnedCasterWeightsAreNormalized` / `.SkinnedCasterWithNoWeightsFollowsItsFirstBone`, `RetailCharShadingTest.ProjectedShadowScalesTheWholeDiffuseLight` (5 sabotages red). rb3-xenon `rb3-render`: 11 of 11 frames identical. **Left:** dclive reads 1.69/1.33 of Xenia's area, with Xenia's arm shadows cut at the gobo pool edge (a lead; the earlier depth check there matched); rollerrink's boombox is still 1.32 (the additive gobo form); the draw's camera-frustum cull of casters is not reproduced. Engine doc `dc3-retail-lighting.md` §14; lane notes `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item11/` | S |
| H-110 | **DONE 2026-11-15 (`np-framecost5`, spotperf round 5): the loader's slice follows the frame period while gameplay is on screen; per-frame getenv reads with no writer are read once.** (1) The rule: while `TheUI`'s bottom screen is `game_screen` and the pacer has a target, `LoadMgr`'s per-poll slice is the image's value × target period / 16.7 ms (10 → 5 ms at 120 fps, 10 at 60 and on the 60 Hz web); everywhere else, or with no target, it is the image's value unchanged. Set through the image's own setter before each frame's poll (`native/src/platform/LoaderBudget_Native.cpp`, `HX_NATIVE`); nothing the Xbox build compiles changed. "Gameplay on screen" is the image's own test (`MetaPanel::Poll`): the win, results and pause screens are pushed over or reached from `game_screen` and count; practice's game_screen counts, its tutorial/welcome screens do not; pause (pushed over game_screen) keeps the scaled slice; boot, menus and loading screens never do. With no pacer target (`DC3_FPS_CAP=0`, headless uncapped: PerformCrowdTest, the headless boots) it is the image's value on every screen; the DTA flow tests are paced at 240. NOT SHIPPED, measured: scaling everywhere (as first decided) made boot to title +0.135 s (6 of 6 pairs); a floor plus the frame's spare time depended on box load (boot +0.128 s, 5 of 6, at load 40–60). MEASURED, 6 alternating full-song pairs, same tree, paired new − base: boot to title −0.023 s (2 of 6 slower), title to main −0.002 s (3 of 6), song select → game −0.007 s (3 of 6); **game → results +0.070 s median (+0.03 to +0.09), slower in 6 of 6 -- a stated trade, not a pass: term 1 waived for game → results only by the coordinator (about 12 frames at 120 on a transition over a second, against visible hitches with dancers on screen)**: the final results screen waits for its load, which at 5 ms a frame takes ~12 more frames (160–162 → 171–175). In exchange, per run, song end frames ≥12 ms 8–18 → 2–7, ≥16.7 ms 2–5 → 1–2, game CPU ≥9 ms 8–10 → 2–5; run-up to results ≥12 ms 16–26 → 6–20, ≥16.7 ms 2–3 → 0–1, game CPU ≥9 ms 15–18 → 0–3 (max 12.5–19.7 → 8.0–12.3 ms). Scaling only while `game_screen` is also the top screen would keep results load time and leave the run-up at 12–23 ms. Flow tests (paced at 240, 2.5 ms in gameplay; every screen they count comes before gameplay, where the rule changes nothing): ymca `game_screen` 3173/3176 → 3171/3172; omg 1910/1904/1904 → 1901/1967/1922, the one late title (frame 70 against 46–48) did not repeat on the rerun (48). Tests: `LoaderBudget.*`, `HeadlessBootTest.LoaderBudgetIsTheImagesOutsideGameplay`, `LoaderBudgetGameplayTest` (default gate; the running engine's LoadMgr: 10 ms in the menu, 5/10/10 ms in gameplay at 120/60/unpaced, 10 ms after leaving; unpaced, every logged budget is the image's); sabotages red, including two unpaced leaks (no target scaled as 120; the setter storing 120 for none). (2) getenv: 931.9 calls a frame in a song → 2.4 (menus 28.5 → 2.0, song select 192.8 → 3.2, results 555.7 → 2.4); getenv's share of main-thread samples 0.62%/0.62% → 0.03%/0.03% (two 30 s song windows a side). Cached after checking for writers (the only `setenv` is `Dc3ProfileApply` at startup): `DC3_IK_DIAG` ×8 sites (473 + 357 a frame in `CharBones::ScaleAdd` and `PoseMeshes`), `DC3_IK_DIAG2`, `DC3_IK_FOOTPLANT`, `DC3_IK_NEUTRAL`, `DC3_TEXT_DIAG`, `DC3_CAM_DIAG2`/`3`, `DC3_HUD_ONLY`/`DC3_NO_UI`, the `DC3_NATIVE_SCORING`/`DC3_POSE_SELFTEST` flags, engine `MILO_DEBUG_PIPELINES`. Left: `DC3_POSE_REACQUIRE_MS`/`DC3_POSE_HOLD_MS` (tests set them in-process), reads under one a frame. Census unchanged (461); the 12 touched Xbox objects byte-identical. Engine doc §41 | S |
| H-111 | **DONE 2026-11-15 (`np-shadow2`, item 12): dclive's gobo-form shadows sit where Xenia draws them, the casters carry the image's half-texel raster offset, and nested casters out of the camera's view are culled as `RndDrawable::Draw` does.** All in the engine's projected-shadow path, DC3 only, behind the rndshape seam. (1) *dclive*: its `a2_spotlight.lit` is scaled (1, 0.86, 0.86). The image draws casters with `NgLight::SetShadowTransforms` (true inverse of the light's world transform, cone projection) and reads with `RndLight::Projection()` (raw rows); the two agree only for an orthonormal light. Native drew casters with the receivers' transform, so its shadow sat ~25 px lower and larger than Xenia's. Ruled out by measurement first (could have gone the other way): shadow where the gobo is ~0, 0 px on both sides; S/G p50 0.78 vs 0.76 and never > 1.2; the gobo pool 176592 vs 176712 px with the same bbox. Mask px native/Xenia: far 1.69 → **0.95–0.98**, near 1.33 → **0.97**. (2) *half texel*: `DxRnd` leaves `D3DRS_HALFPIXELOFFSET` at 0 except in `DrawRect`/`DrawRectDepth`/a point test, so the image's 256 map sits +0.5 texel (+u, +v) from the geometry; native shifts the caster clip by that. Boombox-only (dancers hidden, exactly reproducible): streetside 0.951 → **0.98** (centroid 12 px → 1 px off Xenia's), rollerrink 1.318 → **1.12**, throneroom 0.84 → 0.66 (a 438 px grazing tip on the carpet edge, already short before; cause unknown). In one paused session the offset changed 0 px outside the shadow region. Dancer shots (±5% run to run, the pause lands on one of three frames): rollerrink far/near/cu02 0.98–1.04 / 0.99–1.00 / 1.02–1.03, streetside 0.92–0.99, throneroom 0.85–0.88. A retail-exact 5x5 receiver kernel or wider taps do not move rollerrink's boombox (1.14 / 1.20), nor does map size; not shipped. (3) *cull*: nested caster draws are skipped when their world sphere is outside the camera's frustum (shadow objects themselves are not). Over an omg session it saves 0.0–0.3 caster draws a frame on average and 23 of 33–37 in the frames that cull (the opening venue shot, the win close-up); gameplay shots cull nothing. Toggled in place at 27 shots, the four that cull change no pixel beyond the HUD's noise: the dropped dancers' shadows are off-screen too, so no Xenia frame shows a difference. Tests: `RetailLightingTest.ScaledLightDrawsItsCastersThroughTheInverse`, `.ProjectedShadowLightDrawsItsCastersWhereTheReceiversLook` (now with the half texel), `.NestedCastersOutOfTheCamerasViewAreCulled` (4 sabotages red). **Left:** rollerrink's boombox 1.12 (a ~1 px fringe on both edges of a 20 px band; coverage and gobo match); throneroom's boombox tip. Engine doc `dc3-retail-lighting.md` §15; lane notes `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item12/` | S |

| H-114 | **DONE 2026-11-15 (`np-look13`, item 13 (c)): the retail path draws the normal detail map (shader bit 24); the houseparty floor's broad sheen is Xenia's, not retail's, and is left as it is.** (1) *Detail map*: `CalcShaderOpts` sets bit 24 for a per-pixel material with a `norm_detail_map` and strength > 0; `standard.ps` then fetches it at uv × tiling and adds s·(dx, dy, sat(1 − dx² − dy²)) to the normal map's (x, y, z) before de_normal and the frame (0000030001030035 vs 0000030000030035; all 191 shipped bit-24 permutations carry the normal map). Native's retail path ignored it; now added under `retailLighting > 1.5` and the material fields (rb3/rb3-xenon untouched; their modern path already had `blendDetailNormal`). Venues (strength A/B in one paused session, moved px fix vs base control): houseparty 98,515 vs 928 (`HSP_LivingRoomFloor` 0.15); rollerrink 325,443 vs 980 (`StageCarpet` 2.0); streetside 328,518 vs 1,314 (`Sidewalk` 10, `Street` 5, two fire escapes 1, brick 0.5); throneroom 407,198 vs 1,067 (ten floor/column/wall/statue materials, 0.1–4); dci 60,326 vs 875 (`Ceiling` 1.5); dclive and glitterati carry none and read like the control. Characters: 1 of 1,956 skinned shipped permutations has bit 24, with no rim bits (a scene object). **Containment:** with post off, the bit-24 meshes hidden as the footprint, 0 px outside the footprint and the HUD column move on any venue (post on: ≤ 1 level, the bloom carrying the change). **Against Xenia** (high-frequency energy r1 on the changed surfaces, far02): streetside asphalt 0.85 → **4.19** (Xenia 4.34; p95 58.3 → 66.6, Xenia 66.9), rollerrink carpet 4.54 → **6.66** (6.09), throneroom marble 0.91 → **1.92** (1.72), houseparty wood 2.70 → 3.87 (5.09; the rest is the sheen). Test: `RetailCharShadingTest` kind 4 (plain + detail, 12 cases from the microcode model); 4 sabotages red. (2) *Sheen*: native's floor spec is the microcode's power-18 Phong with matching constants (spec-map alpha decoded from the milo: 0 DXT1 punch-through texels). Xenia's sheen p95 65.0 falls to 33.3 at power 200 (native 32.2 at 18). Retail stills 12/14/02 show no broad sheen; wood p95 / rug p50: Xenia 1.72, native 1.07, retail 12 1.26. Rule added to engine doc §37.10's Xenia artifacts. **Left, untried:** retail multiplies the spec sum by the projected-light multiplier (native does not; it only darkens); retail normalises V per vertex. Item 13 (a)'s rollerrink haze residual is not this (p5/p95 stays 0.128). Engine doc `dc3-retail-lighting.md` §16; lane notes `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item13/c/README.md` | S |
| H-115 | **DONE 2026-11-15 (`np-eq`): the boombox EQ bars move on native, fed from the master-bus meter of the mix actually playing.** Driver: the bars are `multiplier_meter`'s PropAnim on `audio_channels` channel3/4, which only `HamMaster::CheckLevels` writes, from the RMS of `Synth::mLevelData`'s DML/DMR buses (Synth360's `MeterEffect` XAPO on the mastering voice). Native's synth had no meter, so `mLevelData` was empty, `CheckLevels` returned at once and the bars read 0.00 against Xenia's 0.50–0.66 (omg 15.7–21.3 s, both venues). Survey claim (2) verified: the only `MeterEffectMonitor` is `narrator_monitor.effmon`, a separate path that reads 0 on Xenia in this window too. Fix (engine, DC3-only in effect): `audio/MasterMeter.h` taps `AudioDevice::MixSources` after the limiter (desktop audio thread and web `PumpAudio`; web drains only what the worklet has played, `MixFramesConsumed`) in 256-frame quanta, and `MeterEffectState` replays `MeterEffect::DoProcess`'s bookkeeping on the main thread, including its block channel indexing, per-poll reset and lagged RMS divisor. `NativeSynth` creates the image's nine buses and derives DML/DMR as `Synth360::Poll` does. **Pan law (audible):** native panned with `vol*(1∓pan)`, so a hard-panned mono stem played +6 dB. `platform/ImagePan_Native.h` now does `Voice::UpdateMix`'s 5.1 ring, or the cos/sin pair for a stereo voice, folded with the game's own downmix, for `StreamReceiverNative` and `SampleInstNative` (both DC3-only). omg's hard-panned stems are 6 dB quieter, centred stems and stereo samples 3 dB quieter. Levels (mean ch3/ch4, 15.7–21.3 s), Xenia / old law / now: rollerrink 0.567 / 0.705 / **0.578**, dci 0.564 / 0.715 / **0.634**. The retail still shows low bars, as Xenia and now native do. rb3/rb3-xenon never enable the tap (one atomic load per mix callback); their ctest and frame comparison were run on engine `ff5bada`. Cost: 1.8 µs per 512-frame audio period, 0.07 µs a frame main-thread drain, full-song median frame unchanged. Web: the bars show and move (48 frames, rollerrink). No dc3 `src/` change. getenv census unchanged (461). Tests: `MasterMeterTest.*` (5), `StreamReceiverNative.PanFollowsTheImagesRing`, `ImagePanLaw.StereoVoiceIsTheCosSinPair`; 10 sabotages red. Gate 1231/1231, 70 skipped (budget 70). `NativeAudioSeekTest.ReceiverPlaysEveryByteTheImageCycleAdmits` assumed unity gain at pan 0 and now reads back hard left. **Left:** the narrator meter (`FxSendMeterEffect` is never fed); native never runs FxSend chains; the meter sees a stereo fold, not true 5.1. Lane notes `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/eq/` | S |
| H-116 | **MEASURED, NOT SHIPPED 2026-11-16 (`np-texwarm`): creating the game's textures at load instead of at their first draw is not a win.** What a first draw costs (base heads, 120 fps cap): desktop, 16–35 ms of creates in the `game_screen` Enter frame and the frame after it (89–126 ms together), 4.8–5.3 ms in the `perform_endgame_screen` Enter frame, and at most 1.5 ms in any song frame; web, 25.9–34.3 ms in the Enter frame (140–177 ms) and at most 0.4 ms in a song frame. Tried: an engine queue (`QueueGpuTexWarm` / `PumpGpuTexWarms`, 2 ms a frame) fed from `GamePanel::Load` with the game and win screens' panels and the preloaded fonts, with a 5 s hold that never fired. Desktop, 6 pairs: song select → game load +124 ms median (slower in 4 of 6), the first two game frames −42 ms (faster in 6 of 6), total +81 ms; bitmap-texture peak 48.2 → 60.9 MB (rollerrink) and 58.6 → 71.1 MB (streetside). Web, 2 pairs: Enter frame −63 and −8 ms; peak 43.5 → 59.7 MB. mobile-gpu: live textures 256 → 340 MB, and one warm run failed the check's song-started assertion (a 40 ms race the warm provoked). The web Enter frame's texture part (26–34 ms) caps any warm at about the 30 ms bar, and no song frame has a texture cost, so the cheaper drawn-only variant was not built. By the song's end 271–273 of the warm's creates had not been drawn: unused flashcards, `move_data`, HUD and challenge panels. Reverted (dc3 `af366c459`, engine `d2a3f1e`); `src/` is unchanged from np-gfx. **Kept:** `MILO_FRAME_TIMES=-` (`[FT] `-prefixed header and rows on stdout, i.e. the web console; `WriteFrameTimesHeader`) and `[GPUCOUNT] texBytes` / `texBytesPeak`. Tests `TexBytesTest.*` (2) and `FrameTimesTest.*` (2), 8 sabotages red. **Remaining in-song frames ≥12 ms, recurring in all 3 base runs** (desktop, 120 cap; a 12–15 ms reading is usually a 6–9 ms draw after a 3–6 ms pacer sleep taken before the draw): rollerrink, 35 frames: 1.9 s, 18–21 ms, draw CPU 11–14 ms; 3.8–4.0 s, draw CPU; 11.75 s, game CPU; 75–79 s and 84.6 s, 12–20 ms, draw CPU 6–12.8 ms (a camera section); 149.9–151.8 s, up to 21 ms, game CPU 9–13 ms (the endgame dir load); 155.4 s, the endgame Enter (tex 5.3 ms); 159.7–162.4 s, results-load game CPU and file reads. streetside, 22 frames: 11.66 s, pacing; 46.9–47.65 s and 83.6–83.75 s, draw CPU 7.5–8.5 ms; 105.4 s and 111.9 s, pacing; 149.7–151.77 s, up to 22.7 ms, game CPU; 155.3 s, the endgame Enter (tex 4.8 ms); 159.6–162.2 s, results load. Over 16.7 ms: rollerrink 1.9 s, 78.33 s and 151.73 s; streetside 151.61 s. Engine doc §42; lane notes `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/spotperf/round6/` | S |
| H-117 | **DONE 2026-11-16 (`np-texblend`, item 15): dancers' blended cloth normal maps are whole; the washed clothes (cyan/pink tops, gold leggings, pale shirts) are gone.** Cause, from GPU readbacks of the 512² `RndTexBlender` output on houseparty lilt01/bodie01 (each mode in one paused session): (1) the base `DrawRect(0,0,W,H)` was mapped against the 1280×720 virtual screen, not the bound target, so the base filled only the top-left 40 %×71 % and the rest stayed transparent black (coverage 28.5 %); the image's `DxRnd::DrawRect` turns the viewport transform off (`ViewportEnable 0`) and draws in the target's pixels; (2) no controller layer ever landed, because native `RndMesh::DrawFacesInRange` draws nothing. Not the clear, the blend state, the write mask or frame order: the output was composed every frame while a controller was active, as the image does. Fix: `RndTexBlender::NativeCompose` (`HX_NATIVE`) hands the image's two steps to the engine composer (`TexBlendHook`/`TexBlendPass`, rb3's W16-SA path): base over the whole target, then each controller's faces at their UVs with the near/far/custom map at the controller's alpha; the decomp bookkeeping (`mRenderedStates`, `unkc0`) is unchanged and DrawShowing still matches (99.68437, as on main). `TexBlendPass` now keeps its parameters in a ring at a dynamic offset with `BindGroupCache` bind groups (no per-frame create) and is warmed at load (`WgpuRnd::WarmTexBlendPipelines` from `PipelineWarm.cpp`). The general rect fix: `rndshape::kRectTargetPixels` (DC3 true; rb3/rb3-xenon false) maps `DrawRect2D` into a bound texture target's own pixels. Other render-to-texture users that draw rects: TexProc and SoftParticleBuffer would have had the same defect but draw nothing at runtime (rect census over 4 venues + menus: TexBlender was the only runtime rect-into-target user); StreamRenderer (CPU compose), VelocityBuffer, the shadow map, crowd impostors (mesh draws) and the spotlight blur (`#ifndef HX_NATIVE`, SpotBeamPass) do not use the rect path. On screen vs Xenia: rollerrink aubrey04/angel04 tops print white (retail RMnrQemwIa8 agrees), houseparty Lilt's leggings dark and Bodie's sleeves red, dclive Kerith's top not cyan in the awesome state; dci taye04/emilia04, throneroom oblio04/tan04 and glitterati robots carry no TexBlender and do not move; streetside mo/glitch unchanged. Survey no. 5 (throneroom Tan's cheek rim) is not this cause: tan04 has no TexBlender. In-run A/B (post off, composer vs the decomp body with the rect fix, i.e. layers off): 552–6091 px per shot, all on the blended cloth; fix-vs-fix control 0 px in 10 shots; 56 px at Δ1 on a rollerrink backup dancer in one shot. Cost (houseparty, 2 dancers): `TexBlendPass` 0.0136 ms GPU a frame (2 passes, ≤ 2 draws each), 0 pipeline and 0 per-frame bind-group creates in the song; median frame 1.396 vs 1.431 ms before. Enhanced: no larger target — the image's DrawShowing asserts the output "must be no larger than 512 x 512", and the blenders' base maps are 512² with 128² near/far maps (houseparty, both dancers), so a bigger target would only resample the same texels; 4K closeups (DC3_LOOK=enhanced, MILO_WIDTH 3840) show no blockiness or banding on the blended cloth. rb3/rb3-xenon: frame identity (rb3-render 11/11 identical, rb3 ctest 158/158 incl. `TexBlendPassTest`). Tests `TexBlenderComposeTest.*` (4: base covers the target, layer at its UVs, steady frame creates nothing, rect into a target in its pixels), each red under its sabotage. Fix run against base: `CompleteSong` 8.0 → 3.0 ms (its frame's game CPU 9.9 → 5.8 ms), loader 58.0 → 24.5 ms over 12 → 7 frames, warm walk 43.5 → 14.5 ms, frames over 16.7 ms in the window 1–3 a run (8 base runs) → 0 (both venues), longest 17.5–20.3 → 14.6 / 16.7 ms. Re-taken on int36 (with np-texblend; base and fix alternating, 2 pairs a venue): frames over 16.7 ms in the window 4/1/1/1 → 0/0/0/0, longest 18.1–21.6 → 13.5–16.2 ms; perf, streetside: `CompleteSong` 8.0 → 3.5 ms (`GetOutfitRemap` 7.0 → 0), its frame's game CPU 11.7 → 7.0 ms, loader 70.5 → 31.5 ms over 17 → 9 frames, warm walk 54.5 → 15.5 ms; keys queued the same dir by dir in all 8 runs. Left: a mogg stream starting (`StandardStream::Play` + the buffering decode, at least 8 ms a poll in the image, on the main thread natively), 4–6.5 ms in two song-end frames; they are 14–15 ms at the 120 cap. Moving it means a later start (a 2 ms cap: 1–2 frames) or a decode thread; neither built. **Draw-CPU spikes** (round 6's rollerrink 1.9 / 3.8 / 75–79 / 84.6 s, streetside 46.9 / 83.6 s): they do not recur (frames with draw CPU ≥6 ms: 21–263 between runs of one song). They are the same work done slower: 7% more bind-group lookups, 3.5–4x the time a lookup, and in an IPC run +5% instructions, 4x user cycles, IPC 1.18 → 0.32 at the same 5.2 GHz. They follow the box: with 0–4 busy CPUs, 0.0% of frames reach 6 ms (2 runs, ~7,900 frames each); with 12–16, 18.8%; with 24–33, 60%; over all 10 runs with the box sampled (int36 too), none of ~52,000 frames at 0–4 busy CPUs reached 6 ms. Nothing to fix. Tests `OutfitRemapCostTest.*` (3), `PipelineWarmClassesTest.*` (1), `FrameTimesTest.EndSIsTheSteadyClockAtEachRowsEnd`; 7 sabotages red. | H-119 | **DONE 2026-11-16 (`np-look16`, item 16 (a)): dancers self-shadow (standard.ps option bit 19), as on the image.** Survey no. 5 (throneroom Tan's cheek rim) was an unpaired pose: on paired frozen frames the faces are within about 1-3 %. *Census* of the bits `CalcShaderOpts` sets on the 1,413 shipped character materials (95 milos): every one native implements except bit 19 (915 per-pixel materials eligible) and bit 48 point-cube (0 effect: no light in the 7 venues has a cube texture); engine doc §11.2's "19 yes (ShadowPass)" was wrong and is corrected. *Bit 19*: `PrepShadow` already ran natively (about 1.8 texture passes a frame into a 512² 0x42 target), but nothing sampled the map. Now the target's depth is sampleable; while `GetShadowCam()` is set, the scene block carries the shadow camera's clip matrix and c107/c108 and binds that depth; `retailPixelShade` takes the image's four point taps with bilinear weights and scales every point-light term by 1 − 0.75·c107·sat(N·−c108)·(1 − s) (skin: Nk diffuse, Nf back light and spec). The casters (`kDrawExtrude`) cull backwards whatever the material asks, as `CheckForceCull` mode 6 does. *Against Xenia*, paired (same pause time and forced shot both sides, the shot re-forced after the toggle; throneroom, houseparty, dci; far02, near01, cu01, cu02): the on−off mask's area is native/Xenia 0.96–1.22 on 24 frames, on the same shoulders, chest bands and underarms; its darkest 5 % agree within 0.01–0.04, and native's median is 0.02–0.09 shallower because a smaller render averages the map's speckle away (throneroom far02 median 0.608 at 720p, 0.548 at 1080p, 0.518 at 4K, Xenia 0.554). The first reading (Xenia 17,314 px vs native 5,376, closeups 0 px) was unpaired frames, and closeups read 0 because the toggle took effect only at the next shot start. *Who self-shadows* is the image's per-shot rule, followed rather than matched by accident: HamCamShot target `self_shadow` (`mSelfShadow`, applied in `StartAnim` → `SetSelfShadow`), logged identical on both sides in all three venues (far02/near01: the two players on, backups off; cu01/cu02: all four on). Each character casts only onto itself (its shadow group, else its opaque meshes); the WorldCrowd 3D crowd clears self/floor shadow around its draws in game, as on the image. No acne or stair-stepping at 1080p or 4K (isolated darkened pixels 1.6–6.7 % native vs 5.8–11.8 % Xenia), so no enhanced filter. *Warm*: the caster pass's backwards-culled keys are queued at load (`PipelineWarm.cpp`, a context, no new class bit); without it `PerformCrowdTest`'s no-pipeline-after-`game_screen` guard is red (iceicebaby 53 creates vs 52 loads, ymca 54 vs 52, up to 13.2 ms). *Cost* (RTX 3090, `MILO_GPU_TIMERS`, frozen throneroom frame): the sampling adds 0.006 ms (far) / 0.013 ms (closeup) a frame at 1080p and 0.024 / 0.029 ms at `MILO_RENDER_SCALE=2`, inside run-to-run spread; the caster passes it reads were already rendered (0.1–0.3 ms with their main-pass splits). *Web*: the same path, no platform branch; `mobile-gpu.mjs --profile mobile` 10 of 10 checks, textures 256.1 MB both, heap 395.0 vs 390.8 MB, 58.6 vs 59.8 frames/s (run-to-run spread), no validation error. rb3/rb3-xenon: `kSelfShadowMap` false and `retailLighting` 1, gate off. Tests: `RetailCharShadingTest` kinds 5-9 (60 cases from the microcode emulation of the five bit-19 permutations), `SelfShadowNeedsItsCamera`, `SelfShadowCasterTest.*` (2), the caster warm under `PerformCrowdTest`'s guard; 12 sabotages red. | H-120 | **MEASURED, NO CHANGE 2026-11-16 (`np-look16`, item 16 (b)-(d)): floor reflections, venue screens and the dci/houseparty glow match the image.** *(b) Floor reflections* (post off, paired frozen frames at 30 s and the 66 s peak, near01/far02/cu02, each with every `WorldReflection` and `*reflect*`/`*refract*`/`*mirror*` mesh hidden as the control on both sides): the reflection's positive part is native/Xenia 92–105 % in dci, houseparty, rollerrink, throneroom and dclive (dci spatial correlation 0.79–0.98); the dancers' legs, the boombox and the stools reflect in the same places and clip at the same floor edge; on both sides the reflection lies under the floor's own shading, shadow and gobo. glitterati has none on either side; dclive's only difference is its LED stripes at different animation phases. *(c) Screens and emissives* (paired, post off and on, retail and enhanced look, 5 s after each forced shot so Xenia's target-cache notify band is gone): dci screens, the rollerrink sign and dclive's LED grid and logo wall show the same content and bloom within about ±10 %; dclive's logo wall is 6–10 % darker pre-post on native and matches post on. glitterati holds no screen, TV or movie object; houseparty's TV and fridge are in no gameplay shot (whole frames within 0.5 post off). *(d) Glow* (survey nos. 2, 4): not the enhanced look — retail and enhanced give the same bloom within 1 %. dci tubes +4 % (far02) / +7 % (near01) over Xenia, inside the ±5 % venue spread (whole-frame bloom N/X 0.97–1.04 in dci, houseparty, dclive, glitterati); the chandelier on banner-free frames is 46.7 Xenia / 46.8 retail / 47.2 enhanced (far02), so the survey's larger glow was Xenia's notify band on unpaired frames. | H-121 | **DONE 2026-11-19 (`np-look17`, item 17 (b)): the retail look's 8-bit bloom sets round in the shader, so every GPU gives the same bloom; the two DC3LookPost bounds that failed on the GPD's Radeon measure the look, not the GPU.** *Why the GPUs differed*: a float written to an RGBA8Unorm target is rounded by the implementation (Vulkan allows either neighbour; nearest is only a "should"). With the passes' own rounding off, the RTX 3090 (NVIDIA 615.78) put 103 of 184 uniform fields 0.505-0.545 of a level past a level a level low (which ones depends on the level); the Radeon 890M (RADV) and Apple M3 round at 0.5: the 3090 made to round half-up in the shader reproduced the M3's numbers (plain-arm energy 468147 vs 468139, wobble 0.0228 vs 0.0228) and RADV's dead-zone term (0.225) to within 1.5%. Compounded over retail's 8-bit passes this moved its bloom term about 3% and its frame-edge glow 3 levels between the 3090 and the other two. *Fix (engine)*: every pass writing an 8-bit set (`fs_bloom_down`, `fs_down4`, `fs_blur`, `fs_bloom_taps`, `fs_streak`, `fs_glare`, enhanced family [0]) writes a whole level, rounded half to even in the shader (WGSL override `kQuant8`, set only when the target is RGBA8Unorm; RB3's RGBA16Float sets never set it). On real frozen frames on the 3090 (dci, houseparty; far02, near01) the retail look's mean rises 0.25-0.66 levels against the enhanced look in the same frame (retail - enhanced -0.11..-0.44 -> +0.07..+0.23); enhanced is untouched (16-bit sets). Left to the GPU (one level, not compounded): the composite's frame write, the velocity target, the motion-blur feedback. *The two bounds*: `EnhancedBloomKeepsEnergyAndExtentAndStopsWobbling`'s wobble measured the 8-bit rounding contour of sub-level tails (0.010 px 3090, 0.025 RADV); it is now the centroid under a cos² window (120-240 px) on a merged exposure with the tails at 1/16 level: shipped 0.0047 px, retail 0.317, flattened-tent sabotage 0.046, under either rounding; bound `< 0.1 x retail`. (A bilinear-combine sabotage is no longer caught: bilinear moves a centroid linearly; the old bound caught it only through rounding.) `SixteenBitSetsKeepRetailsDeadZone`'s dots-at-64 ratio was a frame-edge band (dots on the border rows, clamp-to-edge, 3 levels on the 3090 / 6 at 0.5 rounding, to row 47-85); the mean is now taken 100 px inside the edges (dots-64 0.000 both, dots-255 9.047 / 8.765) and the tolerance has a 0.05-level floor. Tests: `DC3LookPostTest.RetailSetsRoundEveryPassToTheNearestLevel` (381 / 348 uniform fields per arm, plain / streak, retail and enhFilter), the two reworked bounds; 6 sabotages red (RB3 set pipes, DC3 retail pipes, family [0] unrounded, each in its own arm; all three together; flattened tent; dead zone off). GPD check: branches `np-look17` (dc3, engine), `--gtest_filter=DC3LookPostTest.EnhancedBloomKeepsEnergyAndExtentAndStopsWobbling:DC3LookPostTest.SixteenBitSetsKeepRetailsDeadZone:DC3LookPostTest.RetailSetsRoundEveryPassToTheNearestLevel`. Engine doc `dc3-backend-for-rb3-wii.md` §45; lane notes `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item17/b/` | S |
| H-122 | **DONE 2026-11-19 (`np-look17`, item 17 (a)): headless retail at a size other than 1280x720 draws every viewport in the virtual resolution; the "retail moves while paused" pairs were this, not motion.** *Symptom* (9c on the GPD, reproduced here): paused, a forced cu shot, a frame, `self_shadow FALSE` on every Character, a second frame; at 1268x713 enhanced differs by the self-shadow (~8k px over 2 levels), retail by 425k-660k px with every edge displaced. Nothing moved: 25 frames 0.25 s apart after the forced shot (~2,000 engine frames) were identical (0-4 px). The displacement was a 1280/1268 scale about the top-left corner, and the self-shadow-ON frame was the wrong one. *Cause*: `RndCam::Select` builds its viewport from `TheRnd.Width/Height` (1280x720); `WgpuRnd::FrameViewport` maps it by drawn/logical; the retail look does not follow its target, so the device got no logical size and fell back to the output (1268x713): a Select made mid-pass went on unscaled. PrepShadow reselects the camera after each self-shadowed character (the world after it drawn 1% large); the menu's UI cameras reselect (640x360: its top-left quarter at 2x; 1920x1080: only its 1280x720 corner). Item 17 (a)'s lead (retail 640x360 reading 38-39 against 46, the zoomed framing, the missing HUD at 640/960) was the same defect. No player path: retail windowed and web draw the 1280x720 frame. *Fix (engine `ea92956`)*: GpuDevice keeps `logicalWidth/Height` whenever given (`outputFollowsTarget` now only sizes the output); WgpuRnd passes the Rnd's size whenever the shape follows (DC3), in either look. RB3's shape never sets it, so RB3 is unchanged by construction. Menu at frame 290 against the 1280x720 frame resampled: mean |d| 44.5 -> 2.4 (640x360), 42.7 -> 1.7 (1920x1080). The 9c protocol now gives 5,851 px / mean 0.14 retail and 5,614 / 0.13 enhanced. *Test*: `HeadlessBootTest.RetailHeadlessAtAnotherSizeKeepsViewportsInTheVirtualResolution` (logical 1280x720 in the device line; lit past x=1280 / y=720 0.942 / 0.572); 2 sabotages red (GpuDevice keeping logical only when following; WgpuRnd passing it only when the look follows: lit 0.014 / 0.000). *Recipe for a still retail frame*: any size on `np-look17`; on main, 1280x720 only. Engine doc `dc3-backend-for-rb3-wii.md` §46; lane notes `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item17/a/viewport/` | S |
| H-123 | **DONE 2026-11-20 (`np-websave`): a console that has been played before boots to the title screen.** The boot sequence is decided once at startup (`Dc3BootPlanResolve`, `System_Native.cpp`): a first launch (no global options file) runs the original sequence (splash screens, attract movie, the 4 s autosave warning, title); later launches show no splash and leave attract and the warning after 10 frames each, then wait on the title screen. `DC3_BOOT_INTRO=1`/`=0` forces either; `DC3_SHOW_SPLASH` and `DC3_FAST_BOOT` still win when set. | H-124 | **DONE 2026-11-19 (`np-tbdr`; engine `60253ee`): the standard shader does not run the native light model for a fragment that never reads it.** Found on the owner's M3 MacBook Air, where the menus cost twice a gameplay frame (M-8). `shadeStandard` ran the native model's whole block for every fragment: the shadow map's nine compare taps, the specular fetch, the light loops, the gobo fetch and the emissive fetch. The retail models light in `retailShade`, and a prelit or unlit material takes its register colour, so the results were dropped; a desktop GPU hides that cost and a tile-based one does not. The block is now inside uniform branches: `nativeLit` (native model, lit material), `nativeAdds` (a native rim or reflection on a material that has one), and a non-zero emissive multiplier. **Mac, 1710x962, GPU ms a frame at full clock: main menu 13.08 → 9.59, song select 14.00 → 9.94, lobby 9.03 → 6.79, gameplay 6.72 → 5.77, pause 13.23 → 9.47.** RTX 3090, song select: MainPass 0.92 → 0.74 ms. Picture: `milo-viewer` renders of three venues (glitterati, dclive, streetside) and four posed dancers are byte-identical before and after (engine `43437f1` against `76d230f`, each side rendered twice). `MILO_GPU_PASS_TRACE=N` (engine `43437f1`) is the tool that came out of the hunt: one `[GPUPASS]` line with a frame's render passes in order and their load/store ops | S |
| H-125 | **DONE 2026-11-19 (`np-tbdr`; engine `76d230f`, `e129004`): a mesh draw that cannot change a pixel is not sent.** Song select sent 161 draws of 433 a frame with material alpha exactly 0: hidden list rows, toasts, the letterbox, the hands-up prompt. Each was rasterised and blended across the 4x multisampled frame to leave it as it was. `DrawChangesNothing` (`MeshPipelineKey.h`): material alpha 0 under SrcAlpha or SrcAlphaAdd, no depth write, no stencil write, no bloom mask, no `next_pass` chain. The fragment alpha is the material's times the vertex's and the texel's, so such a draw blends to what it found. DC3 only (`rndshape::kSkipInvisibleDraws`; off for RB3 and rb3-xenon, whose per-draw log has readers). `MILO_DRAW_INVISIBLE=1` sends them as before, and the `[PERF]` line counts the ones left out. **Mac, same runs, after H-124: main menu 9.59 → 6.70, song select 9.94 → 7.55, lobby 6.79 → 4.32, gameplay 5.77 → 5.37, pause 9.47 → 6.75.** RTX 3090, song select: MainPass 0.82 → 0.65 ms. Test: `native/tests/test_invisible_draw_skip.cpp` holds the rule to the blend, depth and stencil states `PipelineManager` builds (56 claimed combinations, each checked against the real state), draws a scene with the layers left out and with them sent and compares the two images byte for byte, and keeps the 1/128-alpha, depth-writing and `next_pass` cases drawn. 8 of 8 sabotages of the rule and the call site fail it | S |
| H-126 | **Open, owner's Mac (2026-11-19): the app launcher asks for the Retina panel uncapped, which costs about three times the frame.** `~/Applications/Dance Central 3.app/Contents/MacOS/DC3Launcher` (made on the Mac, in no repository) exports `MILO_OUTPUT_MAX_ROWS=0`, and the Mac's engine checkout has uncommitted Retina edits (`GpuDevice.cpp`, `MetalSurface.mm`). Together the next launch draws 3420x1924. Measured there, headless at that size, new build: main menu 19.81 ms, song select 21.76 ms, gameplay 15.82 ms at full clock (45, 41 and 57 fps), against 7.84 / 8.99 / 6.54 ms at the 1920x1080 the default 1080-row cap would draw. The fix is one line: drop the export (the engine's cap on a non-discrete GPU is 1080 rows) or set it to 1080. Not changed from here: the launcher and the engine edits belong to the session working on that Mac | S |
| H-127 | ~~A display under 50 Hz holds the game to its rate and nothing says so~~ **DONE 2026-11-20 (`np-display-rate`)**: MEASURED on the owner's M3 Air, 2026-11-19: with a 3840x2160 display as the main display, reported by macOS at 30.00 Hz, the last two launches ran at about 31 fps on every screen (one whole session of 401 s: 75 `[PERF]` reports, 25.8 to 34.1 fps, none near 60), with vsync pacing and GPU time to spare. The game now posts a banner for 15 s at startup (`Dc3SlowDisplayNotice`, `native/src/platform/FramePacer_Native.{h,cpp}`; `FrameCap.ADisplayUnderFiftyHertzIsSaidOnScreen` for the text, `NativeWindowTest.ADisplayUnderFiftyHertzIsSaidAtStartup` and `.ASixtyHertzDisplayGetsNoDisplayNotice` for the call, with the rate given by `DC3_DISPLAY_HZ`). The fix itself is the display's mode (60 Hz; a 4K TV may need 1080p or an HDMI 2.0 cable or adapter). **Limit:** the rate read is the primary display's at startup, as for the frame cap; a window moved to a second, slower display is not noticed. Not seen on a real 30 Hz display by me: the tests give the rate through `DC3_DISPLAY_HZ` | S |
| H-128 | Heat throttles the Mac about 12 minutes in and nothing reacts. MEASURED on the owner's fanless M3 Air, 2026-11-19, drawing 2560x1440 (the launcher's `MILO_OUTPUT_MAX_ROWS=0`) with the GPU 92-95 % busy: macOS went from thermal state 0 to 2 and throttled; song select fell from 62 fps to 32-42, a song from 64-69 to 45-51, pose inference from 21 ms to 50-80 ms. **Built 2026-11-20 (`np-thermal`, engine branch `np-thermal`), tested on Linux with the level injected; the macOS probe is NOT yet compiled or run on a Mac.** A thermal governor in the engine (the rule and its constants: engine `src/gfx/ThermalGovernor.h`; pure, time injected): thermal pressure is the permission (fair or worse), late frames the trigger (one-second windows under 90 % of the target rate with at least 3 and 10 % of their frames late, so a load's hitch does not count; 5 in a row at fair, 2 at serious or critical); each step lowers the output's row cap to the next of 1080, 900, 720 below the present height, never under 720 and never above the cap in force (`ResolveFrameSizesThermal`: the tighter cap wins, and the render scale is never raised by it); 5 s settle after any change; one step back per 90 s at nominal, any fair-or-worse reading restarting the dwell. `GpuDevice::SetRuntimeRowCap` re-resolves and re-applies the frame's sizes between frames exactly as a window resize does (`WgpuRnd::BeginDrawing`, before the frame is acquired), with one line per change: `GpuDevice: thermal level 2 (serious), frames late: output 1920x1080 -> 1600x900 (thermal row cap 900)`. The level is `NSProcessInfo.thermalState` on macOS (windowed runs, read once a second), nothing anywhere else; `MILO_THERMAL_STATE=0..3` and `PUT /api/thermal?level=N` inject one, `GET /api/thermal` and `/api/health` report it. dc3 gives the target rate (`Dc3ThermalTargetFps`: the frame cap, else 60, never above the display's rate) and says the first step down of a session in a 10 s banner, held back until no song is on (`Dc3ThermalNotice`). `MILO_THERMAL_GOVERNOR=0` is the one switch back; with it off, with no level reported, or with no runtime cap in force the sizes and the start-up lines are today's (`ThermalRowCap.NoRuntimeCapIsExactlyTheRulesOwnSizes`). Tests: `ThermalGovernor.*`, `ThermalRowCap.*`, `FrameCap.*Thermal*` (pure), and two headless GPU runs, `NativeExitTest.ThermalPressureWithLateFramesLowersTheOutputAndCoolingRestoresIt` (1920x1080 -> 1600x900 -> 1280x720 and back, read from the log line, a screenshot's size and `/api/health`) and `.ThermalGovernorSwitchedOffLeavesTheOutputAlone`. **Limits:** rb3 and rb3-xenon are not governed (their renderer does not run it and they give no target rate); a frame of 720 rows or fewer that is supersampled keeps its supersampling; the web build has no thermal state to read; whether 1080/900/720 rows actually cool the Air, and how the steps look on its panel, is unmeasured | M |
| H-129 | **MEASURED, NO CHANGE 2026-11-19 (`np-look18`, item 18 (d)): the results screen's dark centre is the image's; Xenia's brighter one (item 17 (c) survey no. 2, centre luma 26.3 vs native 10.2) is its over-bloom.** *Arbiter*: hardware captures of the end card and results screen from YouTube uploads (OMG Easy and Hard, Scream Hard, 1, 2 Step Hard; four channels; private, `archive/` only, as the retail set). Luma at 1280x720 in regions no panel covers, Xenia / native / hardware: the tunnel stripes at the right edge (the calibration band) 26.7 / 25.4 / 21.7, so the three agree within a capture's gain; between the stripes left of the panel mean 31.6 / 2.2 / 2.9 and p25 23.4 / 0.0 / 0.0; right of the panel 23.2 / 4.9 / 3.8; the left stripes below the score box p25 35.1 / 1.0 / 0.2. Hardware and native floors are black; Xenia lays a violet haze over the left half, the known MAX-blend over-bloom on an additive UI layer, not a layer native lacks. | H-147 | ~~The player box is an empty purple frame~~ **DONE 2026-11-21 (`np-playerbox`): with no camera stream the cursor panel's two player boxes are not drawn.** Cause, measured at `main_screen` with no camera: `player_provider_1 player_present` is 1 (the player on the pad; a script setting it to 0 reads 1 again the next frame) and `player_provider_2`'s is 0, so the right box hides its "step into view" figure and waits for a picture that `MeshFilter`'s `depth-image` rule never draws without a camera, and the left box draws the figure. The retail capture (`archive/screenshots/references/dc3_main_menu.jpg`, controller mode, nobody tracked) has the figure in both. Neither says anything true to a player with no camera, so under `DC3_PLAYER_BOX=auto` (the default) `ShellInput::Draw` hides `depth_buffer_left.grp` and `depth_buffer_right.grp` for the cursor panel's draw only (`NativePlayerBox::DrawScope`, `native/src/platform/PlayerBox_Native.h`) and puts their `showing` back, so the scripts that hide and show them (`movie_overlay.dta`, `rhythm_battle.dta`, the party mode screens) read and write what they did. The boxes come back when a camera publishes a frame and go when the stream stops. `on` draws them always, as before; `off` never; `{native_settings set_player_box auto\|on\|off}` changes it at run time; `GET /api/camera` has a `playerBox` member. Frames (1280x720, `main_screen`, one launch, auto / on / auto / on): the right box's region changes by 13,701 and 13,727 px between auto and on, against 0 to 115 px between frames with nothing changed; both boxes are in the `on` frames and neither in the `auto` ones. Tests: `test_player_box.cpp` (12, in process) and `CameraViewTest` / `NoCameraViewTest` (the whole game with and without a camera, switched on and off in the run); six deliberate breaks (rule inverted, restore dropped, a wrong group name, showing a group a script hid, the hook removed, off read as auto) each fail at least one. The Xbox objects of the two `src/` files are byte-identical with and without the change (`App.obj`, `ShellInput.obj`; a control edit changes both). Known: a source that sends skeletons but no camera frames (protocol v2, the synthetic sensor without `--frames`) also loses the boxes under `auto`. Run after the landing, on main `dbff5c5e2` (2026-11-21): `scripts/native_test.sh` 1697 registered / 1627 passed / 0 failed / 70 skipped against a budget of 70, exit 0; the web build boots (`smoke-test.mjs` PASS) and loads a song (`gameplay.mjs`, 0 errors), and its song-select and song frames under `auto` have no box. On the GPD (`d87bc61be`) the 12 in-process tests pass and the two whole-game tests do not start (its system `python3` has no numpy, which the camera frames need). Not done: an `on` frame in a song, which would show that these two groups are also the boxes under the scores. Open, with the menu redesign: whether a player with no camera should get something in the box instead (the dancer, or a pose skeleton). |

Enhanced look only; retail keeps the authored tiling, beat included.** `noise_mono.tex` (8x256, alternating rows: authored scanlines) is tiled (1,3), so 768 map rows run down any screen; the linear filter's drifting blend gave a 2.1–2.8 px line pattern under a beat (toggle proof in six venues; bid and glitterati unaffected). Inspection `{rnd native_look noise_rows_on\|noise_rows_off\|noise_rows_rule}`; the derived constants are memoised per (rows, map height, base, top). Output rows, not drawn rows (`NoiseOutputRows`), so s1 and s2 give one picture (line component corr 1.000). DC3-shape property `rndshape::kNoiseSnapsToOutputRows` (`kDC3RetailModel`; RB3Wii false); on engine `94335fe` rb3-xenon frames 11/11 identical against dfdfd1b, rb3 ctest 158/0/7. WGSL changed (RB3RetailPostDC3 and PostProcPass composites). Tests `DC3NoiseRows.*` (6, including `RetailKeepsTheAuthoredTilingAtEverySize`, `InspectionSwitchesTheRetiling`, `SnapIsMemoised`) and `NoiseMapReadsOneTexelPerOutputRow`; seven sabotages red. | H-149 | **MEASURED, NO CHANGE 2026-11-21 (GPD Pocket 4, Radeon 890M, RADV; dc3 `6e1f27d0f`, engine `dfdfd1b`): the enhanced look's world anisotropy stays 16 at every render scale, specular AA stays on and the world LOD bias stays 0.** This closes the Radeon cost H-137 and H-139 left open. The 3090 paid about 0.007 ms (H-139); the Radeon pays about 15 times that and it is 0.1 ms of a 16.7 ms frame, so there is no by-scale rule. *Specular AA on against off*: +0.010 ms of world passes on 1.90 ms (four rounds, +0.007 to +0.013); 14.0–18.3 % of pixels change by 1 level or more, no blocks. *LOD bias*: the rule and a forced 0 give the same frame and time; -1 against 0 costs +0.076 ms (1920x1080 far02) and +0.105 ms (1280x720 far02) for x1.03–1.04 sharpness, less than anisotropy buys at the same cost, so it is not taken. | H-151 | **DONE 2026-11-21 (`np-look23`, item 23 (c) and (c2); engine `f2afd4a`, `d1b1ed6`, `955d637`, `5930fa8`, `bc763cd`; dc3 `c02eda788`, `0c52490bf`, `7f126f4b9`, `fea3f5d41`): the bright rim round the figures in close-ups is the depth of field, and the original has it; the enhanced look's DOF now gathers by depth through a half-size factor, so an in-focus figure no longer spreads into the blurred background. Retail unchanged.** Held cu01 with each post stage left out in one launch (`{rnd native_post_skip mask}`): the rim goes with the DOF (edge halo over dclive's dancer leg, mean luma 3–10 px outside the edge over 20–30 px outside: 27.4 retail, 4.4 no DOF, 21.7 no bloom, 1.2 neither). NgDOFProc's quarter-size downsample and blur are depth-blind, so the figure's colour leaks ~3 quarter texels (~12 px at 720) into the background beside it. Xenia shows the same rim (halo 25.9, same width and shape), and so does hardware footage of dci, so it is the original's picture and `DC3_LOOK=retail` keeps it. Enhanced only (`LookSettings::postDofDepthWeight`, with `postDofFilter`, world grade only): a half-size pre-pass (`DofDepthWeightFactorHalf`, R16Float, ((w+1)>>1) x ((h+1)>>1), read in scene texels so odd sizes map exactly) writes the blur factor squared from one depth load per half-size texel; the tent downsample (`DofDownsampleTentDepthWeightHalf`) weighs each of its 16 bilinear taps by that factor sampled at the tap, premultiplied, weight in alpha, into RGBA16Float quarter targets; the blurs are unchanged; the composite divides the weight back out and fades to the scene where nothing out of focus was gathered. Final build, held dclive cu01, one launch per size: halo 27.1 → 5.9 at 720 and 31.9 → 6.5 at 1080 s2 (no-DOF floor 4.5 / 4.8; on vs on 0 / 2 px). Retail `dof_depth_weight_off`/`_on`/`_rule` within the no-change control at dclive 720 and 1080 s2, throneroom 720, 640x360, 1280x800 and 1268x713 (largest level 2, as the control). The move cards' opaque interiors move ≤ 1 px between off and on; the score's and labels' translucent pixels move ≤ 2 levels, as they did with the 16-tap gather. Cost of the weight, Radeon 890M at scale 1 (playtest-rig session dc3-decomp-9c, two repetitions): **+0.248 ms at 1920x1080** (factor 0.187 + downsample 0.144 against the plain tent's 0.136; 5.3 % of a 4.665 ms frame) and **+0.121 ms at 1268x713** (4.4 %), inside the acceptance rule (0.5 / 0.25 ms). Measured and rejected on the same 890M: the first gather, one depth load per tap in the downsample itself (16 per quarter texel), +0.836 / +0.369 ms; 4 loads per quarter texel +0.367 / +0.160 with glow returning at partly covered taps; 1 load +0.179 / +0.076 with more of it (up to 41 levels round a raised hand, quarter-texel steps at a houseparty edge). On the 3090 the 16-load gather cost +0.068 ms at 1080 s1 and +0.290 ms at 1080 s2 (GPU to itself); the half factor was timed there only contended (another lane's runs on the GPU): its two passes read 2.7x the plain tent at 1080 s1, against 4.2x for 16 loads in the round-1 launch. The half factor differs most from the 16-load gather in the pocket of background between the thumb and the hand at dclive: darker than both off and 16 by about 7 levels over ~2,000 px at 1080 (worst pixel −21; −47 at 713); it reads as the background's own colour with no figure colour in it, not a fringe. Inspection `{rnd native_look dof_depth_weight_on\|dof_depth_weight_off\|dof_depth_weight_rule}` (the round-1 gather tokens are gone). Prewarm compiles the four depth-weight pipelines when the look starts with the weight on. Rejected options, with their costs, in the evidence README (full-resolution depth-aware gather ~1 ms at 1080, half-resolution bilateral ~0.3 ms). WGSL changed: DofPass gained `fs_dw_factor_half`, `fs_down_tent_dwh` and `fs_composite_bspline_dw`; the existing entry points are unchanged; R16Float as a render target sampled through the filtering sampler is core WebGPU, no new feature. Tests `DC3LookPostTest.DepthWeightedDofKeepsTheFigureOutOfTheBackgroundBlur` (background lift beside a bright in-focus half 36.5 → 0.70 levels, bound 0 ± 1.5; far-background stripes blurred as much as without the weight; no pipeline compiled after Prewarm) and `DC3LookDofDepthWeight.FollowsTheLookAndItsSwitch`. Sabotages red: factor pre-pass writes 1 (lift 36.8), writes 0 (stripes left sharp), composite division removed (−3.4), switch ignored, Prewarm skips the weight's pipelines, factor read one half-size texel toward the background (15.7); one half-size texel toward the figure stays green (the test has one edge). Filter 57/57. On engine `bc763cd`: rb3 `8bf498e8a` ctest 165 registered, 158 passed, 0 failed, 7 skipped; rb3-xenon `0898780b8` rb3-render 11 of 11 frame hashes identical against engine main `f80db1b`, 414 PASS per run. Evidence `archive/.../item23/c/` and `item23/c2/`. The stepped outline its composite left at depth edges (the mix amount came from one depth sample) is H-180. |

| H-153 | **MEASURED, NO CHANGE 2026-11-21 (items 25 and 27, no code change): the four close-up findings of H-152 are the original's picture, and vertex-lit materials are not lit darker natively.** *Item 25*: the same outfits on Xenia against the native retail look (1280x720, render scale 1, `DC3_DISPLAY_GAMMA=off`, omg, venue forced to awesome, held cu01 at song 20 s, outfits and shot confirmed on both sides; Mat dumps identical on both sides). (1) Lens discs: Xenia draws tan01's lenses as the same opaque pale discs (5x5 centres 251,229,243 against native 238,218,241); `tan_lens.mat` is blend 1 (opaque), grey 0xA7A7A7, with a cube environment map and no texture. mo04's (blend 3, alpha 0.45) is a semi-clear fill on both. (3) Cyan fringe on glitch05's hair cards: 217 px on Xenia, 201 native. (4) DCI visors: no face shows through on Xenia either; `DCI_mindctrl_visor.mat` is blend 1. So none of these is a native defect, and none is the "reflections drawn over the surface" report; softer hair edges are an enhanced-look change (item 26). *Item 27*: item 25 had read tan04's alpha-blended, vertex-lit lens 22 % darker natively. Over each side's own lens interior the authored lens is native/Xenia 0.966 and 1.016; made opaque with the environment map off, 0.947–0.981, with the per-pixel-lit face in the same frames at 1.002. Clothing forced from per-pixel to vertex-lit (specular, rim and environment map cleared) changes by the same ratio on both sides: rasa06 torso 1.029 Xenia / 1.034 native, legs 0.990 / 0.986. Two cautions for later probes: Xenia renders some forced states (vertex-lit skin, a vertex-lit material that keeps its specular) as a flat unlit fill, so they are not a lighting reference; and `set per_pixel_lit` / `set use_environ` do nothing on a Mat with a MetaMaterial until `{$m set metamaterial ""}`. Also seen, not chased: dark cloth reads 0.76–0.82 of Xenia with post on and 0.93–1.10 with post off, the direction of Xenia's over-bloom already arbitrated against hardware (H-112, H-129, H-143).
