| H-154 | **MEASURED AND REJECTED 2026-11-21 (item 26, branch `np-look26` at engine `9b11649` / dc3 `27d0062df`, not landed): scaling the cut-out's tested alpha by mip level fills hair in, and also draws venue lines the original leaves out.** *Why hair breaks up under the enhanced look*: it samples the full mip chain with anisotropy, so a thin strand's alpha is averaged with its clear surround and falls under the cut; retail samples two levels, so its strands alias but stay whole. *The change tried*: in the world cut-out pipelines the tested alpha is scaled by `1 + 0.25 x mip level` (coverage-preserving alpha, after Castaño and Golus), one `LookSettings` bool with `{rnd native_look hair_edge_on\|hair_edge_off}`. *What it does well*: strand tips fill in (hair mask at dclive cu01, 1268x713: retail 8,488 px, enhanced as landed 8,240, with the change 8,633); 22 outfit launches and 5 venues show characters the same or better; retail and HUD 0 px; cost on the Radeon 890M at most +0.041 ms at 1920x1080 and +0.024 ms at 1268x713. *Why it is rejected*: dclive's floor carries neon lines (`DLV_PeakLine*.mat`, `DLV_PeakLineFade*.mat`; SrcAlpha blend, alpha_cut, thresholds 90 and 110) whose fade sits just under the cut over a wide area. Retail draws nothing there; the enhanced look as landed draws two faint dotted lines; with the change a solid bright band about 3 px thick appears (core mean luma: retail 30.0, landed 33.8, change 112.1) and its bloom lifts the floor round it from 31 to 50 (34.3k of 35.3k changed px at 713 are floor). It still fills at scale 0.125 (106.3) and at 0.5 (118.0), so the scale is not a lever, and it comes and goes with the camera (847–26,288 px per frame over 90 stepped frames). The same brightening shows weakly on dci's stage light strips (824 px, about +17 per channel) and houseparty's floor stripes. Single-frame flicker in hair is unchanged by the change (oblio04 75.6 against 74.9, glitch05 31.1 against 32.3). **Rule kept from this**: an enhanced cut-out rule may not raise averaged alpha against the threshold, because content authored just under the cut becomes visible; and a look change is checked with a retail frame beside every off/on pair, at more than one camera moment, and in more than one venue (the lane's own frame, one pose at one song time, did not show the band). The replacement tried is the original's alpha test taken at each MSAA sample (H-155). **Correction (H-155)**: the "retail" hair figure in this row (8,488 px) and the statement that retail's strands stay whole came from `{rnd native_look retail}` in an enhanced run, which sampled whole mip chains; a `DC3_LOOK=retail` launch shows hair more broken than the enhanced look as landed. The floor-band finding stands: a retail launch draws nothing there. Evidence `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item26/`, `item26b/`; playtest-rig frames in `/home/free/tmp/np-fitness-scratch/lane-hair*/`. |
| H-155 | **MEASURED, NOT ADOPTED 2026-11-21 (items 26d to 26g, branch `np-look26`, engine merged at `0fafacd`): the original's alpha test at each MSAA sample is built, tested and selectable by token, and the enhanced look keeps the cut-out coverage it had. Fixed on the way: a look switched by token now reads the texture levels that look uploads.** *What was built*: in the world cut-out pipelines of a 4x pass (hair, crowd clothing, palms, floor lines) the original's alpha test is taken at each of the four sample positions, one bilinear fetch each at the level the original's sampler reads (`clamp(lod, 0, 1)`), and the sample mask is the four results (`standard_wgsl.inc` `cutoutSampleMask`, `SceneUniforms::cutoutSampleTest`). Nothing passes that the original's test would not pass at that point, so the floor band of H-154 cannot come back (band core mean luma: landed 58.59, per-sample 58.33; the boost was +25.6). Modes: **B** the four results alone; **C** the original's test at the pixel centre first, a pass drawing the pixel solid and a fail leaving the four results as a fringe; **E** B where the texture is minified by at most one level and C above; **centre** a diagnostic. *The finding that changed the question*: textures are uploaded once, with the level count of the look the process started in (retail 2, enhanced the whole chain), so `{rnd native_look retail}` in an enhanced run sampled whole chains through retail's sampler. Its hair looked whole (oblio04 at dclive cu01, 1268x713: inside the hair mass solid share 1.000, strand breaks 14.40 per 1000 px) where a `DC3_LOOK=retail` launch of the same hold has holes (.974, 28.73). **Retail hair is more broken than the enhanced look as landed, not less** (inside .974 against .996, bangs .302 against .330, strands .473 against .489), so the reason for this work (H-154's "retail strands stay whole") was an artefact of the reference. Earlier cut-out and texture-detail comparisons that took retail from the token (items 24, 26, 26b, 26d) carry the same error; lighting, post and colour comparisons do not. *The fix (landed)*: after a look switch, world, text and HUD samplers clamp to the level count the switched look uploads (`milo::LookSwitchTexLevels`, `ApplyLookSampling`). The retail token in an enhanced run now matches a retail launch in all three hair regions to 0.000. A run that never switches looks gets no clamp, so every launch, rb3 and rb3-xenon sample as before. The other direction cannot be made faithful: a run started retail has two levels to read. *E against the landed coverage* (survey: 31 launches, six venues, four outfit pairs, 640x360 to 1920x1080 scale 2, retail from its own launch): E changes 406 to 4,494 px of a 1268x713 frame. For it: strand tips and fringe ends fill in (oblio04 strands solid share .519 against .489, bangs .346 against .330; higher at every size); it draws nothing a retail launch does not draw (0 to 77 px per shot, on strand ends); the faint dotted `DLV_PeakLine` line the landed coverage draws on dclive's floor, which retail does not draw, goes away; text and HUD do not move (the changed-pixel set is identical with the HUD shown and hidden, 0 px on HUD pixels at 1268x713 and 1920x1080). Against it: the pixels it fills are retail's aliased dots drawn solid, so a silhouette that is smooth today comes out dotted (strand breaks per 1000 px: landed 15.41, E 19.33, B 24.39, retail launch 28.73; plainest in the web close-up, isolated dots above the hair and on the fringe); floor stripe edges are stepped again in dclive's and houseparty's far shots (583 and about 700 px, as retail, invisible at 100 %); palm edges pop more in motion (3.05 against 2.03 px per frame over 90 stepped frames; hair equal, 75.15 against 74.81); and it costs four alpha fetches per cut-out fragment. B alone is retail's cut supersampled four times, with coverage within 5 to 12 % of retail's, and is noisy inside a hair mass (.983 solid). *Decision*: a trade, not an improvement, so no look sets `LookSettings::cutoutSampleTest` and the enhanced look is unchanged. `{rnd native_look hair_edge_on}` selects E, `hair_edge_b\|hair_edge_c\|hair_edge_e\|hair_edge_centre` one mode, `hair_edge_off` and `hair_edge_rule` the landed coverage. Adopting E is one line in `SettingsFor`; before that it needs the Radeon 890M timing (limits +0.25 ms at 1920x1080 and +0.15 ms at 1268x713 over the landed coverage) and the sample-mask bit order checked on RADV, Metal and the web. **Rules kept from this**: a retail reference for cut-outs or texture detail comes from a `DC3_LOOK=retail` launch (or, since this fix, the token in a run started enhanced; never the token in a run started retail); "moves toward retail" is not "better" where retail is the aliased side; and the 20 s pause lands on one of three frames (20.0087, 20.0171, 20.0254), so a second launch shows the same pose only when its hold time is equal, which the capture has to check. *Measured afterwards on the Radeon 890M* (the rig session, landed heads engine `5e8d5c8` / dc3 `1a2c31d08`, mains power, dclive, omg): modes C and E cost nothing measurable against off (largest reading +0.032 ms at 1920x1080 and +0.010 ms at 1268x713, summed over all GPU passes, inside the 0.03 to 0.06 ms spread between segments of one state; signs go both ways); sample-mask bit i is sample position i there too, and `LookCutoutMipTest` and `LookSwitchLevelsTest` pass 14 of 14 with 0 skipped; the retail token in an enhanced launch matches a real `DC3_LOOK=retail` launch exactly at 1268x713 (0 pixels above 16 levels) and at 1920x1080 everywhere except 35 pixels on glitch's upper lash line, where the token draws the lash thinner and lighter (169/118/126 against 180/126/131). Two token launches agree there to the level and two retail launches to 5 levels, so it is the token and not launch noise. **Open**: that residual; it reads like one sampler or material the level clamp does not reach, visible only where the lash is near one texel per pixel. A survey that compares launches with a polled venue-state change has a floor of 16 to 32 pixels unless the engine is slowed before the change so it lands on the same game frame. *Not measured*: the Mac; mask bit order on the web (the web frame was checked by pixel counts and by eye only); the pause menu (its own control fails, the screen animates); 1280x800 against retail (the enhanced look fits the frame to 16:9 by design, so the two frames differ in size). Tests: `LookCutoutMipTest` (11) and `LookSwitchLevelsTest` (3) in `native/tests/test_look_cutout_mips.cpp`. Evidence `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item26d/`, `item26e/`, `item26f/`, `item26f-survey/` (the 890M: `gpd-890m-timing.md`, `gpd-890m-retail-token.md`). |
| H-156 | **DONE 2026-11-21 (`np-song-end-clock`; engine `cee292b`, merged `144ecb0`; dc3 `b03e37589`): a song whose audio ends before its MIDI `[end]` froze at the end on any machine with sound.** Seen on the Mac playtest at the end of Technologic (DC2 export pack): the game sat on `game_screen` at 63 fps, dancers frozen, no results. Technologic has 6,436,006 samples of audio (145.94 s) and `[end]` -- which `perform.dta` turns into `{game_panel win}` -- at 146.89 s; song time stopped at 146.225 s (beat 310.57). Cause: the song clock is the bytes the mixer has rendered from the stream's receivers (`StandardStream::GetRawTime`), and `StreamReceiverNative::IsFinished()` told the mixer to drop a receiver once its stream was finished, 1..2 buffers after the last audio byte; on the 360 the voice plays zero-filled buffers for as long as the receiver is polled and the clock runs on. `IsFinished()` is now false for the receiver's whole life (the destructor takes it out of the mixer). Reproduced on Linux with `MILO_AUDIO=1 MILO_AUDIO_BACKEND=null` (frozen at the same 146.22 s; gdb: stream `kFinished`, reader done); the same run on the fix reaches `perform_endgame_screen`. Test: `StreamReceiverNative.TheMixerPlaysOnThroughSilenceAfterTheStreamFinished` drives the real mixer past a stream's finish point and requires the byte count to keep moving. Why no flow test showed it: a headless run has no mixer and `StandardStream::UpdateTime` runs on its timer there; no gate plays a song to its end against a device. (H-153 to H-155 are taken by handover 25, in flight.) |
| H-157 | **DONE 2026-11-21 (`np-gesture-cooldown`): with a camera running, a keyboard or pad press keeps the menus, and raising both hands gives them back.** Playtest on the Mac: "the gesture detection is truly awful right now... super unusable atm because my arm clips in/out between frames". Under the faithful controller-mode policy the image hands the menus to the hand cursor 5 s after the last press (`ShellInput::Poll`, the helpbar's `controller_mode_timeout`), which is right for a Kinect and wrong for a webcam at a desk: the arm of the person at the keyboard is cut by the edge of the picture, tracked on one frame and gone on the next, and five seconds after a key it was moving the highlight. **Now:** an idle exit that follows a press waits the press hold, `DC3_CONTROLLER_HOLD_MS` (default 300000; `0` = the image), and both hands raised (the image's `HandsUpGestureFilter`, 1.2 s, at least 1.5 s after the last press) leave controller mode at once without also opening the correct-identity overlay. A notice names the way back the first three times a press takes the menus. Start, a song starting and the scripts' own exits are untouched, and so is the auto policy's 5 s handover when a camera comes up with no key touched. `System_Native.cpp` "The press hold", three `HX_NATIVE` hooks in `ShellInput.cpp` and one in `HamUI.cpp`. **Checked** end to end by `scripts/controller_hold_check.py` (synthetic sensor, faithful, 20 s hold, 12 checks: still the keyboard's 9 s after the last press, hands up out in 1.4 s, no overlay, the hold ends at 20.0 s naming itself) as `ControllerHoldTest`; `ControllerHoldSabotageTest` runs it with the hold off and requires the failures (7 of 12 fail, the overlay check among them). **Not done:** after a song the results come up in gesture mode as in the image (the song ends controller mode), so a keyboard player touches a key again there; keeping the keyboard's claim across a song is the next step if the playtest asks for it. The hold length is not in Options yet. |
| H-158 | **DONE 2026-11-21 (`np-binding-log`): a playtest log says which body each player was scored on.** Playtest on the Mac, 2026-11-20: somebody walked through a two-player song (Milkshake) and the log could not place it. The pose server reports at most two bodies (`pose_server.py` clamps `max_persons` to 2), so a third never shows in `POSE_STATS persons=`; the lines that record which body a player is bound to needed `DC3_SCORING_DEBUG`, and the Dock app records no pose session. Now `DC3_BINDING_LOG` (dev profile on, release off) prints the change-only who-is-who lines (`bind pN -> tracking id … rule=`, `stand-in … released`, `tracker id … is tracking id …`, `slot N <- tracking id`, `player->slot binding`) at no more than 20 per 5 s, and the `DC3 MOVE_RATING` line carries `body=<tracking id>`. **Not done, and waiting for a log that shows which rule fired:** no stand-in while the other player is still bound (`PoseIdentity_Native.h` rule 3), never adopting a stand-in as the player after the 5 s window, a third detection for a passer-by, and a per-launch pose recording from the Dock app (`DC3_POSE_RECORD` is only set by `scripts/play.sh`). |
| H-159 | **MEASURED, NOT ADOPTED 2026-11-21 (item 28, branch `np-look28` at engine `dc14f30` / dc3 `907f3e110`, not landed): a screen-space ambient-occlusion pass was built twice, and neither form improves the picture.** *Why it was tried*: a survey of all six venues in their normal (low and high) lighting states against hardware frames found nothing wrong in the enhanced look; what reads flat is the original's own lack of contact shadow under crowd members and furniture (the survey before this one was peak-state only, because a scripted capture scores FLAWLESS; `{game_panel force_venue_state low\|high\|awesome}` forces the state). *Round 1* (engine `f8dd937`, dc3 `476d94724`): a hemisphere estimator on the resolved world frame before depth of field and bloom, 8 directions x 4 steps at half the drawn size, normals from depth, radius 14 units (1 unit is about 1 inch), bilateral blur and upsample, multiplied in. It does put a contact shadow on stairs, the couch and crowd feet. It also soots faces and necks, dims the dance-pad ring, and darkens the whole frame by 1 to 3.3 levels (far shot, low state, 1280x720: dci 651,190 px changed, mean luma 67.65 to 65.38; rollerrink 637,365 px, 87.33 to 84.71; throneroom 231,039 px, 67.25 to 66.22), and costs 3.70 ms at 1920x1080 render scale 2 on the 3090 (0.233 ms at 1268x713). A trade, and too dear. *Round 2*: the same pass cut down to a floor-contact shadow: only surfaces within about 30 degrees of level take it (`smoothstep(0.5, 0.85, normal.z)`), radius 8 units, 8 taps, half the presented size, and no shadow where the bloom mask says the pixel glows (full at mask 0.03, none at 0.10; the ring's median mask is 0.08 to 0.12, a lit floor's about 0.02). Cost 0.209 ms at 1920x1080 scale 2, 0.101 ms at scale 1, 0.060 ms at 1268x713. Faces no longer change (cheek, forehead and nose 0; a few pixels on the headset wire and collar). And almost nothing else does either: far shot, low state, pixels moved by more than 2 levels are 3.09 % at rollerrink and 1.88 % at houseparty, mean luma -0.35 and -0.11; in close-ups 1.76 % and 2.43 %; in the peak state under 1.3 %. At 100 % and at 2x the off and on frames cannot be told apart without flipping between them, and the term's own view is a nearly white frame. *Why*: the venue textures and vertex colours already carry painted shading at exactly the places a screen-space term finds (stair risers, skirting, under furniture), and the characters stand on mirror or emissive floors in the peak state. So the wide form darkens what is already shaded and the narrow form has nothing left to add. *Decision*: not landed. No look sets it and main has none of it; the branch keeps the pass (`src/gfx/AoPass.{h,cpp}`, tokens `ao_on\|ao_off\|ao_rule\|ao_soft\|ao_strong\|ao_only`) and its 13 tests (`native/tests/test_look_ao.cpp`) in case a later change (a dynamic shadow under each crowd member, which is the gap that is real, plainest at streetside) wants the depth prefilter. *Not measured*: the 890M and the Mac; the web build; round 2 at the other four venues; the upsample's depth weight has no test that catches its removal. **Rule kept from this**: before commissioning a screen-space shading pass on content with baked shading, look at the term's own view over the landed frame at 100 %; if the term is dark only where the art is already dark, stop there. Evidence `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item28-normal-state/`, `item28/`, `item28-survey/`, `item28b/`. *A third form, 2026-11-21 (item 43, branch `np-look43` at engine `4350d9e` / dc3 `120bb6198`, not landed)*: commissioned by the coordinator without reading this row, which is the mistake this note records; the rule above would have stopped it. 13 depth reads at half size from one sample of the 4x depth, radius 10 units, one blur, multiplied into the scene image before the depth of field and the bloom; tokens `ao_on`, `ao_off`, `ao_rule`, `ao_show`. Same verdict. Best frame: a seam two or three pixels thick where rollerrink's boombox meets the floor (5,473 pixels by more than 8 levels, largest drop 31, 1280x720 scale 1). Worst: rollerrink's DISCO sign dimmed like a lit surface (12,047 pixels by more than 8 levels, largest 26) and the player's pad ring dimmed (12,184, largest 27), as in round 1; at viewing size the off and on frames cannot be told apart. Cost on the 3090: 0.555 ms at 1920x1080 scale 2 (+9.6 % of the summed GPU passes) and 0.078 ms at 1280x720 scale 1 (+8.9 %), three passes. The lane's own condition for finishing it is a per-draw mask for emissive, additive and pad draws; not pursued. The gap that is real here (a prop with nothing under it: rollerrink's boombox, H-164) is the kind the contact-shadow pass of H-161 was built for; but the boombox is data only (meshes in the shared `chars_base.milo`; no class in `src/` names it), so giving it one needs a rule that names an object, which the crowd's rule did not: not commissioned. Its own `boomie_shadow.mesh` is no way round that: hidden in the venue's data, and shown by DTA on a held rollerrink frame it draws as a flat white slab over the boombox's front (7,781 pixels by more than 8 levels, largest 249; the same setting twice 0) with nothing on the floor. Evidence `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item43/` (`README.md`, `COORDINATOR.md`). |
| H-160 | **DONE 2026-11-21 (`np-look31`, item 31; engine pin `1e7f47f`): every native output goes through the curve the console sends a television, and the dark and middle tones are 13 to 16 levels lower than before.** *What was wrong*: since G-27 the native port applied the game's ramp `pow(v, 0.85)` alone, on the inference that the ramp a game sets is what the screen shows. It is not. `D3DDevice_SetGammaRamp` (0x82BA49C8) passes every ramp through `GammaRampCorrected`, which looks each 10-bit entry up in a 1024-entry table from `GetCurrentGammaTable` (0x82BC9DC8). That table is `Encode(sRGB decode(k/1023))` for the display type `VdGetCurrentDisplayGamma` reports: type 1 (sRGB) is the identity at all 1024 codes, type 2 (television) is the BT.709 curve (`x >= 0.018 ? 1.099 * pow(x, 0.45) - 0.099 : 4.5 * x`), type 3 a power, and any other type takes the television branch (decoded from `build/373307D9/asm/xdk/d3d9i/gamma.s` and `state.s`). So on a television the screen shows `BT.709 encode(sRGB decode(ramp))`: byte 16 leaves as 10.7 (ramp alone 24.2), 54 as 52.3 (67.8), 128 as 129.9 (141.6), 235 as 235.1 (237.3), 255 as 254.3 (the same). The ramp alone is what a console on a VGA cable shows. *Change*: the engine's `DisplayRamp` computes the 256 entries on the CPU (`DisplayRamp::HardwareTable`, the D3D arithmetic step for step in 10 bits) and the pass looks them up from a 256x1 `R32Float` texture. The sRGB type keeps the old per-pixel formula, so rb3 (its shape returns the sRGB type from `rndshape::DisplayGammaType()`) is unchanged. rb3-xenon compiles the engine's DC3 shape with `kDC3RetailPost` false (that repo has no `BloomLocFactor` or `GetBloomMultiplier`), where `DisplayGamma()` returns 0, so it applies no ramp at all and is unchanged for that reason. DC3 defaults to the television type in both looks. *One switch back*: `DC3_DISPLAY_GAMMA=srgb` is the ramp alone, the picture before this row. `off` is the front buffer. A bare number is now that gamma on a television; before this row it meant the ramp alone, which is now `srgb:<g>`. At runtime: `{rnd native_look display_srgb}`, `display_tv`, `display_off`, `display_rule`. *Against hardware*: the reference is capture-card footage of a console from three uploaders (`archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/retail/`), cable and capture device unknown. It is not Xenia: Xenia's headless frames are taken before any ramp (`archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/xenia/INDEX.md`) and say nothing about this curve; Xenia's own default display type is 2, the television. Median luma of fixed scenery, hardware / before / now: streetside asphalt 51 to 58 / 70.1 / 54.9; cardboard 105 to 114 / 122.5 / 109.5; graffiti wall 58 to 60 / 79.5 / 64.4; houseparty rug 22 / 38.7 / 22.8; wood floor 5 to 10 / 25.4 / 11.7; dci floor 3 to 13 / 14.1 / 5.3; the unlit flashcard's red and blue 86 to 91 and 83 to 88 / 100 and 103 / 86 and 89; the results-screen panel of H-143 61.1 / about 75 / about 60. Above about 30 the footage cannot tell the television table from the bare front buffer (they are under 2 levels apart there); below it the hardware is nearer the table. The hardware stills labelled as the normal state match the native `high` state, not `low`. *Survey* (six venues; low, high and peak states; the gameplay camera and a close-up; 1280x720 scale 1; one launch per venue, the curve switched by token on the held frame): the new frame is the table applied to the old frame at every pixel but at most 948 of 921,600 (elements that move while paused), and repeats of one setting differ at no more than 101 pixels by at most 3 levels. Mean luma falls 10 to 14 levels in the normal states and 5 to 11 in the peak states. By eye: floors and shadows gain depth and the grey veil over dark scenes goes (plainest at houseparty and throneroom); saturated colours and highlights do not move; dark suits lose some fold detail. Menus and HUD text stay as sharp (the pass is one table per pixel) and light text on dark panels gains contrast. *Tests*: `native/tests/test_display_ramp.cpp`, 5 tests (the table against the reference values and the public numbers; the sRGB table is the identity at 1024 codes; the sRGB pass is the old formula on the GPU; the television pass is the table on the GPU at all 256 bytes, alpha untouched; the tokens switch and restore). 14 sabotages, 13 caught; the survivor (`>=` to `>` at 0.018) changes no table entry. *Cost*: `DisplayRampPass` at 1920x1080 scale 2 is 0.125 ms with the ramp alone and 0.127 to 0.132 ms with the table (a first form with the table in a uniform array cost 0.28 to 0.35 ms and was replaced). *Not checked*: the target's table code was decoded, not executed (outside check: with an identity ramp the table gives 16 → 6.0, 54 → 38.1, 235 → 232.3, the numbers of a public write-up of the console's conversion); which display type a console reports for which cable is from Xenia's kernel notes only; two results-screen captures from one uploader are brighter than every candidate and unexplained. *Checked after landing (dc3 `93ffa703b`, engine `d7be323`)*: on the Radeon 890M (RADV; playtest-rig session, `display_srgb` and `display_tv` alternated in one launch, four rounds, cu01 in dclive, paused) the table costs nothing measurable over the ramp alone: `DisplayRampPass` table minus ramp +0.002 and -0.008 ms at 1920x1080 scale 2 (pass 0.73 ms, frame 15.9 ms), +0.000 and -0.001 ms at 1920x1080 scale 1 (pass 0.19 ms), +0.002 ms at 1268x713 (pass 0.084 ms), against a limit of +0.2 ms; the `display_tv` frames read 10.4 to 10.7 levels lower in mean in all 24 pairs. On the M3 Air (Metal) a headless YMCA run ended `frame_cap_reached` code 0 with no validation line, and the tokens alternated twice in play gave mean luma 85.09 / 97.35 / 85.17 / 95.64 (tv / srgb / tv / srgb). The full gate on `93ffa703b`: 1743 registered, 1673 passed, 0 failed, 70 skipped. The display tokens log no `[look]` inspection line (the rig tool had to send another token as a marker). *Owner's call*: the enhanced look takes the curve too, because the ramp alone was a porting error and not a look choice, but it darkens the picture seen every day, so it is listed under "Owner decisions pending" below. *Closed empty in the same round*: item 29 (forcing every character and the crowd to level of detail 0 changes no pixel at four venues: nothing is drawn below full detail) and item 30 (the self shadow, floor shadow and spot cut-out that `WorldCrowd::Draw3DChars` switches off on crowd characters in game: nothing to turn on). *Found, not fixed*: `SceneBindGroupCostTest.CameraSwitchesReuseTheirSceneBindGroupsFrameToFrame` fails when it runs in one process after the `LookCutoutMipTest` suite and passes alone, on main too; the gate runs one process per test, so it does not show there. Evidence `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item31/` (`README.md`, `gamma_ref.py`, `coordinator/`), `item29-lod-probe/`, `item30-crowd-shadow-probe/`. *Added in handover 29 (engine `47e9df9`)*: `display_srgb`, `display_tv` and the other display tokens now print a `[look]` line when they take effect, as every other inspection token does, so a capture's log says which curve a frame was taken under. |
| H-161 | **DONE 2026-11-21 (`np-look32`, item 32; engine pin `e1d6a3e`): each 3D crowd member has a soft contact shadow in the enhanced look. New work beyond the original, which draws no shadow of any kind for its crowd; the retail look draws none.** *Why*: on a pale floor the crowd floats. Streetside's sidewalk crowd, right behind the dancers in the close-up, was the plainest case. Screen-space ambient occlusion was measured twice for this and not adopted (H-159), and the game's own shadow paths are switched off for crowd members with nothing to turn on (item 30, under H-160). *What was built*: `WorldCrowd::Draw3DChars` (`src/system/world/Crowd.cpp`, under `HX_NATIVE`, `kDrawNormal` only) asks the engine for one ellipse per member after its draw: on the member's origin plane lifted 0.03 of its height, half axes 0.20 and 0.13 of the height. The engine (`platform/ContactShadowHook.h`, `gfx/ContactShadowPass`, `WgpuRnd::QueueContactShadow`; engine doc section 50) draws each as one quad with an analytic falloff, `1 - 0.45 * g`, `g = (exp(-3 r^2) - e^-3) / (1 - e^-3)`: 45 % at the centre, exactly nothing at the rim, so there is no edge. Multiply blend, depth tested against the world and the characters already drawn, no depth write, no alpha write (the frame alpha is the bloom mask), main frame pass only, before the transparent draws and the text. No full-screen pass. `LookSettings::crowdContactShadow` is on for the enhanced look and off for retail and for rb3 and rb3-xenon, which queue nothing. The one switch: `{rnd native_look crowd_shadow_off|crowd_shadow_on|crowd_shadow_rule}` on a running game, or `DC3_LOOK=retail`. *Why the lift is 0.03*: streetside's sidewalk members stand with their origin up to about 2 units inside the floor; at 0.005 most of each ellipse was under the floor's depth and showed as thin streaks. *Measured* (3090, held frames, omg, enhanced, television curve, 1280x720 scale 1, each setting twice in one launch; pixels changed by more than 8 levels and the largest luma drop; the same-setting repeats read 0 to 3 pixels): streetside close-up low 2,607 and 46, high 2,795 and 43, peak 193 and 11; streetside wide low 1,593 and 63, high 1,528 and 56, peak 38 and 8; houseparty 51 to 889 pixels, drops 10 to 35. Rollerrink and dclive (largest drop 3, but for dclive's wide peak frame at 275 pixels and 6), dci (largest 11, on the near-black peak floor) and throneroom (none) show nothing I can see: the crowd's feet are behind rails, on tiers or out of frame in these two cameras, and throneroom has no crowd in them. *Sizes* (streetside, close-up low): 611 pixels and a drop of 43 at 640x360, 2,607 and 46 at 1280x720, 5,987 and 47 at 1920x1080 scale 2: one picture at three sizes, and the HUD labels and move cards are identical with it off and on. *Web* (the integration build, headless Chrome, a held streetside frame): wide 1,580 pixels and 61, close-up 2,802 and 44, against native's 1,593 and 63 and 2,607 and 46, with every same-setting repeat at 0. *By eye*: streetside's close-up gains a soft dark pool under each pair of sneakers and the crowd stands on the sidewalk; the toe caps darken slightly, which reads as occlusion; in the wide shot the camera is near grazing and each pool is a thin dark line under the feet; houseparty's dark wood shows it only in 2x crops; no hard edge, no quad hanging over a kerb, nothing on the dancers or the HUD. Where it does nothing it is invisible, not wrong. *Cost*: 1920x1080 scale 2, streetside, alternated windows: off 2.42 and 2.51 ms summed over all GPU passes, on 2.37 and 2.38, so below what the timers resolve. The pipeline is built at init with the other fixed-key pipelines (compiled on the first crowd frame it cost 4.5 ms). *Guards*: `ContactShadowGpuTest` (4: darkens the centre and leaves the rim and the alpha, writes no depth, a nearer surface stays clean, retail and the rb3 profile take none while the enhanced frame draws one), `CrowdTest` (shadows equal the 3D draws under enhanced, 0 under `DC3_LOOK=retail`), `FixedKeyPipelinesTest.CrowdContactShadowJoinsTheWarmForTheLookThatDrawsIt`. Seven sabotages, each caught: strength 0, the setting on for every profile, depth write on, depth compare Always, the `Crowd.cpp` call removed, the flush removed from `EndActivePass`, the warm removed. *Not checked*: a member standing on a step edge (none is in view in the two cameras of the six venues), so a pool hanging over a step is possible and unseen; impostor crowds (no shipped venue draws them). *Checked after landing (dc3 `6b81dfc3f`, engine `94b0139`)*: **M3 Air (Metal)**, headless, streetside confirmed by `{gamedata get venue}`, the wide shot held with `cheat_pause` at 1280x720: `DC3_CROWD_STATS shadows=36`; off against off and on against on 0 pixels; off against on 9,202 pixels changed, 1,364 by more than 8 levels, largest drop 50.5, no pixel brighter; no validation line. **Radeon 890M** (playtest-rig session; streetside high, 1920x1080 scale 2; limit stated first, +0.1 ms on the frame): wide shot (36 shadows) +0.023 ms (standard error 0.028); the close-up (76 shadows) had no clean launch, four rounds reading +0.032, +0.021, +0.080 and -0.088 ms. *Still owed by the rig*: scale 1, 1268x713 and the low state (the handheld went onto battery, where it is not timed). Evidence `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item32/` (`README.md`, `COORDINATOR.md`, `sheets/`, `sab_S7_warm.log`). |

| H-163 | **DONE 2026-11-21 (`np-look34`, items 34 and 34b; engine pin `b368031`): text in the enhanced look is sharper at 1080 rows and above. Glyph pages sample with a LOD bias of -1; the edge of a move name at 1920x1080 is 0.84 pixels wide where it was 1.87.** New work beyond the original; the retail look and the Native profile (rb3, rb3-xenon) sample text exactly as before. *The premise was wrong and measuring corrected it* (item 34): text was thought to be magnified and soft for that reason. It is minified at every size up to 2160 rows (the glyph pages are drawn for twice 720 rows: a move name covers 0.297, 0.442 and 0.594 output pixels per texel at 720, 1080 and 1440 rows). Text and HUD draws sample trilinear, with no anisotropy and no bias, from a sampler clamped to the first two mip levels (`LookSettings::uiMipLevels = 2`, the original's `min(NumMips()+1, 2)`), so at 1080 rows a glyph is read almost wholly from the half-size level and its edge is two pixels wide: the 10 to 90 % rise of the move name's "D" stem is 1.22 / 1.88 / 1.96 pixels at 720 / 1080 / 1440 rows. *What was built* (item 34b): `LookSettings::uiLodBias` beside `uiMipLevels`, carried in two spare slots of the scene block (the struct stays 800 bytes), read only by pipelines whose constant `kTakesUiBias` says so (`PipelineKey::uiBias`: 0 none, 1 glyph page, 2 other HUD art); at render scale above 1, where text samples with `textureSampleGrad` at output-pixel centres, the gradients are multiplied by 2^bias. Enhanced: -1 on glyph pages only, the two-level clamp kept. Tokens `{rnd native_look text_bias_off|text_bias_half|text_bias_on|text_bias_rule}`, `text_levels_2|text_levels_all|text_levels_rule`, `text_bias_glyphs|text_bias_all_ui|text_bias_scope_rule`; a bias token compiles nothing (a uniform), a level token makes new samplers as the anisotropy tokens do. **The one switch back in the enhanced look: `text_bias_off`.** *Measured on held frames* (streetside, omg, each rule switched by token on the same frame and taken twice; the repeats are identical): move-name stem rise 1.22 to 0.98 pixels at 1280x720 (-20 %), 1.87 to 0.84 at 1920x1080 (-55 %; 1.88 to 0.84 at render scale 2), 1.90 to 0.79 at 2560x1440 (-58 %), 1.57 to 1.43 at 3840x2160; stroke width at half height 3.20 to 3.25 pixels at 1080 rows, the undershoot beside the stem 0.003 of the step before and after (no rim), the drop shadow as dark as before; the help bar's text 1.59 to 0.88 at 1080 rows. At 640x360 and 960x540 the small text (move names, the menus) is the frame it was, bit for bit on a held frame, because the clamp makes the bias a no-op at that minification; the score's large digits, which are minified less, get a little crisper there (546 pixels changed, largest 16 levels, at 360 rows; 1,528 pixels, largest 92, at 540 rows; the coordinator's correction to the lane's "identical"). At 1268x713, the handheld's size, the held HUD frame changes in 4,031 pixels, all in the score and the two move names, and by eye the score's digits are plainly crisper and the move names slightly. In motion the song list's detrended frame-to-frame flicker fell (3.19 to 2.08 % at 1080 rows, 2.44 to 1.33 % at 1440) and no rule raised any burst above the same-rule repeat; at the two sizes under the logical 720 rows the rule's bursts read at or below today's (move column 0.05 to 0.12 % against 0.13 to 0.71 % at 1268x713 and 0.05 to 0.14 against 0.07 to 0.23 at 640x360; song list 3.07 against 3.82 and 4.38 against 4.42; help bar 0.16 against 0.34 and 0.19 against 0.44). By eye at 100 % and 2x the move name and the help bar are plainly crisper, with no stair-stepping and no rim. *What was tried and not adopted*: the bias on every HUD draw (the flashcards' fine line texture comes up as moire, edge energy +23 % at 720 rows, so glyph pages only); -1 with the clamp lifted (identical at 720 rows and above; at 360 rows it restores strokes the clamp drops, "EX T" reads "EXIT" again, but stroke contrast falls 155 to 127 and the move names' shadow lightens); no bias with the clamp lifted (worse everywhere: strokes vanish at 360 rows); -0.5 (1.87 to 1.39 at 1080 rows; kept as the fallback token `text_bias_half` if a playtest reports sparkle on scrolling text). Two code comments that forbade this were measured and rewritten, not deleted. *Cost*: no texture read is added (one add, or one `exp2` and a multiply, per text fragment). Tests `LookTextSamplingTest.*` (5, one on the GPU through the shipped `diffuseSample` with a three-level texture); seven sabotages, all red (one survived its first run and the test was tightened). A run started in the retail look and switched to enhanced by token keeps unbiased text (the pipelines' constant is decided at start), as the world LOD bias does. *Not checked*: HUD text caught mid-motion in play (the song list is the moving-text measure); the main menu; venues other than streetside; frames under 720 rows still drop thin strokes ("EXIT" at 640x360), which needs a size-dependent clamp of its own. | H-165 | **DONE 2026-11-21 (`np-look36`, item 36; engine pin `b368031`): a blurred figure in front of the dancers has a soft outline again in the enhanced look. It ships on a discrete GPU (the field and the spread); every other adapter (integrated, the web build) keeps the depth of field it had, pass for pass, until the handheld's timing is in.** *What was wrong* (found by the survey, H-164): H-151's depth-weighted blur weighs every tap by `f^2`, whichever side of the focal plane it is on. Behind the focus that is the fix it was built for (no halo of blurred backdrop around a sharp dancer). In front of the focus it is wrong: the blur at a near object's edge holds only the near object's colour, and the composite steps from that straight to the sharp figure behind, so an out-of-focus crowd member kept a cut-out outline harder than the original's (largest edge step 1.16 to 2.31 times the retail token's on the same held frames). *Change* (engine `DofPass`): `dof_near(z) = smoothstep(0.2, 0.6, -t)`, exactly 0 at and behind the focal plane, so the far field is H-151's to the pixel. **The field**: where the centre pixel is in the near field the colour is `mix(weighted blur, depth-blind blur, dof_near)`, so the in-focus figure shows through the near object's blurred edge as it does in the original. **The spread**: the composite's mix amount becomes `max(f, coverage)`, the coverage being the blurred near field, so the near object's blur also lies a few pixels over the sharp figure beside it, which a lens does and the original does not. With the near field on, the factor pre-pass is a second shader (`fs_dw_factor_half_near`) writing an RG16Float target (R `f^2`, G `dof_near`), the tent downsample and the two blurs each write a second quarter target (the depth-blind tent, coverage in alpha), and four fixed pipelines (`DofDepthWeightFactorHalfNear`, `DofDownsampleTentNear`, `DofBlurNear`, `DofCompositeNear`) are warmed with the others where the adapter's rule has the field on (and compiled once on first use where a token turns it on); the spread is a uniform that compiles nothing. **With the near field off the depth of field is the one before this row, pass for pass**: the original factor shader into the R16Float target, the same pipelines, formats and texture reads (the one difference is the uniform block, 32 bytes where it was 16), so an adapter that does not take the field pays nothing for it. `LookSettings::postDofNearField` and `postDofNearSpread`; retail and the Native profile (rb3, rb3-xenon) take neither, and `DofPass::Run`'s new arguments default off. **Both parts are decided by adapter class**, as supersampling is: `postDofNearField` and `postDofNearSpread` are the values on a discrete GPU (on), `postDofNearFieldIntegrated` and `postDofNearSpreadIntegrated` the values on anything else (integrated, CPU, unknown, the web build: off until a handheld timing says they fit). The class is `GpuDevice::IsDiscreteGpu()`, the adapter info's `adapterType == DiscreteGPU`; `LookDofNearField(look, discrete)` and `LookDofNearSpread(look, discrete)` pick, and the outcome is printed once when the device is up: `[look] adapter class: discrete GPU; depth of field near field 1, near spread 1 (profile enhanced)`. Each on and off token writes both classes' values, so it forces its part on any GPU, and each rule token returns each class to the profile's. No environment variable. Tokens `{rnd native_look dof_near_on|dof_near_off|dof_near_rule}` and `dof_near_spread_on|dof_near_spread_off|dof_near_spread_rule`. **The one switch back in the enhanced look: `dof_near_off`** (already the default on an adapter that is not discrete). *Measured on held frames* (the two parts forced by token; cu01 at song 20 s, each setting switched by token on the same frame and taken twice; largest step across the near silhouette as a ratio to the retail token; today / field / field and spread): streetside 1920x1080 scale 2, low state, 449 rows, 1.65 / 0.80 / 0.37; houseparty 1920x1080 scale 2, peak, 681 rows, 1.27 / 0.88 / 0.73; streetside 1268x713, 1.87 / 0.75 / 0.74; H-151's dclive frame at 1280x720, where the rows are a dancer's own hand only partly in the near field, 1.21 / 1.21 / 0.86 (the field alone does not soften that one; the spread does). The lane's tables at 640x360 and 1280x720 agree (field 0.85 to 1.04, field and spread 0.39 to 1.04). H-151's halo at dclive is 5.9 with the rule off, with the field and with the spread (27.1 with the depth weight off). Wide shots, and dci's close-ups with no near object, change within the same-setting repeat (at most 2 levels); no opaque HUD pixel changes. By eye at 100 % and 3x: the cut-out line is gone with the field and the edge looks like the retail token's; with the spread the figure reads as a properly defocused foreground object, with no stepping from the coverage read; at dclive the spread puts a faint haze from the forward hand over the watch strap behind it (largest change 126 levels at the hand's edge), which is the hand's defocus. *In motion* (H-167): each state is steady frame to frame, with and without the spread, and across nine camera cuts the near field settles on the first frame after the cut. *Cost* (3090, shared, +-0.05 ms; the depth of field's passes): 1920x1080 scale 1, 0.198 ms today, 0.198 with the field, 0.271 with the spread; scale 2, 0.598 / 0.640 / 0.865. Reads added, only with the field on: 8 per quarter texel in each blur; in the composite 4 inside the near field, and with the spread 1 at every pixel. *890M*: not timed yet (the handheld is on battery, where the rig session does not time), and until it is neither part runs there by default. The limits were stated first, on the frame total over `dof_near_off`: at most +0.25 ms at 1920x1080 scale 1 and +0.13 ms at 1268x713, which is what H-151's +0.5 / +0.25 rule has left after the depth weight's +0.248 / +0.121. If the field alone is within them `postDofNearFieldIntegrated` goes on; if the field and the spread together are, `postDofNearSpreadIntegrated` goes on too. *Tests*: `DC3LookPostTest.DofNearFieldSoftensANearObjectsEdgeAndLeavesTheFarField` (largest edge step in levels per pixel: retail 107, weighted 209, field 108, field and spread 13; the same scene mirrored behind the focus changes no pixel; no pipeline compiles after the warm), `DC3LookDofNear.FollowsTheLookAndItsSwitch` and `DC3LookDofNear.TheNearFieldFollowsTheAdapterClass`; the rendered test also checks that the factor target the device made is R16Float without the near field and RG16Float with it. Ten sabotages, all red: `dof_near` returning 0, `dof_near` on both sides of the focus, the spread ignoring its uniform, the warm skipping the near pipelines, the spread token writing the field, the spread's helper ignoring the class, the spread's on token writing the discrete value only, the off path writing the two-channel target, the field's helper ignoring the class, the field's on token writing the discrete value only. *Both classes drawn* (streetside low state, cu01 held, 1280x720): on the build machine's discrete GPU the rule's frame is the forced-on frame (92 pixels apart, none by more than 2 levels; the same setting twice is 7 to 95 pixels) and 42,284 pixels from the forced-off frame (16,066 by more than 8 levels); in the web build, which is not a discrete adapter, the rule's frame is the `dof_near_off` frame to the pixel (0 of 921,600, two takes of each), 24,698 pixels from the field's frame and 37,281 from the spread's, the page logs `[look] adapter class: not a discrete GPU; depth of field near field 0, near spread 0`, and the on tokens work there with no validation error. *Not checked*: the rule drawn on a native integrated adapter (the 890M and the M3, asked of the rig session; the one line no native test reaches is `PostProcPass::Run` passing the device's class); the edge between two blurred panels that are both behind the focus (dci, survey crop 01c; the far field's rule, untouched here); a steeper near ramp so the field alone softens a partly-near hand; songs other than omg. Evidence `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item36/` (`README.md`, `COORDINATOR.md`, tables, sheets, sabotage logs). *Checked after landing (dc3 `35949154d`, engine `eb31faa`) on the M3 Air (Metal), headless, 1280x720, throneroom held on `P1_area1_far01` with `cheat_pause`, by the landing session*: the class line reads `[look] adapter class: not a discrete GPU; depth of field near field 0, near spread 0 (profile enhanced)`, and `dof_near_off`, `dof_near_rule`, `dof_near_on` and `dof_near_on` with `dof_near_spread_on` are 0 pixels apart. So the rule is off on that adapter, as designed. That is **not** a test that the near field draws on Metal: a wide shot has no figure in front of the focus. *The near field on Metal* (same heads, same machine, by the landing session; item 36's close-up: streetside, low state, `cu01`, omg held at 20.28 s, no choreo performer): forced by token, off against on is 21,725 pixels (7,936 over 8 levels, largest 56) and off against on with the spread 32,569 (12,898 over 8, largest 121), the same counts in two takes, all in the lower part of the frame (y 435 to 719) on and around the figures in front; the rule against off 0 pixels; the same setting twice 333 pixels (a two-state flicker of the hold, in the same places for every setting). At 2x the edge of the figure in front is a hard cut with the field off, a soft outline with it on, and spread over the pavement with the spread; no artifact, no validation line. So the near field draws on Metal, and the Mac has nothing against the rule going on for adapters that are not discrete; its cost on the M3 was then measured (the field +0.03 ms at 1268x713, +0.05 to +0.06 at 1920x1080, +0.11 at 2560x1440; the field and the spread +0.04, +0.06 to +0.07, +0.12; all inside the limits) and H-178 turns both parts on for an Apple GPU. |

| H-168 | **DONE 2026-11-21 (`np-look39`, items 39 and 39b; engine pin `31d9220`): throneroom's smoke is a soft haze again, not flat translucent polygons. A faithfulness fix in both looks, with no switch: a particle system with `animate_uvs` off draws its whole texture on each quad, as the original does.** *What was wrong* (found by H-166): the engine's particle draw always gave a quad one tile of the system's tile sheet (`num_tiles_across` x `num_tiles_down`). The original does that only when `animate_uvs` is set: the shipped `particles` vertex shader branches on constant c51.x, which `DxParticleSys::DrawShowing` loads with `mAnimateUVs ? 1 : 0` (`src/system/rnddx9/Part.cpp`; target 0x82625690 read from the listing: `lbz` of the flag, 1.0 or 0.0 into four lanes, `SetVConstant` 0x33), and with it clear the quad's uv is the corner's own 0/1 and the tile counts are not read. The decompiled code was right; the native layer under it was not. Throneroom's smoke systems have `animate_uvs 0` and a 6 x 5 sheet on `smoke.tex` (256x256, 30 small puffs), so native drew one puff magnified over each quad. *Change*: engine `ParticleTileUV(tile, across, down, animateUVs)` (`Part_Wgpu`), chosen by `rndshape::PartAnimatesUVs(sys)`; in the DC3 shape that is detected through an `AnimateUVs()` accessor, which dc3 adds to `RndParticleSys` (`Part.h`, inside `HX_NATIVE`, no Xbox code change). rb3-xenon compiles the DC3 shape without the accessor and keeps the per-tile read (its retail `DrawShowing` loads neither c50 nor c51); rb3's shape reports one tile, the whole texture either way. **Which systems change** (item 39b, read from the data, then checked in the running game): all 328 particle systems in the 6,539 shipped `.milo_xbox` files (5,399 base, 1,140 DLC with none; two independent counts agree file by file). 13 have `animate_uvs 0` and more than one tile: throneroom 8 (`far_smoke`, `far_smoke1`, `far_smoke2`, `leftBuildings_smoke`, `rightBuildings_smoke`, `dust_gust`, `peak_smoke`, all 6 x 5, and `god_rays`, 4 x 4), houseparty 2 (`light_pop`, `light_pop1`, 4 x 4: a burst of streaks during the change to the peak state), streetside 1 (`peak_hydrant`, 2 x 2, which the original keeps hidden through low and peak on omg, and native too), and 2 in `world/shared/particle_systems/fire*.milo`, test scenes nothing loads. No HUD, menu or character system is among them (their 12 multi-tile systems all animate their uvs), dci, rollerrink and dclive have none, and nothing sets these fields at run time; the systems loaded in the six venues match the data's count (27, 15, 15, 18, 7, 3). *Against the original* (Xenia, shots matched by name, `DC3_LOOK=retail` at 1280x720 with the display curve off, every native frame twice with 0 pixels between the takes): **throneroom's far smoke** (low state, `P1_area1_far01`): the triangles and wedges with straight edges are gone and the haze between the columns is soft and mottled like the original's; held at the same song time (15.51 s) it reads as the original's, mean luma right of the left column 42.3 against 42.7 with the same spread (H-169). An earlier reading of "still lighter, with weaker grain" compared frames about 5 s apart in the song. **Houseparty's `light_pop`**: hard-edged flat rectangles before, tapered soft streaks after, the original's shape. **Streetside's `peak_hydrant`**, forced to show on both sides with the same commands: large angular shards before, small four-point sparkles after, the original's. In all three the frame after the fix is the closer one, and none got further (coordinator's review: the crops looked at side by side, `item39/COORDINATOR.md`). The original also applies the material's texture transform (c20, c21) to particle uvs and native's particle path does not: every particle material loaded in the six venues has an identity transform (170 of 170 rows), and so do the 13 systems' stored materials, so nothing draws differently for it. *Tests* (`native/tests/test_particle_color.cpp`): `ParticleColorTest.WholeTextureWithoutAnimateUVs`, `DC3ShapeReadsAnimateUVs`, `RenderedSheetWithoutAnimateUVsIsWhole` (a 2 x 2 sheet on one particle: four quadrants with the flag off, tile 0 alone with it on). Two sabotages, both red (the draw ignoring the flag: greens 40, 40, 40, 40 where 40, 100, 160, 220 are expected). The other venues' frames before and after differ by less than two launches of one binary do. *Not checked*: the HUD, menus and characters in the running game (the census there is from the data); the transforms of UI, HUD and character particle materials (none of those systems takes the new path); houseparty's first burst is cream on native and lavender-white on the original, in both binaries, so not this fix; throneroom's peak systems (`peak_smoke`, `god_rays`) were not compared with the original; `run_objdiff` was not run on `DxParticleSys::DrawShowing` (the lane's tree had drifted from its patched manifest; the listing was read instead). Evidence `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item39/` and `item39b/` (`README.md`, `COORDINATOR.md`, sheets, logs, tools). *Checked after landing (dc3 `35949154d`, engine `eb31faa`) on the M3 Air (Metal), headless, 1280x720, throneroom held on `P1_area1_far01` with `cheat_pause`, by the landing session*: the background reads as soft haze with no straight-edged polygon (there is no before and after on the Mac, because the fix has no switch); 0 validation lines in the run. |

The wrong reading had two causes: Xenia's red outline traces the far arm's dropped elbow, where native shows a dark sleeve that does not separate from the backdrop and a pink trail on the hand, so the two silhouettes read as two poses; and those Xenia frames were not paused and took their times from their files' write times. Coordinator looked at that pair (`item42/sheets/06_pose_matched_2640_…`): the three figures look the same on both sides. So never read a cell that holds a feedback overlay (the lead's outline, trail, ring, flames or text): those follow the rating, not the renderer. For the lead itself use a paused pair. *Not checked*: other venues, the enhanced look, the high and peak states, shots other than far01, texture formats and mip counts as uploaded; a close-up of the backups (their faces are about 15 pixels across here, and a 2 pixel shift moves such a patch by more than the few per cent in question); the boombox's body, which reads lighter and more lavender on native in a box that also holds its lit stars and the text; a paused pair at 16.89 and at 19.88 s (the lead's pose is settled at 18.1 s only); the native backups' driver layers; how far the backups' poses drift from run to run (no noise table). | H-170 | **MEASURED 2026-11-21 (item 41; nothing landed, branch `np-look41` kept): dclive's white logo wall is clipped by the grade, not by the scene target, and a highlight roll-off at the frame write does not make the picture better.** The survey (H-164) had found the wall behind the high-state close-up a flat white sheet in both looks and on hardware footage; the owner's direction allows the enhanced look to do better where that is a real improvement. *Where the range goes* (dclive, the high state forced, `cu01` held at 20 s of the song, 1920x1080 at render scale 2, non-HUD pixels; each step a token on one held frame, plus an uncommitted debug build that writes the composite at half scale so that values over white survive): the 8-bit scene target has 0.05 % at luma 250 or more (7.43 % have one channel at 255: saturated coloured lights, not white); the grade alone 3.50 %; the whole composite 8.50 %, with the largest channel at 1 or more on 22.5 % of pixels and at 1.25 or more on 5.0 %, none at 1.5 (99.9th percentile 1.35 to 1.40). So the bloom's screen blend (1.55) and the colour matrix lift a fifth of the frame over display white and the one saturate on the frame write flattens it. No target is too narrow. *What was built* (engine `0768fd8`, `42141e9`, dc3 `a6140a61a` on `np-look41`): a shoulder on the graded colour's largest channel inside the existing composite (the identity under 0.8, a cubic reaching white at 1.4, channel ratios kept), enhanced look only, tokens `highlight_on`, `highlight_off`, `highlight_rule`; three tests, green, and six sabotages, each red; no pass, target or texture read added; 0 changed pixels where the HUD is opaque at 1920x1080, 1280x720 and 640x360. *Why it does not land*: with the rule on, the share at luma 250 or more falls to 0.00 % in all 14 held moments (dclive, houseparty, dci, streetside), but the 99th percentile of the frame's luma falls from 254 to between 229 and 239 and nothing is at display white any more. The wall turns from a white-pink glow into a milky grey-pink and the logo is no more legible, because the bloom has already merged letters and wall into one glow on a background that the depth of field has blurred; in the far shot the sign's panel separates from its letters slightly less, not more. An offline check of five other curves on the unclamped frames (per channel, a higher knee, the part over white moved towards white) found none better: those that keep the peak cannot be told from today's clip at viewing size, those that keep the hue look milky. *What would change the picture*: the amount of bloom the grade adds over a bright blurred background. That is the original's art direction and would move every venue; not proposed. *Not explained*: at 1280x720 the rule moved 21,411 pixels under the knee on dclive's LED walls, 5,251 of them lighter, mostly by one level. A shoulder cannot lighten a pixel, so something drawn after the grade reads the frame; a held frame's off against on can therefore move pixels that a change to the composite's output did not touch. Evidence `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item41/` (`README.md`, `COORDINATOR.md`, 18 sheets, tests, tools). |

Pixels changed outside the score box: 0 on every frame that held still; pixels lighter there: 0. In a 24-frame burst the rim is on the new digits in the frame the score changes. *Cost*: no pass, target or copy; 0.06 to 0.44 ms more in the HUD pass at 1920x1080 scale 2 on the 3090 (a loaded box; the spread is not explained). *Coordinator's review* (`item44/COORDINATOR.md`): on lavender and on red the digits stand clear where they did not; at 1080 rows the outline is heavy; and in the low state, where the score already reads well on a dark backdrop, a near-black line now sits between each digit and its own glow (`street_720_mul_cu_low_score_3x.png`). That is the common case, and to my eye it is slightly worse with the rim. **Recommendation: not switched on as it is.** If outlined score digits are wanted, the next form darkens only where the backdrop is bright. Tokens `{rnd native_look text_backing_on|text_backing_off|text_backing_rule}`; `LookTextBackingTest` (4) green; 5 and then 3 sabotages, each red; rb3 and rb3-xenon run the Native profile, which refuses the token, and were not built. *Not checked*: a pixel test through the real draw path that a glyph's solid core is unchanged (the frames' core mask is derived from the change, so its 0 is circular; the draw order is the argument); strength 0.40; a fully black backdrop; the main menu, results and pause screens; the retail look with the token; the integrated GPU; the web build; other modes' HUDs; whether anything reads the frame's alpha after the HUD. Evidence `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item44/` (`README.md`, `COORDINATOR.md`, sheets, tools, sabotage logs). |

| H-175 | **DONE 2026-11-21 (item 47 step 1, `np-look47-s1`; step 2 is H-176): in the enhanced look on a discrete GPU a character's projection texture is drawn with the frame's 4x MSAA, so the Break It Down dancer's outline and the pose-off silhouettes are smooth.** The fault is H-173's finding 2: the dancer is drawn through `projection.tex` / `projectionp2.tex` (`world/shared/chars_base.milo`, `projection.rndtex` / `projectionp2.rndtex`; 1024x1024, no mips) in a texture pass with one sample, so her outline steps one output pixel at a time at every window size. *The change* (engine `0585758`, 4 commits on `eb31faa`; dc3 `a8f20dabb`, 4 commits on `9fc2e066b`): `LookSettings::texPassMsaa` (on in the enhanced profile for discrete adapters; `texPassMsaaIntegrated` stays off until the 890M is timed); a texture pass the rule names is drawn into a 4-sample colour and depth pair and resolved into the texture; `{rnd native_look rtt_msaa_on}`, `rtt_msaa_off`, `rtt_msaa_rule`; the warm list holds a character's texture-pass pipelines at both sample counts. The engine's `CreateRenderTarget` and `CreateDepthTarget` gained a last, defaulted `samples` parameter (no caller in rb3 `ee7c91f84` or rb3-xenon `963239e94`). *The rule*: a texture pass is drawn like the frame when its target is `kRendered` with no mips (the engine's half) and the predicate the consumer registered says yes. dc3's predicate (`native/src/platform/TexPassRule.cpp`): the target is the `output_texture` of at least one `RndTexRenderer`, and every such renderer draws a `Character` and has `force_mips` false. A census of the shipped files (9 renderer objects in 8 base files, 7 distinct, none in the DLC, none made in code) has it reach the two projections and nothing else: not `keep_me.tex` (the photo scene's renderer draws a Group, and the stream recorder reads the texture), not the camera snapshots, `iconman.rndtex` (`force_mips`), the flashcard renderers, the endgame `render.rndtex` or `face_baker`. With no predicate registered nothing is reached, so rb3 and rb3-xenon cannot change and need no source change. The predicate is asked only where a pass that clears the target begins, and the answer is kept with the target: the Flows in `chars_base` clear and set the renderer's `output_texture` at run time, and a sampling call must not drop a 32 MiB pair and make it again. Round 1's rule (the engine's half alone) had reached `keep_me.tex`, 2 passes at 4 samples; found in review. *Measured* (1280x720, enhanced, RTX 3090; the final-rule binary, xxh3 `0cef6a30ec9ccad6`): Break It Down held at 22 s with the token flipped in the held frame, 5,554 pixels change by more than 8 levels, all in the dancer's box, and the held frame shot twice is 0. Round 1's binary, the same pass: 5,452 pixels at 32 s, 5,356 and 5,410 at 1268x713, 12,675 and 12,164 at 1920x1080; one row of red across her thigh edge at 52 s reads main `0 0 7 150 156`, off `0 0 7 150 156`, on `0 0 3 122 156`, a real retail launch `0 0 7 150 157`; of the pixels changed at 22 s, 47 leave the range of the off frame's 3x3 neighbourhood by more than 8 levels and none by more than 32, so no dark or light line is drawn round her. Pass lines of the final-rule binary: Break It Down, 1,802 `projection.tex` passes at 4 samples and both `keep_me.tex` passes at 1 sample, 640x480; Strike a Pose (both projections are drawn all through the mode), 10,842 and 10,479 passes at 1 sample with step 1 off, 9,499 and 8,338 at 4 samples with it on, `keep_me.tex` 2 passes at 1 sample in both; H-174's pose-off silhouettes are stepped off and smooth on for both players. The retail look and the Native profile keep one sample pass for pass (`RetailAndNativeKeepOneSample`; the retail launch's frames are stepped as before). *The trade, accepted by the landing session against the outline*: inside the figure at 720 rows step 1 softens geometry edges a little: mean absolute Laplacian 16.49 to 14.79 (10 % lower), mean gradient 11.50 to 11.07 (4 %), a belt edge 2.84 / 2.88 px to 3.23 / 3.19 px (10 to 90 % width). That her face and outfit are softer than the same character's in the main frame is not step 1; it is the same with step 1 off (step 2 below). *Cost* (3090): the pass goes from 0.042 to 0.066 ms at 1268x713 and 1280x720 (+0.024, +0.025), +0.025 at 1920x1080 and +0.029 at render scale 2; memory 8 to 36 MiB a projection, two projections in a two-player mode. No pipeline is compiled after the first projection pass; the first frame with the dancer costs 15.7 ms of CPU against 14.0 ms on main in one pair of launches (round 2's step 1 binary read 14.7). The 890M is not timed: from the 3090 figure it is estimated at +0.19 to +0.36 ms against the landing session's limit of +0.15 ms at 1268x713, so step 1 is off on non-discrete adapters; the tokens time off and on in one launch there. **Timed on the owner's M3 MacBook Air (Metal) by the landing session, 2026-11-21** (dc3 `2bd6355c1`, engine `31f2631`; `bid_timing.sh` as written, the frame held, unpaced, 8 alternating states of 6 one-second windows; limits stated first, +0.15 ms at 713 rows and +0.30 ms at the other two; on minus off): frame span +0.085 ms at 2560x1440 (15.054 to 15.139), +0.091 at 1920x1080 (5.377 to 5.469), +0.096 at 1268x713 (2.704 to 2.800); the pass 0.275 to 0.361, 0.265 to 0.367 and 0.257 to 0.348 ms; the two off frames byte-identical, the two on frames byte-identical, 22,003 pixels over 8 levels between them, all in the dancer's box, no line round her; 0 validation lines. Under the limit at all three sizes. On that tile-based GPU `gpu_sum_ms` is not a frame total (pass spans overlap) and a paced launch with the song running drifts by 0.6 ms between states of one setting; the figures are `gpu_span_ms`. `texPassMsaaIntegrated` is one boolean for the Mac and the 890M, and the 890M is untimed, so step 1 stayed off on both until H-178 gave the look an adapter class for Apple GPUs, where it is on. `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item47/timing/mac_m3/README.md`. *Tests* (`native/tests/test_look_texpass.cpp`, 14): the predicate dc3 registered, read back from the engine, says no for a target no renderer draws into, for a Group's renderer and for a second texture that renderer is pointed at, for a Character's renderer with `force_mips`, for two renderers of which one is a Group's and for a renderer whose output was cleared, and yes for a Character's; `AugmentedPhotoKeepMeIsNot` loads the shipped `ui/augmented_photo.milo` and asserts no for `keep_me.tex` and for a snapshot-shaped texture set as that renderer's output (the test the landing session asked for by name); with no predicate nothing is drawn like the frame; an answer is kept across sampling calls until the next clearing pass. Shown red once each: the predicate saying yes for any renderer (3 tests), ignoring `force_mips` (1), `EnsureRenderTargetData` asking the rule again (1). *Checks on engine `0585758`*: dc3 full gate (`scripts/native_test.sh`) on `a8f20dabb`: 1,774 registered, 1,704 run, 1,704 passed, 0 failed, 70 skipped (budget 70). Web build (`scripts/web/build.sh --release`) rc 0; in headless Chrome on that build Break It Down with `rtt_msaa_on` draws the dancer with a smooth outline and with `rtt_msaa_off` a stepped one, 0 page errors in four runs (the browser's adapter is not discrete, so the rule leaves step 1 off on the web and only the token reaches the 4-sample pass and its resolve there). rb3 `ee7c91f84`: ctest 165 of 165 pass (the same 7 skips), the flag census check clean. rb3-xenon `963239e94`: rb3-render 11 of 11 frames identical between `eb31faa` and `0585758` (two runs each, 414 PASS each), health selftest `verdict=PASS`, `gates_pass=513 gates_fail=0`. **Step 2 (landed as H-176; this block is as written before it landed)**: at 720 rows the 1024 target has about one texel per screen pixel and is resampled bilinearly, so the Break It Down dancer is softer than the same character in the main frame, with step 1 off as well as on (her eyes are smudges; the belt buckle and the weave of the shorts are gone). The same held frame with a 1024, a 1536 and a 2048 target at 720 rows: mean absolute Laplacian inside the figure 15.37, 22.04, 25.97; 1536 brings back the lashes and the buckle, 2048 the weave, about what the main frame draws. The size rule the landing session decided on: the target's side times the output's rows over 360, the longer side at most 2048 (a 1024 target is 2048 from 720 rows up, 1536 at 540, 1024 at 360 and below; one constant, `texPassBaseRows`); the rule as first built divided by 720 (1024 at 720 rows, one texel a pixel) and so kept the softness. In motion at 720 rows (160 consecutive frames from each of three launches, 131 at the same song times): 18 % more detail at 2048 and 12 % more frame-to-frame change; in the slowest quarter of frame pairs, where aliasing would show, change per unit of detail is 1.4 % higher over head and torso and 6.9 % higher on the striped top (3.5 % at 1536); no pixel-sized flashes in the difference maps; the collar's white piping reads slightly beaded at 2048 and 1536 in stills (a one-pixel line minified about 2 to 1). **1536 at 720 rows (`texPassBaseRows` 480) is the fallback if crawl on thin lines is seen in play.** Memory at 2048 with 4 samples: 144 MiB a projection (36 at 1024), two in a two-player mode. Discrete adapters only until the rigs session has timed it. Not checked: an `iconman.rndtex` pass in the running game (no route found in the budget; the rule turns it away by `force_mips`, tested on a made renderer, not the shipped one); the 890M; the original (the Xbox 360 footage of the mode is 480 rows). | H-179 | **DONE 2026-11-22 (`np-flow-queue-order`): native runs the flow queue in the order its nodes were created instead of by address, so Break It Down always has its tunnel. On the M3 Air it was missing for the whole launch in about half the launches.** *The cause*: `FlowManager::mFlowQueue` is `std::map<FlowNode *, QueueState>` and `Poll` runs it in the map's order, by address. One poll of a Break It Down launch holds both the venue's `start.flow` (`world/bid/bid_geom.milo`, which reaches `show_timeywimey.flow`) and `reset.flow` (`world/shared/chars_base.milo`, which runs `hide_timeywimey.flow`); when `start.flow` has the lower address, reset runs second and hides the tunnel's six drawables. On the console the nodes come from `FixedSizeAlloc::Refill` (`src/system/utl/PoolAlloc.cpp`), whose free list ascends, so address order there is close to creation order; a host allocator's differs each launch. *The change* (dc3 `8b72d12ad`, merged as `3bc33a12d`; all under `HX_NATIVE`; `src/system/flow/FlowManager.{h,cpp}`, `FlowNode.{h,cpp}`): `FlowNode` counts constructions (`CreateOrder()`) and the queue's key is `FlowQueueKey`, ordered by that number. `QueueCommand`, `CancelCommand` and the body of `Poll`'s loop are the console's text. *Measured* with an experiment build that can run the queue either way and trace each poll. M3 Air, 1268x713, held on the recap, the two orders interleaved: by address the tunnel is drawn in 3 of 8 launches (`start.flow` ran first in the 5 without), by creation number in 12 of 12 (`reset.flow` at position 64 and `start.flow` at 135 of that poll every time; creation numbers 3250 and 12861 on both machines). RTX 3090: by address 2 of 2, by creation 5 of 5, by reversed creation 0 of 3, by queueing order with only that pair swapped 0 of 3. On the 3090 creation order differs from a launch's address order in about 6% of the pairs that share a poll (queueing order in about 35%), and 11% of such pairs change order from launch to launch by address. *Tests*: `native/tests/test_flow_queue_order.cpp` (4; the nodes are placed in one buffer so address, creation and queueing order are three different orders; 3 fail on unchanged code, `RunsInCreationOrder` gave 1,2,0). Console side: 0 of 48,289 functions moved. | H-180 | **DONE 2026-11-22 (item 51, `np-look51`): in the enhanced look, a pixel on the outline between something in focus and something blurred is composited per sample, so the outline is as smooth as the scene's own 4x anti-aliasing instead of stepped. On a discrete GPU only: timed on the M3 it is over its limit at every size (addendum at the end of this row), and the 890M is not timed; the retail look is not touched.** *The cause*: the enhanced depth of field's three composites (H-151's depth-weighted one, H-165's near-field one, and the plain one behind `dof_depth_weight_off`) read the mix amount from depth sample 0 of the 4x depth buffer and applied it to the resolved scene colour. A pixel half covered by a dancer was therefore kept whole (sample 0 on the dancer: the figure's share plus the SHARP background's share) or blurred whole (sample 0 on the background: the figure's share lost), pixel by pixel along the outline. That is the stepped outline round the dancers and the thin dark dotted line inside streetside's soft foreground band (the landing session saw the same line on the M3). *A first rule, rejected*: the smallest mix amount of the pixel's depth samples (engine `92afe2a`) keeps every half-covered pixel whole, sharp background share included, so a bright background detail shows as a one-pixel rim round the figure. On the held dci `cu01` frame at 1268x713 a backup dancer's fingertip over a pillar's white stripe, pixels (914..916, 189), reads 232 to 254 with no depth of field, 170 to 186 with the rule off, 217 to 237 with the first rule and 171 to 199 with the rule that landed. *The rule* (engine `a3cadd1`, `46b5f12`, `f46b629`): where the depth samples of a pixel give mix amounts more than 1/1024 apart, the composite reads the main pass's 4x colour samples and applies the depth of field to each one: `out = scene + (1/N) * sum_i a_i * (rgb_i - col_i)`, with `col_i` sample i of `MSAA4xColor`, `a_i` that sample's own mix amount (`max(f_i, coverage)` in the near-field composite) and `rgb_i` what the depth of field makes of that sample (`mix(col_i, weighted blur, w)`, then the near blur where the sample is in the near field). A half-covered pixel becomes the figure's share plus the BLURRED background's share. Where the samples agree the pixel runs the code it ran and loads no colour sample, so the picture away from depth edges is bit-identical. Three more fragment entry points (`fs_composite_bspline_dw_edge`, `..._dwn_edge`, `..._edge`) and two bind group layouts with the 4x colour at binding 10, compiled the first time the rule is on. `MSAA4xColor` gains `TextureBinding` usage (`Rnd_Wgpu.cpp`); `MsaaSampleView()` hands its view to `PostProcPass::Run` and `DofPass::Run`, and with no view or the rule off the pipelines of H-151 and H-165 run unchanged. **No pass's load or store op changed**: 6 passes a frame end on the 4x colour with store and resolve (`MainPass` and 5 `MainPassResume`), before and after, and all 19 pass names have the same ops (`MILO_GPU_PASS_TRACE=30`, 211 frames each, `item51/build/r2/analysis/gpupass_compare.txt`). A steep out-of-focus surface whose samples are more than 1/1024 apart takes the per-sample path too (the dclive floor grid changes by at most 3 levels), which is extra colour loads with no outline to fix: the cost depends on the frame. *Per class*: `LookSettings::postDofEdge` / `postDofEdgeApple` / `postDofEdgeIntegrated`, picked by `LookDofEdge(look, class)` (H-178's classes). Enhanced: on for a discrete GPU, off for an Apple GPU (`kAppleDofEdge = false`, beside H-178's six constants) and for any other non-discrete adapter; retail and Native off. `{rnd native_look dof_edge_on}`, `dof_edge_off`, `dof_edge_rule` (dc3 `20c7320f9`, `c455171b3`, `e3f5f0908`; `SetLookDofEdgeForInspection`, false under Native; on and off write all three classes) print `[look] depth of field edge rule %d, %d on a non-discrete GPU, %d on an Apple GPU (inspection, profile %s)`, with `(the rule)` after the third number for `dof_edge_rule`. **The startup class line and the forced-class line of H-178 gain `, edge %d` after `near spread %d`.** *Limits for the two rigs, stated before any run* (frame GPU time on a held close-up, each on state between two off states): the edge rule alone over `dof_edge_off`, +0.10 ms at 1268x713, +0.20 at 1920x1080, +0.35 at 2560x1440; and the depth of field's additions on top of H-151's depth weight (near field, spread and the edge rule together) within H-165's +0.13 / +0.25 / +0.45 on any adapter that is not discrete, which on the M3 leaves the edge rule +0.09 / +0.18 / +0.33. **Timed on the M3 on 2026-11-22: over the limit at every size, so the Apple class stays off (addendum at the end of this row). health 513 gates pass, 0 fail). | H-182 | **MEASURED, NO CHANGE 2026-11-22 (item 54, no code): the mip levels the original never displayed are the same pictures as the ones it did, in every shipped texture, so the enhanced look's whole authored chain shows nothing stale, different or shifted.** *Why asked*: the original uploads at most two levels of a regular texture (`DxTex::SyncBitmap`, `min(NumMips()+1, 2)`); the enhanced look uploads the whole authored chain (`bitmapMipLevels` 0), so every level from the third down is a picture nobody saw on the console. Three surveys by eye (H-164, H-173, H-181) found nothing; this is the exhaustive check. *Census*: all 6,539 `.milo_xbox` files of the disc and the DLC, 0 errors; 58,406 `Tex` entries, 58,062 parsed (the other 344 are 0x0 textures with no picture); 57,939 are `kRegular`, all with a cached bitmap, every parse ending exactly at its block end; 55,078 have three or more levels (14,427 distinct pictures) and all were decoded (DXT5 51,051, DXT1 3,500, DXN 527). *Each level from the third down against level 1 reduced to its size*: the RMS difference divided by the level's own contrast has median 0.111, 99.9th percentile 0.469 and largest 0.716 for colour textures (0.793 for normal maps), where a level of another texture swapped in on purpose scores 1.38 and a level shifted by half its width 1.61; level 1 against level 0, which the original did display, reaches 0.623 by the same measure. Mean brightness in code values: median ratio 0.999, 1st percentile 0.924 (0.962 over textures of at least 16 code values); the mean drifts down with depth, 0.16 code values at the third level and 1.26 at the seventh; no channel's mean is more than 8 code values from level 1 in any texture (230 objects, 77 distinct, are more than 4 away), and no alpha mean is more than 8 away. The authoring tool averaged code values (the better fit for 49,453 textures; linear light for 10), which is the space DC3 shades in. A normal map's lower levels are level 1's normals averaged and renormalised (within 2.4 code values RMS), not shortened. Every chain of three or more levels stops at a smallest side of 16, none reaches 1x1, and no level is other than half the one above. *The darkest lower levels*: the robot dancers' face-screen pictures (`robota01`, `robotb01`, `tanrobot01` visemes: 0.876 to 0.90 of level 1 at 16x16, the same face flattened inside each 4x4 block), then the 16x16 level of some dark character maps (0.90 to 0.94, about 1 to 6 code values on textures of 20 to 41). Coordinator looked at the robot face sheet and the darkest trouser map: the same pictures. *H-140 settled*: its 0.896 is a sharpness ratio (mean |Laplacian| against a retail launch, `item20/a/README.md`), not a brightness; the boombox's lower levels (`boomy_main` and its maps) are within 1.2 % of level 1 in code values and as sharp as a box reduction of it (one map, `eq_array`, is softer), so the enhanced look's lower reading there is correctly filtered smaller levels in place of the two-level cap's aliasing, and no bias is added (H-149). *Not checked*: which material binds each texture and how far away it is drawn in play (so whether a given level is ever sampled); whether each `ui/` texture draws through the two-level text and HUD sampler; cube maps and render targets; the lane's decoder against a GPU's (comparisons between levels do not depend on it); a retail launch against an enhanced launch on one held frame. Evidence `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item54/` (`README.md`, `analyze_output.md`, `outliers.md`, `boombox.txt`, `textures.tsv`, 12 sheets, tools, the two instrument controls in `selftest.txt`). |
