The browser does give strings: `GPUAdapterInfo.vendor` is `nvidia` there (architecture `ampere`). *The rule, web build only* (`ClassifyWebLookAdapter`, engine `src/platform/LookProfile.cpp`; the facts are filled in `GpuDevice_Web.cpp` once the adapter is acquired): vendor exactly `nvidia` on an adapter that is not a fallback adapter is discrete; vendor `apple` with `texture-compression-bc` (an Apple silicon Mac; an iPhone or iPad has no BC), not a fallback, is Apple only while the new constant `kAppleInBrowser` is true, and it is **false**; everything else (`amd`, `intel`, `arm`, `qualcomm`, an empty string, any fallback adapter, and `NVIDIA` in capitals, which is not the specification's normalised identifier) is other non-discrete, as before. An AMD or Intel string does not tell a desktop card from a handheld's integrated GPU, so those stay off. Natively nothing changes: the three-fact rule and the class line's text are byte for byte what they were. **Frame sizing is not touched**: it reads `IsDiscreteGpu()`, which stays false in a browser, so the web still draws at render scale 1 with the canvas capped at 1080 rows, and the two frame lines are the same before and after. No new environment variable or token, and no per-class value changed. The class line in the browser now ends `[browser vendor "nvidia", architecture "ampere", texture-compression-bc 1, fallback 0, backend WebGPU]`. *The picture* (headless Chrome 148, RTX 3090, 1280x720 page shots, one page load each; coordinator looked at the sheets): perform song at streetside, low state, `cu01` held: every repeat of a setting is 0 pixels, and each switch between the rule and `adapter_other` (the old picture) moves the same 57,545 pixels, 16,293 by more than 8 levels, largest 132. The blurred crowd figure in the foreground loses its hard cut-out edge against the in-focus dancers and is soft all the way out, and the dancers' outlines over the blurred background are smoother. Break It Down (song running, so a look, not a count): the dancer's outline in the projection is smooth under the rule and steps one projection texel at a time under `adapter_other`. A whole song to the results screen (dclive, easy), three runs: 0 page errors, 0 WebGPU error lines, and 0 pipelines created after the song starts (H-104's check; the warm covers the class's pipelines). *Cost in the browser*: the whole song seven times at 60 a second (p50 16.6 ms in all): frames over 20 ms 330, 302 and 372 under the rule against 165, 408 and 535 under `adapter_other`, and the engine's main-thread frame time 5.27, 5.65 and 5.38 ms against 4.80, 5.80 and 6.14, on a box whose load average was 16 to 25 of 32 cores, so one setting's spread contains the other's (the lane's first three runs alone read as a cost; four more by the coordinator did not). One page load on the held frame, the class switched other, rule, other, rule, other, rule, other, 8 s a state: the rule against the mean of the other either side reads +0.36, -0.99 and -0.93 ms of main-thread frame time, and the game-frame interval is 16.66 to 16.67 ms at p50 and 16.78 to 16.89 at p95 in every state. **No frame-time cost is measurable in the browser on the 3090.** The GPU's own time cannot be read from a page; natively on the 3090 the near field and spread cost +0.07 ms at 1920x1080 (H-165) and the projection's two steps +0.065 ms (H-176). GPU texture memory (`&gpu_profile=record`): a perform song at its results screen holds 176,101,520 bytes under the rule against 175,179,664 under `adapter_other` (+0.9 MiB); **Break It Down holds 309,469,328 against 141,697,168 bytes, +160.0 MiB**, the projection drawn at 2048 with 4 samples as on a native discrete GPU (H-176). That is accepted: natively the same card is discrete too and by default also supersamples 2x2, so the browser is the lighter of its two cases; if it proves too much on a small card (a 2 GB laptop GeForce was not tried), the size rule can be kept off on the web alone. *The Apple class in a browser* (the landing session's run, no code): headless Chrome 157 on the M3 MacBook Air, the web snapshot of dc3 `41cbd92b8` / engine `1adba5c` with the class forced by token. The adapter reports vendor `apple`, architecture `metal-3`, with `texture-compression-bc`. `adapter_apple` draws correctly on Metal: the perform song held (streetside, low, `cu01`) differs from `adapter_other` in 12,228 pixels by more than 8 levels (largest 76), all on the foreground crowd figure, which goes from a hard stepped outline to a soft blur (the same state twice: 1 and 0 pixels); Break It Down held at 66.95 s differs in 6,472 (largest 205), all inside the dancer's box, a stepped outline and blocky hair becoming a smooth outline and sharper hair (1 and 0); 0 GPU error lines and 0 page errors in every run. A collection forced 45 s after the last results screen found 0.1 and 0.0 MiB more. **Chrome's GPU process** (nvidia-smi, per process, once a second) rose to a plateau and stayed: 251 MiB at the first song's start in both runs, 293 at the second, 306 and 298 at the third, 305 and 297 at the end; after boot it never fell more than 8 MiB below its own peak, the forced collection included. The collected textures are not handed back to the driver, they are used again: the venue change made 28 to 29 MiB of new textures and the process grew 14 and 6 MiB. (Read as small textures sharing larger allocations inside Chrome; Dawn's allocator was not read.) H-184's 200 MiB steps were the 2048-pixel 4-sample projection targets, large enough to come and go as allocations of their own, and neither of these venues makes one. The page profile (`dc3GpuProfile.texBytes`) counts creations only: 234.3 and 233.5 MiB in 727 and 725 textures by the end, against about 140 MiB created and not yet collected. The coordinator's estimate in the lane's brief, 150 to 300 MB released at a song change, was wrong by about ten times: a song's own textures are 23 to 45 MiB here (BC-compressed, two levels, identical bitmaps shared). Coordinator recomputed the collections and the process's falls from the raw samples. *The census* (engine `src`, reading only, 22 release points): everything a song change releases goes through one place, `CleanupGpuTex` (`platform/Tex_Wgpu.cpp`), called from `RndTex::~RndTex`; the canvas-sized targets (frame depth, the 4-sample frame colour, bloom, depth of field, the retail post sets, spot beams) are released only at a canvas resize or a look token, most of them while the frame's encoder is open, so a destroy there would have to wait in a queue until one more frame is submitted (retail queued its releases too: `DxRnd::AutoRelease`, retired after `Present`); the canvas's own texture must never be destroyed. *A window resize, measured by the coordinator* (one run, the same snapshot, Break It Down running under `adapter_other`; in the enhanced look the canvas buffer follows the window, a new box taken once it has held 250 ms, capped at 1080 rows): the viewport switched between 1920x1080 and 1280x720 eight times, 4 s apart. Chrome's GPU process, flat at 213 to 215 MiB for the 15 s before, went 367, 402, 549, 596, 754, 787, 940, 977 MiB: about 147 MiB more at each resize to 1080 rows and about 40 at each to 720, every earlier set still held. One collection 20 s after the last resize, with nothing sent, took it from 977 to 296 MiB in one step. 0 GPU error lines, 0 page errors. So a fullscreen toggle or a window drag in play leaves each old set of canvas-sized targets on the GPU until the next collection: 764 MiB above the baseline here at the worst. **Decision: not built.** A song change, the only release every session has, does not hold two songs; a resize does hold the old targets, for seconds, and on the cards measured that is waste, not a fault; and a destroy queue adds a way to draw with a dead texture (a validation error and a dropped frame). Read from the code, not yet seen in a frame. *Not checked*: a song change into or out of Break It Down on a discrete-class browser (the one case in play that releases the large projection targets, H-184's 160 MiB); which textures were collected (the events carry bytes only); when the engine dropped each texture (so "after gameplay ended" is an upper bound on how long any was held); a run alone on the box or a third run; more than three songs; any GPU but the 3090, any phone, any browser but headless Chromium. The leak was 4, 2 and 4 textures a song, which is each venue's cube maps: rollerrink uploads four (`CubeTex.cube` twice, `DiscoBalls.cube`, `RRcube.cube`; 64-pixel faces with three levels, `RRcube` 128 with four) and dci two (`Glass.cube`, `DCIcube.cube`), ten uploads at ten addresses. In bytes that is small: at most about 0.9 MiB a song at rollerrink and 0.25 MiB at dci if the faces are uncompressed (computed from the face sizes, not measured; the engine's byte counter does not count cube maps). **Failure (2), the previous song's reflections on a new cube map, was not seen in play**: in the diagnostic run no upload landed on an address a dead cube map had held; it is shown in the unit test only. Coordinator recomputed the song-select counts from the raw logs. *Tests* (`native/tests/test_cube_tex_release.cpp`, a real device): `CubeTexReleaseTest.DeleteReleasesTheGpuCopy` (the texture and holder counts return and no entry is left for the dead address), `.RecycledAddressUploadsItsOwnFaces` (a second cube map built by placement new in the first one's storage, so the address is shared by construction and not by the allocator's choice; told apart by level count, 3 for 64-pixel faces and 1 for 16-pixel, and by view identity while the first view is held), `.FacesChangedAfterTheFirstDrawUploadAgain` (one face resized leaves no GPU copy; six resized upload at the new size; one face's pixels changed gives a new view). All 3 red before the fix, at 12 assertions. Five sabotages, each red: the destructor hook removed (3 of 3 tests), the cleanup erasing nothing (3 of 3), both `Update()` calls removed (1), only the `Sync()` branch's call (1), only the `Reset()` branch's call (1; this one passed every test until the one-face-resized check was added). *Consumers*: rb3 and rb3-xenon draw through `Rnd_Wgpu_RB3.cpp`, which has no cube map table, and neither calls the new function: nothing for them to adopt. | H-190 | **DONE 2026-11-22 (`np-present60`, item 60; engine `0531485`, `60615c9`; dc3 `5ce84ea32`, `fc9aa891b`): a frame drawn smaller than the rectangle it is presented in can be stretched with a clamped Catmull-Rom cubic instead of one bilinear tap. Enhanced turns it on for a discrete GPU only; an Apple GPU and anything else keep the one tap until each is timed, so no machine's picture changes today.** *Why*: a row cap (a non-discrete GPU's 1080 rows in a larger window) or a thermal step leaves the present pass to stretch the frame by a factor that is not a whole number, and one bilinear tap is the softest way to do that. *Whom it reaches*: neither rig today. The handheld presents 1:1 (a 1268x713 frame in a tiled 1268x750 XWayland window that Hyprland doubles with nearest neighbour, H-189) and the owner's Mac draws its surface's pixel size with the cap off; it will reach the handheld once its window is a Wayland one (a 1920x1080 frame into about 2536x1426, 1.32) and the Mac under a thermal step (1440 to 1080 rows, 1.33). *The filter* (engine `gfx/PresentScale`, `SetUpscaleFilter`): the Catmull-Rom cubic's 4x4 footprint read with 5 bilinear taps (the four corner taps dropped, the rest renormalised), then clamped to the smallest and largest of the four nearest texels (3 gathers), so its negative lobes cannot overshoot beside a glyph's stem. Only when the frame is smaller than its rectangle on both axes; never at a scale of 1, on a downscale (the box filter), on the alpha-keeping copy, or at a whole-number scale, which stays nearest (at 2.0 nearest reads 0.83 panel px on text against 2.08 for the cubic forced). The pipeline is made on first use. *Measured, all a simulation of the panel on the RTX 3090* (enhanced look, `adapter_other`, streetside; the stretch done offline through the engine's own `PresentScale::Encode`): synthetic slanted edges at 1.32, 10 to 90 % rise in panel px: 1.73 to 1.49 on a hard edge and 2.21 to 1.93 on a soft one, overshoot 0.000 (0.028 unclamped); at 1.585 (a 1600x900 step) 2.08 to 1.79. Text in game frames, one tap / cubic / drawn at the panel's size: FRIENDS' SCORES 1.87 / 1.59 / 1.45, the song row BETTER OFF ALONE 1.71 / 1.58 / 1.39, HUD score 2.33 / 1.91 / 1.55; thin strokes keep more of their level (SORT BY 143 to 164, 171 drawn at the panel's size). On the 900-row step the cubic reads about like the 1080-row frame with one tap. Pixels changed against one tap on the same 1080-row frame, of 3.6 million, over 8 levels: 34,684 (HUD), 30,595 (close-up), 32,201 (song select); the no-change control 0. The 9-tap form is within 0.04 px of the 5-tap one everywhere. *Today's handheld picture against a stretched 1080-row frame*: the doubled 713 rows are hard 2x2 blocks (0.8 px by the same measure, stepped); the 1080-row frame has no steps and 2.3 times the detail with softer edges: better on hair and outlines, a trade on small text, and 2.3 times the pixels for the 890M. *Cost*: the pass alone on the RTX 3090, 1920x1080 into 2560x1440, CPU-timed: one tap 0.036 ms, the clamped 5-tap cubic 0.073. **No 890M or M3 number.** The limit stated before the lane ran is 0.25 ms over the one tap on the 890M; the lane's only projection is 0.3 ms (an estimate, the 3090's ratio applied to an estimated 0.3 ms tap), over it. Hence the rule by adapter class (`presentUpscale`, `...Apple`, `...Integrated`; `LookPresentUpscale` picks for `GpuDevice::LookClass()`, as the depth of field's edge rule does): enhanced on / off / off, retail and Native off. On a discrete GPU the enhanced look draws at render scale 2 and the present reads the drawn texture, so even a frame capped at 540 rows in a 720-row window is shrunk, not stretched; it stretches there only with `MILO_RENDER_SCALE=1` and a row cap, or a cap under half the window's rows. So the rule is dormant everywhere until a rig's timing turns a class on. Tokens `present_up_on`, `present_up_off`, `present_up_rule` write all three classes. A new log line states each present size once per change: `GpuDevice: present 1920x1080 frame into 2560x1440: clamped Catmull-Rom upscale (5 reads + 3 gathers)` (or `bilinear (one tap)`, `nearest (whole-number scale)`, `no upscale`). rb3 and rb3-xenon: nothing changes, their profile is Native and the setter is never called with on. The web build does not present through this pass. *Tests* (`native/tests/test_present_scale.cpp`): with the setter never called a 4/3 stretch equals the one tap bit for bit and no pipeline is made; a step edge rises faster with overshoot at most 0.01 at 4/3 and 1.6 (the unclamped form must overshoot, which proves the measure sees one); all 256 flat levels exact and the border does not bleed; a scale of 1, a downscale and whole scales unchanged; a steady frame creates nothing; the look by class with a drawn frame under each forced class; a timing print for the rigs. Sixteen sabotages each seen red and reverted (ten by the lane, six by the coordinator on the class rule). *Checks*: the full native gate on the pre-rebase heads (engine `6e9ac6a`, dc3 `4fd313092`): registered 1816, passed 1746, failed 0, skipped 70 against a budget of 70; the patch is line for line the same after the rebase onto engine `4dc0c52` and dc3 `39b29ea85`, where milo-tests, dc3-native, milo-viewer and the four tools build and the 34 present, look-class and cube-map tests pass. Consumers on the rebased engine head `60615c9`: rb3 (`c511d0891`) ctest 165 of 165 with its census check OK; rb3-xenon (`ac0af8749`) rb3-render 11 of 11 frames identical to engine main `4dc0c52` in two runs each (414 PASS, 0 FAIL each), selftest `NATIVE_GATE_RESULT verdict=PASS expected=18 verified=18`, health `gates_pass=513 gates_fail=0`. *Asked of the rig session*: `milo-tests --gtest_filter='PresentUpscaleGpu.TimeEachFilter'` on the 890M and the M3 (the clamped 5-tap line minus the bilinear line at 2560x1440, against 0.25 ms), and in a stretched window `present_up_off` against `present_up_on` reading `PresentScalePass`. *In a real window* (this box, a private Xvfb, RTX 3090, enhanced, `MILO_RENDER_SCALE=1 MILO_OUTPUT_MAX_ROWS=540`, tokens over the debug server): `present 960x540 frame into 1280x720: clamped Catmull-Rom upscale` at start, `bilinear (one tap)` after `present_up_off` and the cubic again after `present_up_on`, twice each; in dumps of the X root after each switch the static menu text (63,000 pixels) differs by more than 8 levels in 0 pixels off against off, 0 on against on, 3,731 and 3,729 off against on; under the rule `adapter_other` and `adapter_apple` give the one tap and `adapter_discrete` the cubic; 640x360 gives `nearest (whole-number scale)`; at the default render scale 2 every case reads `no upscale`. *Not checked*: any rig; a stretched frame in a window on a real desktop or at a rig's sizes; the compositor's own doubling beside the engine's stretch on the real panel; overshoot on game frames (the measure's floor there is about 0.02; the 0.000 is the synthetic edge's); Lanczos or Mitchell. Coordinator looked at nine of the lane's sheets at 100 % and read both diffs. **Update 2026-11-22 (`np-present60b`): on for an integrated GPU too.** The rig session timed `PresentUpscaleGpu.TimeEachFilter` on the GPD's 890M (RADV, Mesa 26.2.7, on mains, no game, 3 runs 30 s apart at dc3 `3eb227def` / engine `fe848a9`): one tap about 0.28 ms a pass (0.036 on the 3090; the pass is bound by writing its 3.7 million pixels), the clamped cubic **+0.004 ms** over it into 2560x1440 and **+0.012** into 2536x1426 (means of three; the spread between runs, about 0.01, is as large as the difference, so read it as under about 0.02), against the limit of 0.25 stated before the lane ran. The lane's projection of 0.3 ms did not happen. So Enhanced's `presentUpscaleIntegrated` is on; Apple's stays off until the M3 is timed. The GPD's picture does not change today: it presents its 1268x713 frame 1:1 into an XWayland window, so nothing is stretched. Test `PresentUpscaleLook.EnhancedOnAllButAppleAndTheTokens` (renamed). Evidence `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item60/` (`README.md`, `COORDINATOR.md`, `sheets/`, `logs/`, `sab/`, `window/`). |

## Owner decisions pending

- ~~Public web deployment (W-5)~~ Decided 2026-11-13: tailscale-only, no auth needed; redeployed from main.
- Which Mac / macOS / Xcode; Python pose server acceptable on Mac (M-7, P-8). (Dawn: the bootstrap uses Google's prebuilt unless `DAWN_PREFIX` names another.)
- ~~Land engine branch `np-macprep2` (ThreadCall without `sem_t`)~~ Landed in engine `fe22a2e` (as `2035eb9`).
- ~~Which machine and platform the first live playtest runs on (L-1 assumes Linux desktop).~~ Moot: live sessions have run on the GPD (Linux) and the Mac, native and browser.
- ~~Push or hold (L-6).~~ Decided: push; all four repos pushed 2026-11-19.
- tnr's owner: a CPU weight, or a `cmpagent` route, for `decomp-ops` builds (H-37). Until then they stay at `-j32`.
- ~~Shared-engine changes need a heads-up to the rb3 owner~~ Decided 2026-11-13: we own the engine and act as rb3 owner; land engine changes after checking rb3 and rb3-xenon (first landing: engine `4e738fe`).
- **Display curve of the enhanced look (H-160, 2026-11-21).** Since H-160 both looks show what the console sends a television, which is 13 to 16 levels darker through the dark and middle tones than every native build before it. For the retail look this is a correction. For the enhanced look it is a choice: keep the television curve (deeper floors and shadows, the look the game was graded for), or go back to the brighter ramp-only picture (`DC3_DISPLAY_GAMMA=srgb`, or flip the DC3 default in `rndshape::DisplayGammaType()`). Frames of both beside hardware stills: `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item31/coordinator/sheets/`.
- **Spot beam strength in the enhanced look (H-162, 2026-11-21).** The pale blue veil behind the dancers at streetside is one spotlight's volumetric beam, and the original draws it at the same strength (6.45 levels of lift on Xenia, 6.50 natively). Both looks keep it. If that veil is what reads as "lights blending on top of shaders", a weaker or narrower beam in the enhanced look can be built as a look setting with a token; `MILO_NO_SPOT_BEAMS=1` shows the game with no beam at all, to judge by. Nothing is changed until asked.
- **A backing for the score digits (H-164, 2026-11-21).** At the peak state the magenta score sits on magenta and red backdrops and is hard to read, in both looks and on hardware footage. A thin dark outline or backing in the enhanced look is a HUD design change, not a rendering fix. A prototype was built so that the decision can be made on frames (H-171, item 44, branch `np-look44`, off in every profile, not landed): the score reads better on bright backdrops, and on dark ones the rim cuts the digits off from their own glow. Coordinator's recommendation: not switched on as it is. To decide: drop it, or continue to a form that darkens only bright backdrops. Frames: `archive/np-gfx-2026-11-14/lanes/sharp/item44/sheets/` (`dclive_720_mul_far_peak_score_3x.png`, `street_1080s2_mul_cu_peak_score_3x.png`, `street_720_mul_cu_low_score_3x.png`).
- ~~Highlight roll-off in the enhanced look (H-164, 2026-11-21).~~ Measured 2026-11-21 (H-170): a prototype on a branch, not landed. The detail is not there to keep: the bloom has already merged the wall and its letters, so a shoulder only takes the frame's peak away (99th percentile of luma 254 down to 229 to 239) and the wall turns milky. Recommended closed; the branch `np-look41` has the tokens if the owner wants to see it.
